holder: 「貴公司有雙認證,批號追溯一定非常嚴謹。客戶詢問某批的生產履歷時,同仁是從 ERP 直接拉出,
還是去現場對紙本與 Excel?」 The answer reveals the incumbent without the prospect feeling inspected.
Generalises to any audited standard (IATF, GMP, HACCP, AS9100).

**Q22 — First block for a high-SKU plant drowning in inventory.**
**Never open with a full ERP.** Lead with **S&OP 產銷協調與存貨精確度管理 (控制呆滯)** + **多規格精準
成本毛利分析**. The argument that lands: with thousands of spec combinations, we find which SKUs are
**Cash-cows (high margin, high turns)** and which are **Cash-killers (dragging the cash down)**.

**Q23 — The close for a cash-starved prospect: 絕不主動報價.**
Offer a free half-day **產銷存健康診斷 (S&OP Quick Scan)** — sample the SKU inventory structure on site
and quantify 「降低 30 天存貨 = 省多少利息、多賺多少現金」. The diagnostic IS the next step; the number
it produces is what creates the 立案.

## §29 — Inbound IT-led call with a failing incumbent: 高賣低做 (NOVA live 2026-09-23 · job ac1dff8c · Narula Nonwoven reconcile · 南高-商機助手)
- **An IT gatekeeper's "budget X, go live now" is NOT C1.** 立案三要項 still need: the loss quantified by GM/owner, a project team that includes finance and the owner, and a signatory-set timeline. Grade C2 until then.
- **高賣低做:** ENTER on the pain the customer names (e.g. BOI material separation + a failing annual close caused by a missing production module); keep the VALUE PITCH to GM/owner on the bigger money (inventory days → released working capital). Link them with the causal chain (no production data → consumption untracked → BOI/close fail → stock piles up).
- **Pull finance in with a compliance check,** not a demo: offer a 1-hour BOI/tax/closing check by a consultant with the finance owner, report to GM + owner.
- **Open-source + heavy customisation competitor:** name the rewrite-at-every-upgrade risk openly; the quoted customisation is the starting price. Frame the failed incumbent's sunk cost as the past, a customisation-heavy choice as an open-ended future.
- **A price example spoken on a call becomes an anchor.** Re-frame it at the next meeting as a standard-edition example; the customer's real scope (multi-company, MES, BOI) is a different tier; no formal price before a site survey.
- ⚠ In this answer NOVA mislabelled DigiWin's product with the competitor's name ("Next ERP") — always check product names in NOVA wording before reuse.

## §30 — Thai make-to-order engineering job shop (jigs, custom machines, molds, injection) with a stretched balance sheet: cash-cycle wedge, iGP MTO scope, founder-board authority (NOVA live 2026-09-23 · job 6b37006e · Poomjai Engineering run · 南高-商機助手)

Profile: 100 percent Thai founder-partnership job shop, ~200 staff, several factories and two legal entities, ฿300M-class revenue, greenfield (Excel), margin rebuilt after a downcycle, current ratio below 1, AR growing faster than sales, owner publicly asking the bank for credit that follows the job cycle.
- **Stage**: C2-candidate, never E, when pain is clear but there is no IT base, no project, no budget line.
- **Wedge order for this profile**: the cash cycle FIRST (progress payments, retention, AR and inventory linked to project milestones: "collect faster, hold less stock, release working capital"), quote-versus-actual cost SECOND. "Cost visibility" alone does not move an owner whose wound is the credit line.
- **iGP standard scope for MTO project control (what we may claim)**: WBS milestones tied to AR/progress-billing conditions; project hours with wage-rate allocation; project issue/return against the project BOM with over-issue control; actual material-labour-overhead vs quoted budget variance. **Say "no" to**: automatic scheduling for MTO with design changes (manual in-system adjustment only); automatic CAD/PLC integration (custom middleware).
- **Authority in a founders' board with a family operator**: the registered directors (two-jointly) hold signature and budget; the non-director MD is the coach: deepest pain, easiest access, no signature. Visit the MD first on "cash flow and credit-line optimisation"; confirm signing authority by asking whether the board will want the solution session to cover the financing effect and whether to invite them; arm the MD with a project cash-recovery self-assessment sheet for the board.
- **Score anchor**: 45/100 for this profile (pain clear, execution hard). Refusal reflex: "no budget" or harsh payment terms.
- **Objection**: "we are an engineering company, not a factory; every job is different" → "exactly because every project is unique we do not propose a repetitive-manufacturing ERP; we lock design changes, labour hours and the billing rhythm together so you see the cash gap while the job is open, not after it closes."
- **Cases cited unnamed (TW turnkey automation maker: AR days minus twenty; TH precision mold/jig shop: project-hour capture on 5-axis parts)** — verify identity and currency before any slide (case-name hygiene rule).

## §31 — Hazardous batch-chemical maker inside a family group, ERP unknown: compliance-clock wedge (NOVA live 2026-10-02 · job c47d25bf · Nitro Chemical Industry run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Wedge = eMES (lot traceability + QC-to-CoA) + WMS (dangerous-goods storage, shelf life), hooked on dated compliance clocks** (dangerous-goods transport rule changes, annual hazardous-chemical reporting) — not a big ERP first. Holds whether or not the group runs SAP: if SAP is there, position as its plant-floor extension, never a competitor; establish SAP's real scope in the first 15 minutes.
- **Two-plant family group:** talk at group level; frame the smaller plant as the first demonstration site to be replicated — it engages the second generation's group-management ambition. Champion = the second-generation operator + any named digital-transformation VP; budget may still sit with founders or the finance head — learn the second generation's authority limit.
- **Sister companies with the same board:** separate conversations; plant one sentence at the end of the first visit.
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA called coatings/ink/resin cases "100 percent applicable" and attributed lot-tracing results to them that the case pages do not state — use such cases only as same-process-family neighbours with their year; and NOVA's own D-grade for a cold target is not our funnel (D needs contact).

## §32 — Sub-gate Thai family IMPORTER-wholesaler (no BOM, ~฿38M, unnamed ERP in use, book-vs-floor pain in its own job ads): Go-lightweight, displace not bolt-on (NOVA live 2026-10-08 · job f91c51ba · Three Brothers Furniture run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Qualification:** below the iGP ฿50M floor and trading-only is NOT an automatic No-Go when the prospect already runs an ERP and its own ads name a 帳實不符 pain → **Go-lightweight**: cloud SaaS subscription, 輕量商貿方案 (trading scope). If parked as No-Go, the three dated re-entry triggers are: revenue crosses ฿50M · warehouse expansion / new branch · 2nd-gen succession with a digitisation push.
- **Incumbent play:** "帳實不符代表現有系統缺乏控制力，外掛 barcode 只會增加維護成本，應引導置換基本型 ERP" — displace the SME package, do not sell WMS/barcode on top. Brand-discovery question that does not sound like an audit: 「為了準確掌握產品毛利，目前系統在處理進口關稅與運費分攤到各批家具時，會計和倉庫最常需要用 Excel 手工調整哪些數據呢？」
- **Wedge / boss line for an importer with ~100 inventory days:** 「展廳與倉庫的即時庫存真相」 — 「老闆，如果能讓展廳和倉庫庫存一目了然，不再因為找不到貨而漏接訂單，還能幫您釋放數百萬的積壓資金，這對您今年營運有幫助嗎？」 (cash released, orders not lost; the ฿ figure stays unspoken).
- **Authority, two family directors either signing:** the one who runs 內務 (finance + warehouse) is the coach/推動者, the one who runs 外務 is the 核決者/signer. Identify by asking who signs POs and supplier payments last, and who led the ERP-clerk interviews. Win the 內務 director first.
- **Deal shape:** first-year software + service ฿300–500K; SaaS subscription only (no on-prem, no financing); OSMEP BDS co-payment (up to ฿200K) is a strong 助攻 that answers the payback objection — pitch it.

## §33 — Thai subsidiary of a PRC-LISTED group, already on SAP, paper on the floor, new building under construction (NOVA live 2026-10-23 · job 21cb2866 · MLOptic (Thailand) run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Play = eMES + quality traceability UNDER SAP, never ERP.** First cut = the paper traveller card made electronic plus two-way batch-to-serial genealogy. For multi-stage precision processes where a middle step runs in batches (e.g. a coating-furnace run) and the last step is serialised (e.g. a lens or module), NOVA's diagnosis is that SAP rarely carries the batch→serial link, so the floor writes it on paper; customer audits (IATF 16949, ISO 13485, PPAP/8D) turn that into days of trace work. Lead with the audit clock, then quantify.
- **Authority split for a PRC-listed parent:** the Thai GM / plant head holds the "pain and solution veto" and is the champion; HQ in China holds "budget approval and architecture review" (group IT, actual controller). Before the first visit, contact the DigiWin China (鼎捷) branch in the HQ city to learn what HQ runs and whether the group is already a 鼎捷 account, and whether HQ centralises plant IT or lets the overseas plant choose its MES.
- **Stage:** zero contact = **E** in the pipeline even with strong OSINT pain (behaviour-sequence rule: the customer has shown no buying behaviour). The skill's OSINT field may still read C2-candidate on the evidence; record both.
- **First-visit next step NOVA wants:** no contract talk; ask the plant to hand over one real paper traveller card and come back with a paperless trace simulation on their real routing (proof of value). Ask the Thai GM's local spend limit.
- **Case-name hygiene:** NOVA named 大立光, 亞洲光學, 玉晶光, 揚明光 as optics references; none is in the 662 mirrored TW cases or the Monk cards (grep 2026-10-23). Verified optics-adjacent cases: 聖威光電 3134 (2021, optical sub-modules: 齊套率 27→76, on-time +28, yield 99.62→99.9), 群燿 1172 (2018, TIPTOP), 大昱光電 1377 (2018, Workflow + EasyFlow), 新鉅 1566 (2018, IFRS).

## §34 — Dead ACP record + mis-keyed booth lead: close the record, grade the booth lead D, 2-week wake-up rule (NOVA live 2026-10-23 · jobs acc2c7b9 + 2da8c50a · Wonder Packaging / Vandapac run · 南高-商機助手)
- **A struck-off (ร้าง) or dissolved tax ID on the ACP list is closed as DISQUALIFIED with a CRM note that says WHY the record existed** (e.g. "struck off 2002, no filings; the booth lead was keyed here by a parser error and is tracked under its own record"). Never let the booth intel live on under the dead tax ID.
- **Booth lead where only an operational employee was met (R&D / Asst Mgr), pain vented but owner and IT not reached = stage D (需求探詢), NOT C2.** C2 needs the ability to start internal consensus and reach the power holder; an operational complaint ("the system no longer fits") is 作業層抱怨, not 核決層企圖.
- **2-week wake-up rule:** a trade-show lead left untouched for months is cold; one value-first touch (an industry compliance checklist / white paper that the contact can carry upward), and if no real conversation within 2 weeks, drop to E and hand it to marketing nurture. Risk named by NOVA: a long-idle Dynamics AX shop gets pitched a D365 upgrade by a Microsoft partner.
- **Opener for a large multi-plant automotive Tier-1 on an old Dynamics AX:** lead with IATF 16949 traceability (lot pull for OEM audits) framed as FILLING AX's shop-floor gap (eMES / quality traceability), not ripping AX out; use the Asst-Mgr contact as information source (小蜜蜂) and ask to be introduced to the plant head / IT head quickly. NOVA's sample email asserted "we recently helped similar-sized auto parts groups": case-name hygiene applies, name a real dated case before sending.

## §35 — Sister company already on DigiWin's customer wall + lead in a colleague's 私田: group-extension, sold on its own value, through the field owner (NOVA live 2026-10-23 · job 57122a9e · Winbest Industrial (Thailand) run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Position = 集團關係延伸案 (group-relationship extension) but an independent sale** with its own contract and value case. An older parent plant and a newer sister differ in culture and pain; never assume the sister's reasons transfer.
- **Three internal checks before ANY contact:** (1) what the sister bought (Workflow or T100), its licence count, go-live status and satisfaction; (2) who our coach (小蜜蜂) is at the sister, their relationship to each signatory, and how the original decision chain ran; (3) the sister's contract entity (Thai company, Taiwan parent or offshore OBU), which shapes the new deal's commercial structure.
- **Private-field rule:** when the ACP record sits in a colleague's 私田, the first move is a meeting with that colleague; never contact the customer around them. The field owner asks the sister's AM to probe how the parent plant costs and stocks today, then a JOINT visit framed as after-sales care plus a plant-upgrade exchange, with shared/split credit.
- **Tier for a ~฿250M, 100–200 staff, single-plant converter at ~3% net margin:** Workflow ERP plus iGP/EasyFlow, not T100 (TCO). Put multi-company in the blueprint from day one, roll out in phases (parent first, then link to the sister), because shared directors plus a medical-wholesale sister imply intercompany flows.
- **Dual-track wedge for a Taiwanese-run family firm:** door-opener to the operating/younger signatory = ISO 13485 / DHR lot traceability (audit risk); closer to the founder-signatory = volatile resin cost and per-order actual margin, plus the cash in long receivables. Never open with a high quote or a heavy system at a thin margin.
- **Stage:** NOVA rated C2 (隱性, 尋找權力人士) for zero contact WHEN a sister is an existing customer; a zero-contact record with an ACP grade of A is read as a system default or stale data. Contrast §33 (zero contact, no customer link = E).
- **Hygiene catch:** NOVA treated the logo-wall sister as a confirmed customer and supplied Chinese characters and first/second-generation labels for registry names that appear in no source. Keep customer status unconfirmed until DigiWin TH confirms; never adopt NOVA-supplied names or family roles as fact.

## §36 — ACP "prospect" that is a colleague's JUST-WON deal (contract signed, implementation under way): not your pipeline; intel to the field owner; phase 2 at the go-live review (NOVA live 2026-10-23 · job f835b232 · ACCAUTO (Thailand) run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Detection first:** read the ACP record's 所有日報 / 最近日報內容 before any research verdict. A grade-A record whose daily report says 合約已簽回 is a WON account, not a target. The Step 0.5 probe (meetings, transcripts, Obsidian) does not see ACP and will say COLD.
- **Role and funnel:** not entered in the researcher's sales pipeline; track it as a reference / partner account. Do not contact the customer. Offer the field owner the OSINT as an intel pack (parent background, stock or capacity signals) plus manufacturing-expert support; the field owner decides whether to use it.
- **Phase-2 add-on for a fast-growing export parts plant with a stock build-up and a second plant:** WMS first (book-vs-floor gap once stock opens in the new ERP), then eMES with quality traceability for TS16949/IATF lot genealogy. Timing = the phase-1 go-live review: the consultant names the need, the field owner opens and quotes it, the researcher supplies cases.
- **Reference prerequisites:** parent (HQ) consent, with the local DigiWin team in the HQ city if the group is PRC-based; operations of the new plant actually running on the DigiWin system; KPI baselines agreed with the customer PM during implementation so a before-and-after exists.
- **Questions NOVA wants asked of the field owner:** is the customer PM local or seconded from HQ; which pain is most urgent (plant ramp or stock); do the directors want to copy HQ's MES experience to the Thai plant.
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA illustrated KPIs with invented targets (stock-turn days down by a set share, audit trace from days to hours); never carry such figures as data. NOVA also misplaced the new plant's start date relative to go-live; check dates against the dossier.

## §37 — Installed-base account whose MA has LAPSED during a management change at a loss-making plant: MA alone first, then a health check, then margin consulting (NOVA live 2026-10-23 · job 3e6c44c8 · Greatland Electronics Taiwan (Thailand) run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Stage:** a lapsed-MA installed-base account whose top management has just been replaced is **C2 (找權力人士提案)**, even when the ACP grade is B and the IT head once promised to sign: the old buying consensus died with the old management, and the new team's 非做不可 reason and timeline are not re-confirmed. Pushing for a signature now risks a new-team budget freeze.
- **Value framing:** never sell the MA as "system maintenance / upgrade". Sell it as **新線投產防動盪保險** (launch insurance): when the plant is adding equipment or taking new orders, the MA is what keeps the ERP stable through the ramp, so it is not an ordinary IT cost.
- **Sequencing: separate, then step up.** (1) Sign the MA alone, at the lowest threshold, to keep approval short; bundling a large consulting fee lengthens the chain. (2) Use the fresh MA to offer a free system health check on the new lines and processes. (3) Use the health-check findings to sell cost-and-margin consulting, which is the deliverable a new management team can report upward to the owners.
- **Field rules:** the account is the field owner's; coach, never take over. Where the parent is in Taiwan, pair the field owner with the DigiWin Taiwan AM for the parent, if one exists, to call high on the owners' plan for the overseas plant.
- **Risk NOVA names:** a company four years in loss cuts external consultants and IT first; unless system stability is tied to the new lines starting cleanly and to margin recovery, the renewal can sit pending indefinitely.
- **Product-line read:** NOVA calls GDC (Genero Desktop Client) plus FineReport the standard TIPTOP GP set-up. Claude vetting: T100 also runs on Genero with GDC, so confirm the product line with the field owner before writing TIPTOP GP as fact.
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA placed the decision with the Taiwan parent's "龔氏家族"; the family name was our own name-reading inference fed into the question, so NOVA echoed it. Never carry NOVA's restatement of our inputs as confirmation.

## §38 — Installed-base customer haggling over a small user-licence add-on, field owner lending accounts to hold the price: end the loan with a date, then sell (NOVA live 2026-10-23 · job e8f08dba · Sevens Piping Products run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Stage:** an add-on whose customer is already using loaned seats for free is **C2 (需求未共識/暫緩)** even when ACP says B and the opportunity sits at 報價提交: the loan stops the customer's pain, so its urgency to buy is gone, and the price round is a stand-off, not an evaluation.
- **Risks NOVA names for lending accounts:** it kills the close (free use lowers willingness to pay and the deal drifts indefinitely); unlicensed use is an internal-control and IP exposure with unclear liability if data is damaged; and it anchors the customer on "hold out long enough and DigiWin gives resources away".
- **Stop-loss sequence:** a real expiry date on the temporary licences, sent with one firm quote; a subscription (annual or quarterly) option if a one-off purchase is the objection; the field owner's manager holds the line so the field owner keeps the relationship. A price gap can be offered as credit toward a committed phase-2 project instead of as a discount.
- **Phase 2 for a US-export, listed-product metal plant (e.g. grooved fire-protection fittings):** casting and machining quality traceability / eMES, lot genealogy from melt or material through machining and coating to shipment. Do not raise it while the seat dispute is open; raise it at a system health check after the seats close, opened by the consultant and quoted by the field owner.
- **Researcher's role (colleague's 私田):** never open a second opportunity on the same account in ACP; log it in your own notes as a support or collaboration opportunity; help with group intel through DigiWin China (whether the PRC group is a 鼎捷 account, on what system, whether HQ sets overseas-plant IT) and with the wording of the loan-expiry notice.
- **Decision reading:** the sole director who personally escalates the price is the 核決者; the finance VP who negotiates first is the evaluator and price shield.
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA proposed telling the customer a head-office audit forces the expiry, having the installer "mention" that other customers had US shipments returned, and promising a two-hour lot trace and "many UL/FM-audited foundry templates in China": none is evidenced, so none may be said. NOVA also invented a group name ("浙江珍珠管業 / 珮爾管業") from an email domain we supplied; never adopt NOVA-supplied entity names.

## §39 — Late-stage (B) greenfield BOI plant of a Taiwan-listed parent in a colleague's 私田: close fast, plan back from production, settle the HQ template before signing (NOVA live 2026-10-23 · job 1d2e0582 · Fulltech Fiber Glass (Thailand) run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Stage:** quote and simple PO inside the customer's finance → purchasing → legal chain = **B**, but NOVA flags a fall-back to **C2** if HQ IT intervenes or no Thai PM is named. Greenfield + BOI approval alone still never lifts above C2 (§8, §17); here the buying behaviour (case opened by finance, PO escalated to the purchasing supervisor) is what carries B.
- **Milestones back-calculated from trial production (NOVA's plan):** sign → kickoff and current-state study within 1–2 months → To-Be blueprint (build-phase finance and fixed-asset flows) → configuration and SIT → **go-live about 3 months before trial production**, running build-phase asset receipts and spot purchases → UAT and production-module simulation → trial production → mass production. Claude vetting: when customs/BOI import roles are already being hired, machinery may land before NOVA's go-live; confirm the first BOI import date and pull the fixed-asset module forward if needed.
- **Phase-1 modules for a build-phase plant:** GL/AP/AR/FA plus PUR/COP/INV; manufacturing modules tested before trial production.
- **BOI scope split:** **eMT (machinery) = 100% in phase 1** (asset cards linked to the BOI approval and the eMT equipment list, eMT-format reconciliation report), because build-phase imports are machinery; **RMTS (raw materials) = architecture-ready in phase 1** (BOI/non-BOI dual-track stock), with RMTS report-format testing done before trial production.
- **Typical sticking points with a Taiwan-listed subsidiary:** parent legal on liability caps and IP/source-code ownership; HQ payment terms (60–90 days, progress-based) against DigiWin's milestone billing; who absorbs Thai withholding tax on service fees. **Hold:** consultant day rate, maintenance-fee start date. **Give:** first-payment share (tie later payments to blueprint and opening-balance milestones), HQ payment process with dated deadlines, the HQ-legal-reviewed text as the final contract — but get the simple PO and a deposit signed this month to lock price and scope.
- **Three checks before signing:** (1) does HQ IT require its own ERP or template (accounts, item codes, BOM) for the Thai plant — if yes, HQ IT must be in the kickoff or consolidation fails later; (2) who is the real Thai PM (seconded HQ manager or a not-yet-hired accountant); (3) has the sole signatory (often the parent chairman) or his delegate agreed the budget, so purchasing's approval is not overturned at the signature.
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA gave the parent's Taiwan company number wrongly (84154515; public record 16834268), misread ฿225.3M of fixed assets as 22.5億泰銖, and called the parent's controlling shareholder a "confirmed" TIPTOP GP / T100 customer that our case mirror does not show. Verify every identifier, amount and customer claim NOVA returns.

## §40 — PRC-private-parent valve group with TWO Thai entities (exporting sister + new pre-revenue plant company), colleague's T100 quote silent 80 days: C2, two-entity blueprint, find the signatory (NOVA live 2026-10-23 · job 6df7b8e8 · Galaxy Industrial (Thailand) run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Stage:** a quote sent with no logged follow-up for ~80 days, only a manager met and no signatory known is **C2 (找權力人士提案)** even when ACP says B and the SDR forecasts signing at 80%. Three facts keep (or restore) B: the power holder's stance (does the contact decide; what does the director/HQ think), concrete feedback on the quote (changes asked, a scenario demo requested), and the hard date behind the signing month (BOI filing / machinery-import deadline vs production start).
- **Scope vs user count:** for a vertically integrated plant (casting, machining, vulcanising, coating, assembly, pressure test) with an operating sister, NOVA calls a 10-user T100 quote too small for production + BOM + stock + finance + customs/BOI; if the budget is tight, fall back to Workflow ERP with the Thai pack rather than an undersized T100. Claude vetting: user count depends on Thai headcount, so treat this as a sizing question for the field owner, not a fact.
- **Contract entity and blueprint:** sign with the entity that holds (or will hold) the BOI certificate; put BOTH Thai entities in the blueprint from day one when the sister already trades and holds stock, because transfers, internal trading or subcontracting between them will break a single-plant phase 1.
- **BOI phase 1:** eMT (machinery assets) and RMTS (raw-material duty, BOM-based reconciliation reports); confirm the BOI certificate and its tax-exemption sections first (consistent with §15, §39).
- **Listed-product US buyers (fire valves to a fire-protection OEM):** show quality traceability as the target picture in the phase-1 blueprint, deliver it as phase-2 eMES; phase 1 must already carry lot/barcode stock so eMES can attach.
- **Researcher's role in a colleague's 私田:** brief the field owner (sister's exports, US buyers, BOI and audit angle), help her lift the pitch from a 10-user price to group structure + BOI + buyer audits, and check through DigiWin China (the parent's city branch) what the parent runs and whether the owner sets the Thai plant's system.
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA's sample script promised a valve could be traced "within 2 hours" to its casting day and melt number and warned of a buyer "stop-order" risk; neither is evidenced, so neither may be said to a customer. NOVA also addressed Peter as a southern-region senior rep; he is Head of Distributor.

## §41 — Taiwanese-founded PCB-chemicals group, Thai TRADING sister already selling + new pre-revenue PLANT company, Taiwan parent apparently on 鼎新, colleague's deal stalled on unrecorded "doubts" (NOVA live 2026-10-23 · job 48191b04 · Chemax Electronic Materials (Thailand) run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Stage:** an opportunity moved to 合約遞交 and then back to 報價提交, with the customer's doubts unrecorded, a meeting with the bosses still being arranged and ~80 days without a report, is **C2 (找權力人士提案)**, whatever ACP says. Three facts restore B: the plant's production start date (the 非做不可 date), a Thai project owner named by the bosses with authority over resources, and who funds the Thai ERP budget and for which entities.
- **In-family parent:** when the Taiwan parent uses a 鼎新 product, the Thai companies should go on the same line (Workflow ERP Thai version if HQ runs Workflow): HQ IT can support it, reports and logic match, and intercompany data is easiest. First move: DigiWin TW (the parent's branch) confirms product, licence and MA status, and HQ IT's stance on overseas plants.
- **Two Thai entities (trading + plant):** one blueprint, phased; **trading company first** (it already has revenue, stock, receivables and Thai tax), plant second when production starts. Selling BOM and work orders to a plant that has not started hands the customer a reason to wait.
- **Contract entity:** NOVA prefers the Taiwan parent or the profitable trading company over a loss-making plant company. Claude vetting: signing from Taiwan triggers the cross-border withholding issue in §17; if the plant holds a BOI certificate, §39/§40 favour the BOI entity. Decide with the field owner.
- **Phase 1 for formulated batch chemicals with technical service:** Thai VAT (P.P.30), AR/AP/GL, import purchasing, sales, stock with **lot and expiry** and a dangerous-goods store, technical-service visit and consumption logging linked to sales/stock. Phase 2: versioned formula BOM (confidential), work orders and batch records, lab CoA/MSA linkage, cost accounting, BOI raw-material records. Claude vetting: when the plant company already carries large PP&E and BOI machinery records may apply, fixed assets and eMT belong in phase 1 (§39).
- **Joint play in a colleague's 私田:** DigiWin TW meets the Taiwan bosses in Taiwan, the field owner works the Thai contact; next step a half-day diagnostic of the trading company by TW and TH consultants, not a new price.
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA asserted that PCB makers' supplier audits "usually" cannot be passed without an ERP, named large Thai PCB plants as the prospect's future customers, put a "several million baht" damage figure on a bad batch, guessed the production start, and suggested showing "PCB 藥水業成功案例" that do not exist in the case mirror. None may be used. Its reading of the bosses' identities echoed our own hypothesis from the question.

## §42 — Colleague's "starter pack" quote (5 users + 财税包 + 送泰文包 + 25 人天, 3/7折) to a large exporting contract-manufacturing plant, silent 143 days: C2, lift scope through the HQ-city 鼎捷 AM, phase 2 in the blueprint (NOVA live 2026-10-23 · job 33aaadaa · MECO Industries run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Reading the quote:** NOVA reads a 5-user, 财税包 + 泰文包, ~25 consultant-day quote in Thailand as the classic quick-start / finance-and-tax package, most likely **Workflow ERP GP or 易飛**, built to get a new plant filing with the Thai Revenue Department, not to run production. It reads **"3/7折" as licence at 3折 and services at 7折** (licence margin can be discounted deep, consultant days cannot). Claude vetting: NOVA's product guess and the discount reading are its interpretation, not the quote; confirm both with the field owner before repeating them.
- **Sizing:** for a 20,000 m² multi-process plant (casting, CNC, stamping, moulding, assembly) exporting to North America, NOVA calls 5 users and 25 days "嚴重低估": enough for opening-period bookkeeping, not for BOM, routing, stock or traceability. Same direction as §40 (10-user T100 too small for a casting-to-assembly plant); treat as a sizing question for the field owner, since Thai headcount is unknown.
- **Stage:** quote sent, only a non-director GM met, shareholders offshore (HK holding, Canadian minority), 143 days without a report = **C2 (找權力人士提案)**, consistent with §38/§40/§41. Three facts restore B: a real conversation with a power holder above the GM; a customer project team and a scheduled manufacturing/traceability demo or site study; a re-issued go-live date with a budget sized to the plant.
- **Authority:** for a multinational SME group (overseas HQ + offshore holding + a China sister plant + a Thai plant GM), NOVA places ERP budget approval with the shareholders/directors above the Thai GM, the GM being the executor. If the China sister plant is a 鼎捷 account, NOVA strongly prefers the same product line for the Thai plant (shared item codes, BOM, customers; group reporting). First move: the field owner finds the China plant's 鼎捷 AM (search the CRM by the Chinese company name or its credit code) and asks what it runs, its plans for Thailand, and who decides.
- **Phase 2 timing (differs from §36/§38 where a contract already exists):** with no contract yet, put 'phase 1 finance and tax, phase 2 core manufacturing and traceability' into ONE blueprint at the re-engagement meeting, rather than waiting for go-live; a 5-user finance-only deal is small enough to stall.
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA placed director Wang Hao "at the Nanjing head office" and called the Nanjing company the parent; neither is evidenced (registry shows a Hong Kong holder and two Canadian holders; the group site names Canada as HQ). NOVA's sample opener asserted "large-volume" exports and "very hard" IATF/VMI audit demands from North American buyers on four shipments of window hardware; neither may be said to a customer. Opening a customer call with their own customs records needs care: use it as the reason to ask, never as surveillance.

## §43 — Thai plant of a PRC group that already runs T100, Thai entity hosted on the group's mainland T100, colleague quoting a separate "Thai segment" add-on (software + consulting): C2, sell Thai localisation + Thai-site seats + local consulting on the SAME instance, budget in the HQ city (NOVA live 2026-10-23 · job f21605c1 · Lao Plastic Industry (Thailand) run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Reading "泰国T已经部署大陆":** NOVA reads it as a single-host, multi-organisation T100: the Thai legal entity and site are built inside the parent's T100 in China and reached over VPN. Usual blockers for a Thai entity on a China-configured T100: Thai VAT (P.P.30) and withholding forms, e-Tax invoices, Thai-language documents for local staff, BOI eMT/RMTS reconciliation, TFRS-format statements for the Thai auditor. Claude vetting: an interpretation; first confirm with the field owner whether Thai VAT and BOI are already posted in T100 or kept by an accounting firm.
- **What to sell:** T100 Thai localisation + Thai-site user licences + local consulting by DigiWin TH bilingual consultants; NOT a separate system, because the parent needs consolidation and supply-chain links on one instance.
- **Stage:** a quote at 報價提交 to a director who cannot sign contracts, with the Thai finance and BOI seats still being hired and no schedule = **C2 (需求探詢 / 推動內部共識)**, whatever ACP says. Three facts restore B: the sole signatory or the HQ IT/finance owner states support and budget; the new Thai finance manager is in post and has seen a Thai tax + BOI walkthrough; an agreed schedule and scope check.
- **Budget and contract:** a loss-making, group-funded Thai plant (interest-free current liabilities) has no budget of its own; the decision sits at HQ. NOVA's split: licences may be bought by the parent through DigiWin China (the HQ-city branch), Thai consulting contracted by the Thai entity with DigiWin TH to keep a Thai invoice for VAT and avoid cross-border withholding on services. Claude vetting: NOVA's withholding percentage is unverified; do not quote it.
- **Two-side play:** DigiWin China's AM for the parent calls high in the HQ city (budget, HQ IT owner, the original T100 project history); the Thai field owner works the local GM and meets the new finance manager on Thai tax and BOI as onboarding help. Do not sign a large Thai-entity contract before HQ agrees.
- **First-year loss:** may be raised only as a management question ("how is the Thai plant's product cost worked out and reported to HQ?"), never by quoting the customer's filing at them.
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA's sample scripts used invented figures (an accountant losing "50 percent" of the time to manual tax reports, fixing later costing "three times" more, year-one COGS of "2 to 3 times revenue is normal", "we have studied many Chinese plants in Bang Pu"); none may be said. It also treated BOI status as confirmed from two job postings.

## §44 — ACP B-record whose only stated need is NOT a DigiWin product (low-voltage works: shop air-con, Wi-Fi, CCTV for a new line), SDR already declined, bot moved the stage to 報價提交 with amount 0; small batch-chemicals maker below the iGP Go band (NOVA live 2026-10-23 · job 88b69b95 · EXSTAR Asia Corporation run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Not an ERP opportunity; grade E (公田/長期經營)** in the researcher's funnel. Building and infrastructure requests (弱電, HVAC, CCTV) are capex on the plant, not a process or system evaluation; 立案三要項 are all zero. A stage of 報價提交 / grade B with 預計商機金額 0 set by MKT Bot in the same minute as the SDR's "不适合参与" note is a system artefact: ask the field owner (SDR + sales leader) to move it back to E or close it with a note, because a zero-amount B pollutes the regional forecast funnel.
- **New production line = false C2** on its own. Wake-up triggers NOVA gives for a batch-chemicals supplier: (a) about two months before the new line starts (manual recipe and batch records double); (b) a customer audit (IATF/ISO) that reaches the supplier's lot records; (c) the next statements showing stock or overheads still rising.
- **Sub-gate batch-chemicals maker (~฿60M, ~30 staff, latest year a loss): Go-lightweight + watch-list, never heavy on-premise.** First cut = lot traceability (recipe/batch sheet mix-up proofing, fast CoA) rather than recipe costing (sensitive, complex) or traded-goods stock; tier = SaaS lightweight (iGP subscription / Workflow cloud), low first payment.
- **Authority in a small Japanese-Thai JV:** the GM who runs operations is the influencer and solution owner; the Japanese president who signs is the budget gate. Ask the field owner first: the GM-president trust line, who decided past IT purchases, and how the new line is funded.
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA rounded the inventory up (฿15M / 150–160 days vs DBD ฿13.39M / 116 days), assumed a "Japanese HQ" that the registry does not show, and leaned on an automotive-customer premise that came from OUR question, not from evidence. Check every figure and premise NOVA restates against the dossier.


## §45 — Partner AGV quote (象田) in a colleague's 私田, benchmarked against a forklift OEM (中力 / EP Equipment) on price; large fast-growing harness plant with a new plant due, stock 4× in two years, thin margin, high leverage (NOVA live 2026-10-23 · job ed890822 · Denka Kogyo (Thailand) run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Stage:** an ACP C1 whose only buying behaviour is a hardware price contest (partner AGV vs a forklift maker's AGV), with the expected signing date lapsed and ~30 days without a report, is **C2 (找權力人士提案)**: no shared view of a system benefit and no 非做不可 reason is on record. Three facts restore C1: a new decision date and plant timetable with a software budget; the split of authority between the operating GM and the signatory; the customer's own quantified intent on the stock problem.
- **Role: value seller and integrator, never price-matcher.** Split the quote into partner hardware and DigiWin software (WMS + AGV dispatch + barcode lot traceability); sell the pairing as the material-flow design for the new plant. NOVA's line, internal: AGVs without a WMS move the wrong material faster.
- **Tier and first cut for a ~฿660M, ~500-staff, multi-plant IATF harness maker:** T100; first cut WMS with barcode lot traceability tied to the AGVs and the new plant, then MRP and programme costing.
- **Payment design under red flags (net margin <1.5%, D/E >2.5, current ratio <1):** separate hardware and software contracts; hardware back-to-back with most of it collected before shipment; software/services milestone-billed (NOVA proposed 40/30/20/10) with next-phase licences released on payment. A proposal to the field owner, not policy.
- **Reading ACP noise:** a contact Category of 老客 and an opportunity created as 贏單 then moved by MKT Bot, with 累計銷售金額 0 and no contract link, are most likely migration defaults; check the contact's history before treating the account as installed base.
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA called the opportunity amount a system default although the record history shows the SDR typed it; promised a "฿30M cash release" from WMS + MRP; asserted the forklift OEM cannot integrate with ERP/MES; converted an ambiguous 100万 into baht via RMB; and guessed the GM represents a Taiwanese or PRC shareholder against a registry with no PRC holder. None may be used. It also echoed our unverified "the contact is the director" hypothesis as fact.


## §46 — ACP C1 whose only stated need is CCTV, at a TAIWANESE GREENFIELD plant held through a Samoa company (parent unknown, pre-revenue, capital raised in steps) (NOVA live 2026-10-23 · job 9b31803e · JEI BAO International run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Stage:** a low-voltage request (CCTV) is not an ERP opportunity even at a well-funded greenfield plant; 立案三要項 are all zero. NOVA grades it **E (公田/長期經營)** and asks the field owner to move an ACP C1 / 方案提報 back to E or D. Same rule as §44, now confirmed for a large greenfield, not only for a small operating firm.
- **Who decides ERP at a Taiwanese overseas greenfield:** NOVA's prior is that the Taiwan parent (HQ IT or the owning family) chooses the ERP brand and the Thai plant (build lead / plant manager) collects requirements and tests; the Thai plant does not pick a new ERP on its own. NOVA attached a 70/30 split to this; the figure is unsourced and must not be quoted.
- **First move = identify the parent and its system**, before any pitch: ask DigiWin TW / DigiWin China whether the group is an account and on which product. Play A (parent on DigiWin): replicate the parent's system to Thailand with Thai localisation; first cut BOI bonded and import-export control. Play B (parent on SAP or another foreign ERP): two-tier, a local DigiWin ERP (plus MES and BOI) under the parent's ERP; first cut subcontract control plus Thai tax and BOI.
- **Colleague's 私田 etiquette:** the field owner keeps the CCTV job as his relationship tool; the channel head offers parent-side intel through DigiWin TW and agrees credit in advance (NOVA suggested the Thai team keeps the booking or a split under the cross-border commission rule; a proposal, not policy).
- **Timing for a greenfield:** the entry window opens 6–9 months before production (BOI approval, roof-topping, machinery import planning); an ERP project must be signed 3–6 months before production so BOM and stock are initialised before the first trial run.
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA guessed a specific Taiwanese screwdriver maker as the parent with no registry or name evidence; asserted that a large capital increase "必定" means BOI promotion; and offered a "SAP costs three times as much locally" line. None may be used. It also read the ACP contact and a director as possibly the same person although the romanisations differ.


## §47 — Partner AGV quote (象田) at a Taiwanese-parent aerospace/satellite CNC plant in a colleague's 私田: C2; eMES traceability first, MES dispatch drives the AGVs; the parent decides (NOVA live 2026-10-23 · job 7e248cd3 · Longbee Packaging (Thailand) run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Stage:** an ACP C1 whose only record is one partner AGV briefing followed by a re-price, with no amount, no date and ~6 weeks silent, is **C2**: no internal consensus and no project team. Three facts restore C1: a quantified must-do reason (handling labour limiting machine utilisation, or a dated customer audit), a named customer project owner (a PM or IT lead), and a go-live date with a budget. Same rule as §45, now for a profitable plant without price competition.
- **First software cut depends on the pain, not on the AGV:** for a CNC plant serving aerospace/LEO-satellite buyers, NOVA leads with **eMES** (work orders bound to first-article and CMM measurement records, tool life, lot genealogy) and sells "MES dispatch drives the AGVs"; contrast §45 (harness plant with stock out of control: WMS first). Claude vetting: §1's "never MES before a stable ERP" still holds where no ERP of record exists; eMES-first assumes one does (here an IT job ad asked for ERP experience).
- **Taiwanese parent that advertises its own ERP/MES/APS "smart factory":** the parent sets the Thai plant's system or holds a veto. First move: the DigiWin TW field owner for the parent's area checks the CRM for the parent and asks what ERP/MES HQ runs and whether a listing is planned. Parent on 鼎新 → Taiwan sales calls high on HQ, Thailand delivers (replicate + Thai localisation, as §41/§43). Parent not on 鼎新 → two-tier: DigiWin Thai version for Thai tax and the plant floor, feeding the parent's system.
- **Tier:** T100 as the long-term base if group consolidation or a listing is in view; Workflow ERP first if the plant needs a fast go-live.
- **Liquidity (current ratio near 0.5 while capex-heavy and profitable):** protect payment with a high first payment and either a parent guarantee or the parent contracting and paying. A proposal, not policy; a Taiwan-signed contract raises the withholding and Thai-invoice issues in §41/§43.
- **Authority:** the director who is also the parent's current registered representative is read as the 核決者; ask at owner level "roll out HQ's system, or let the plant pick a local partner that reports up?"
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA supplied a wrong Taiwan UBN for the parent (16104867 vs public 16047369), upgraded "registered 負責人" to "董事長", claimed parent systems are "very costly to land in Thailand" and two-tier is "lowest TCO", and wrote customer questions that quote the filing ("revenue up 7×"), assume a "huge SpaceX order volume" and hint at losing order share. None may be used (§43 rule on quoting filings).

## §48 — ACP C1 quote at the Thai 100% subsidiary of a Taiwan-LISTED automation SI that was once a 鼎新 ecosystem-alliance member and sells its own WMS: C2; customer first, partner second, never a WMS fight (NOVA live 2026-10-23 · job f5e18598 · Mirle Automation Inter (Thailand) run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Stage:** a quote already submitted, the contact a sole-signing director, but amount 0 in ACP and ~24 days of "chasing purchasing" with no date or budget is **C2 (退回)**, not C1: 立案三要項 not met. Three facts restore B: the customer's own agreement that project margin/stock control is a problem worth fixing, a stated budget range and decision date, and an agreed executive demo on project costing / Thai tax.
- **What was probably quoted (hypothesis only):** Thai-localised Workflow ERP or an e-Tax / project-costing add-on. NOVA leaned on the contact's Category 老客; per §45 that field is often a migration default, so the field owner must say what was quoted.
- **First cut for a project-type AS/RS installer subsidiary (lumpy revenue, thin margins, parent 100%):** project (WBS) cost control, budget vs actual on parent-supplied equipment, local subcontract and site labour, plus Thai tax compliance; not generic 進銷存.
- **Role:** 「先客戶，後夥伴」. Close the subsidiary's own system need first; co-sell (AS/RS hardware + ERP/MES) only after trust exists. Boundary to propose: hardware + WCS/equipment control = the SI; ERP, MES, accounting and upper-layer WMS = DigiWin. Never fight the SI's own WMS head-on.
- **Researcher (not field owner):** help through the field owner and sales leader; use DigiWin TW's relationship with the parent to learn HQ's system and the subsidiary's pain, then hand the intel to the field owner. Never quote or contact directly.
- **Risk:** a group with its own software subsidiaries may copy HQ's system or build in-house; DigiWin's difference must be Thai compliance and speed of go-live.
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA said the parent is "極高機率" a 鼎新 ERP customer on TIPTOP/T100 with no evidence beyond size (unverified; the only public link is a 2018 alliance blog); invented a "2% project-margin" target and a "2026 Q4" decision date; wrote a customer question claiming revenue "grew very fast" (DBD shows −53% in the latest year); and named a "Yanyun ERP" product not verified in our corpus. None of these may be used. Its joint-visit idea (researcher with the field owner and sales leader, framed as after-care from the TW ecosystem relationship) fits §35 only if the field owner leads and asks for it.

## §49 — ACP C1 whose only stated need is a CCTV upgrade, with the field owner setting up an executive-level briefing (stage 高階排擬), at the ramping Thai plant of a Taiwanese custom injection moulder whose parent already runs ERP/MES/WMS/QMS + a command centre (NOVA live 2026-10-23 · job 3a71b8c9 · Grand Dynasty Industrial (Thailand) run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Stage:** an executive meeting about CCTV does not change §44/§46: still **E (長期經營)**. 立案三要項 are all zero for core systems; a CCTV proposal reaching a VP is a relationship step, not a software opportunity. Do not let 高階排擬 on a CCTV record inflate the funnel.
- **How the field owner tests for software without hijacking the CCTV meeting:** two questions, framed around the customer's own pressures: (a) when overseas customers come to approve parts transferred to this plant (N+1), what records will they audit and when will the plant reach the parent's level; (b) does HQ want this plant's production and financial data on its command-centre dashboards, and when. Claude vetting: strip any customer names or figures that came from customs records or filings before the field owner says it.
- **Parent with an integrated stack (ERP + MES + WMS + QMS + machine networking + 戰情中心) and the Thai plant not yet on it:** the system decision sits with the Taiwan parent. Parent on 鼎新 → "母建子隨": copy HQ's standard, DigiWin TH adds Thai localisation and local implementation, data back to the command centre. Parent not on 鼎新 → two-tier, ledger passed up. First move: DigiWin TW's customer-success manager for the parent's area checks the parent (registry number, contact history, ERP brand).
- **Timing for a plant already in ramp-up:** not too late; the ramp and customer-audit year is when manual stock, traceability and (if BOI) duty-free material reconciliation problems surface. First cut if a door opens: ERP base plus MES lot traceability together.
- **Tier:** NOVA said T100 + sMES (investment size, LSR, automotive/medical, future consolidation). Claude vetting: conflicts with §17 (start iGP/Workflow; T100 only when HQ demands consolidation) and §4 gates when the Thai plant's revenue is tiny; if the parent is on a 鼎新 product, the tier follows the parent. Treat the tier as open until the parent's system is known.
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA attached an unsourced 90 percent "decision in Taiwan" figure; assumed BOI promotion and imported machinery without evidence and threatened "巨額罰款"; claimed customers will not approve a plant without MES; promised a 3-month digital site that passes transfer audits first time; proposed telling the customer its 2025 gross loss would turn into 2026 revenue (quotes the filing, §43); named the parent's US customers in customer-facing questions; and upgraded an ACP contact to "signing director and plant head" on a romanisation that does not match. None may be used.

## §50 — INSTALLED-BASE reference customer (on 鼎新 ERP, DigiWin AGV contracted) whose new asks are a partner AS/RS (立庫) and partner CCTV, AGV stalled on site readiness, lead in a colleague's 私田: C2; WMS + barcode as the brain over the partner's WCS; CCTV stays E; protect the reference (NOVA live 2026-10-24 · job 0c79f53b · Jumpway run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Stage:** an ACP C1 on an existing customer whose only recorded asks are partner hardware (AS/RS proposal, CCTV meeting) with amount 0, no PM and no date, ~3 weeks silent, is **C2 (找權力人士提案)**; installed-base status does not lift it. Three facts restore C1: a quantified must-do reason (customer JIT/line-stop exposure or a dated new-plant milestone tied to the system), a named customer PM for the new-plant systems, and a go-live date for WMS/MES aligned with the plant timetable.
- **Software attach and boundary:** WMS + barcode lots first, eMES reporting later. **DigiWin WMS = the brain** (ERP documents, lot control, FIFO, slotting, pick/put orders); **partner WCS/PLC = execution** (stacker crane, conveyors, AGV movement, returning completion to WMS). Consistent with §45 (AGVs without a WMS move the wrong material faster) and §48 (hardware + WCS = SI; ERP/MES/upper WMS = DigiWin).
- **First cut for a JIT supplier with several shipments a day:** barcode receiving/shipping and a **shipping-buffer area under WMS**, then raw-material and WIP stock by barcode; not MES first.
- **CCTV:** still **E**, a relationship tool handed to a trusted partner (same as §44/§46/§49), even on an installed-base account.
- **Protecting a reference site when a DigiWin-sold AGV is stalled by the customer's site:** turn the vendor's rectification list into a site-readiness checklist for the owner, and propose a **pilot zone** in a building already running, so the owner sees progress before the whole plant is ready.
- **ACP shows 累計銷售金額 0 on an account the customer says runs 鼎新:** check whether the contract was signed by another legal entity, in Taiwan, or through a partner and never linked to the customer code; ask the AM side and the customer's finance for the contract header.
- **Non-owner's role:** hand the field owner the plant-flow intel and the financial read; do not quote, promise scope or commit dates to the customer directly.
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA assumed a "台灣母公司" signed the 2023 contract (the registry shows Thai 100% and no parent is evidenced); wrote a customer question that quotes the filing (fixed assets, falling margin; §43 rule); and offered "百萬級硬體折損" and "100% 正確" lines. None may be used.

## §51 — INSTALLED-BASE small plant (~฿45M revenue, five loss years, margin halved, fresh capex) where the field owner quoted an eMES "upgrade" and the customer has not yet shown interest; SFT vs eMES waits on the customer's own reporting logic; lead in a colleague's 私田: C2; SFT as phase 1, eMES as phase 2 (NOVA live 2026-10-24 · job 01ab8e49 · Forward Glass (Thailand) run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Stage:** a submitted quote that the customer has not acknowledged, with the reporting logic undefined, no customer PM and no date, is **C2**, not C1, even on an installed-base account (same rule as §45/§47/§50). Three facts restore C1: a quantified must-do reason (a buyer audit on lot genealogy or a dated new-line start tied to the system), a named customer project owner (production control head or plant manager), and a go-live date with a budget held by the Thai plant or the parent.
- **SFT before eMES for a small, loss-making discrete plant:** at this size and with losses, eMES licences, machine connection and consulting are hard to fund; the first floor pain is work-order progress and labour-hour capture, which SFT covers. eMES first only when the buyer demands in-process parameter capture per lot (for example furnace or coating parameters bound to the batch). Keep phase 2 alive by writing SFT into the proposal as "shop-floor digital phase 1" and stating the eMES upgrade path in it (NOVA proposed a licence trade-in clause; a proposal for the field owner, not policy).
- **Wedge for a Taiwanese owner after a capex year:** utilisation and scrap on the new equipment, measured from reporting data, not a margin promise.
- **Parent role:** with a Thai plant in loss and funded from the group, the Taiwan parent is the approver. First move: the DigiWin TW account manager for the parent confirms which ERP the Taiwan plant runs and what the new Thai capacity is for.
- **Non-owner (channel head) role:** convene the parent's TW AM, the Thai field owner and presales to align; help the field owner repackage a single eMES quote as an SFT-then-eMES two-step; supply SFT cases from auto parts or glass processing; never contact the customer around the field owner.
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA promised to "lock back the lost 10 points of margin", addressed the signatory as chairman on our surname-only input, called the parent loans fact although we gave them as an inference, framed the buyer's IATF audit as given although the consignee is a US sourcing company, and wrote customer questions that quote the filing ("fixed assets doubled this year"; §43 rule). None may be used.

## §52 — ACP C1 at the Thai plant of a PRC-LISTED giant whose HQ production system is already replicated to Thailand; the field owner is delivering e-Tax + barcode material issue there, and the latest asks are IT infrastructure (storage, hyper-converged, NetApp): infra in its own funnel, software C2, first cut = the new building's WMS + BOI localisation (NOVA live 2026-10-24 · job a85fb03d · Victory Giant Technology (Thailand) run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Two funnels, never one.** Storage, hyper-converged and similar hardware/project resale is not an ERP or application-software opportunity; keep it in a separate hardware/project funnel so it does not distort the software forecast. Same logic as §44/§46/§49 (low-voltage, CCTV), now for IT infrastructure sold by our own field team.
- **Software stage = C2 (找權力人士提案)** when HQ has publicly replicated its planning, materials and QC system to the Thai plant and the Thai side has no quantified must-do reason, no timetable and no budget of its own, even though a DigiWin delivery (e-Tax, barcode) is under way. Three facts would restore C1: the field owner names what was delivered and under which contract; DigiWin China confirms HQ's system; the plant names a BOI or new-building warehouse need with an owner and a date.
- **What DigiWin sells next to an HQ-replicated core:** the Thai layer and the edge, not the core. NOVA's order: (1) WMS and barcode for the NEW building, copied from the first building's barcode work; (2) BOI duty-free machinery and material accounting as Thai localisation; (3) a quality-data link (in PCB, multi-petabyte inspection data is usually AOI/AVI images; turning inspection results into SPC/QMS signals is DigiWin's ground, storage is not). Precondition: learn HQ's system through DigiWin China (鼎捷 product = same-instance extension, §43; foreign ERP = a Thai layer feeding it, §46 Play B).
- **Budget and decision:** a loss-making, group-funded Thai plant whose key posts are filled from HQ and whose key materials and equipment go through HQ's central purchasing has its system and budget decisions at HQ; Thai IT gathers requirements. Small Thai-only items (e-Tax, scanners, label printers) may be bought locally.
- **Channel head's role (non-owner):** cross-border facilitator. Leave the field owner's hardware deals alone; ask DigiWin China's key-account side whether the PRC parent is an account, which product it runs, who the HQ CIO/CFO/supply-chain head are and who covers them; offer the field owner a call-high on HQ with credit agreed in advance (NOVA's cross-border split is a proposal, not policy).
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA declared the account "已成交客戶" from our description although ACP shows 累計銷售金額 0 and no contract link (§45: check the contract before calling it installed base); attached an unsourced "100%" to HQ's decision power; assumed the "1300W" quote was in baht (no currency was given); claimed a parent system "絕無可能" has Thai BOI functions (overclaim); warned of a price war with named hardware distributors on no evidence; and dated the new building's topping-out to year-end although the parent's IR record put it in Q2. None may be used.

## §53 — Pure-TRADING machinery/tool distributor group (no BOM, capital < ฿5M, ~฿310M across two sister companies, agent for a Chinese woodworking-machine brand + own design-to-CNC software), ERP proposal at C1 in a colleague's 私田 after the owner's wife said "pure trading, needs customisation, budget risk": false C1 → C2; no Thai service/dispatch package; the value is a channel tie-up (NOVA live 2026-10-24 · job 178eb472 · TA-CHOU INDUSTRY run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Grade:** a "偽 C1" → C2 (Pending). No quantified must-do reason, no project team, no timetable, months without a report: stop ERP proposal activity; no demo, no POC (the customer would use it to price-compare); never promise customised service/dispatch.
- **Deliverability (NOVA's claim, verify with TH delivery before repeating):** DigiWin Thailand has NO Thai standard package for the equipment-agent service side (maintenance contracts, field dispatch, service invoices, withholding-tax handling, multi-language); the TW 設備代理業 modules are not a Thai deliverable without 二開. If forced, the only first cut is multi-location spare-parts stock.
- **Competition:** do not fight Yonyou (trading ERP, strong Chinese localisation, price) or a local Thai CRM on a trader's budget; a win would be a low-price customisation project.
- **Value = channel/ecosystem:** a distributor that sells and installs whole CNC panel lines plus design-to-CNC software at Thai furniture plants is a door into those plants (machines + design files → ERP/MES). NOVA puts it at Head-of-Distributor level, not SDR. Claude vetting: always agree the approach with the field owner first (private-field rule), run /research-partner before any meeting, and never speak NOVA's "many KDT + 鼎新 ERP/MES integration cases" (not in the published corpus) or its "Jubili = Thai CRM leader" (vendor not found).

## §54 — Pre-revenue PRC-parent label/packaging greenfield where the GM (a DBD signatory) stated the ERP scope but deferred to "the boss" until orders arrive, then 121 days of silence in a colleague's 私田: C2; parent first via DigiWin China; phase 1 = finance + Thai tax + buy/sell/stock, phase 2 = BOM/MOC/cost/MRP (NOVA live 2026-10-24 · job 68d9cc6d · Jinghong New Material (Thailand) run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Stage:** a scope statement from the GM ("finance and operations both") is a real need, but "will ask the boss once orders come" means no budget, no schedule and no project owner, so **C2 (找權力人士提案 / 推動內部共識)** whatever ACP says, even when the GM is a legal signatory. Three facts restore C1/B: (a) first core orders confirmed and a dated mass-production schedule, from which the ERP go-live is back-calculated; (b) the owner (or the GM, with the owner's written OK) confirms a budget range and names a Thai project owner; (c) the customer commits to an evaluation schedule and lets DigiWin map requirements or demo as a named candidate.
- **First move for a PRC private parent:** search the parent's full Chinese name (and credit code, once verified) in DigiWin's internal CRM, then raise a cross-border opportunity notice so the DigiWin China AM in the parent's city (NOVA: Suqian sits under the Nanjing business unit) visits HQ and confirms the ERP brand and group IT plan. Parent on a DigiWin product → extend its template with a Thai localisation interface (same logic as §43); otherwise a stand-alone Workflow ERP for the Thai plant with a finance link back to the parent (same logic as §46 Play B).
- **Phase-1 "survival compliance" scope for a plant just taking its first orders:** GL/AP/AR + Thai localisation (VAT, WHT, BOI basics) + PUR/COP/INV. **Phase 2 when orders are steady:** BOM + MOC + actual cost + MRP. Claude vetting: when the plant already carries large PP&E, fixed assets (and eMT, if BOI-promoted) belong in phase 1 (§39, §41).
- **Label / shrink-sleeve industry pain priors (NOVA's, not evidence about any one plant):** film bought by weight but issued and slit by length/width, with leftover rolls of unknown length piling up (dual-unit and lot-controlled roll stock); plates and cutting dies that are costly and wear out by impressions (plate/tool records); large, batch-varying make-ready waste that averages hide (actual cost per order); battery and brand customers who expect lot traceability from finished label back to film roll and ink batch.
- **Re-entry line:** go back on the customer's own trigger ("you said second half; it is Q4: are orders in, and has HQ asked for any reporting or system requirement for the Thai plant?"). Do not open with a quote or a demo.
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA supplied a Chinese unified social credit code for the probable parent that appears in no source we read (verify before any use); its re-entry script assumed BOI promotion and treated the unproved parent as fact ("我們景宏"); it claimed battery makers require a trace "within 2 hours" (unevidenced, never to be said, as in §40); it named Taiwanese SME ERP vendors as likely local competitors with no evidence; and it treated a 2021 IPO kickoff as a live IPO plan. None may be used.

## §55 — Export plant of a PRC private parent (first full year ~฿450M upholstered seating to US retailers, 2.7 gross margin, loss, negative equity, group-funded) where a colleague's quote PAUSED because the Thai plant has no suitable finance person and the customer's IT-side "ERP owner" refuses the finance scope (NOVA live 2026-10-24 · job da936077 · KAIRUIZE SMART HOME run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Stage:** quote given, project paused, the contact rejects the finance scope, no schedule, no budget = **C2 (需求探詢 / 內部未共識)** whatever ACP says. Three facts restore B: a power holder (group CFO or the Thai plant GM) joins and confirms finance and cost control are in scope; a restart date and budget range (NOVA suggests tying it to a dated external clock such as a duty step-up); a commitment to an executive briefing, demo or requirement mapping.
- **The no-finance-person blocker:** NOVA's play is **finance kept by the parent's HQ finance team remotely on the Thai plant's system (option d)**, with phase 1 putting purchasing, stock and production live in Thailand while any local bookkeeping firm runs in parallel (option b). Reason: Chinese parents trust HQ finance most in the early overseas years, local finance hires are hard to find and keep, and HQ control of overseas assets and cash matches the group's intent. **Never help the customer recruit their finance person** (NOVA: "極不合適"): if the hire fails or commits fraud, DigiWin carries the relationship and legal risk, and it does not move the decision.
- **Phase 1 for an upholstered-seating export plant (NOVA's list):** purchasing, sales, stock, export documents (packing list, invoice); BOM, work orders, subcontracting; AR/AP/GL kept remotely by HQ. NOVA claims T100 handles dual units and multi-attribute cutting (fabric, leather, foam, frames) and Thai export documents and VAT/WHT as standard and "far better than Workflow". Claude vetting: unverified, and it conflicts with earlier rulings that a Thai-pack Workflow ERP exists (§40, §42); confirm with TH delivery before repeating; size by the KB §4 tier bands.
- **Origin and US duty (Section 232) as a wedge:** NOVA calls it a "golden" entry to the group owner. What may be said: the system keeps a BOM, purchase and work-order trail that supports answers on material origin and actual production. What may not: any promise that an origin audit will be passed. Claude vetting: NOVA's "one-click" origin-ratio report and its "reduce the risk of being judged an origin-laundering plant" are overclaims and are not to be said.
- **Route:** the parent's city DigiWin China unit (NOVA's guess for Kunshan: Shanghai or the Nanjing/south-Jiangsu unit) checks the China CRM by the parent's Chinese name or credit code, then calls high at HQ (finance director or chairman) on "who owns the Thai plant's books and cost"; the Thai field owner keeps the local contact. Claude vetting: NOVA told Peter to hand the contact to China sales and to open with "the Thai plant lost 12M, duty compliance risk, overseas funds out of control"; overridden: the field owner owns the contact, and the loss is raised only as a question (§43). NOVA also treated the unproven parent chairman as the confirmed owner.

## §56 — INSTALLED-BASE Thai plant on DigiWin's mainland-China line (E10 6.0) asking for an upgrade to 8.0 plus "a few users" after pausing a bigger ERP project for lack of staff; licence not sold by DigiWin TH (累計銷售金額 0); lead in a colleague's 私田 (NOVA live 2026-10-24 · job 6e2d4973 · POWER METAL (BANGKOK) run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Stage:** an installed-base upgrade with amount 0, no date, the customer's "internal discussion pending" and the larger project paused = **C2 (找權力人士提案 / 推動內部共識)** whatever ACP says. The paused larger project fails two of the three 立案 items (schedule, project team); the small one is still undecided.
- **Nature of the deal:** licence add-on + upgrade service on an existing customer: a defensive technical-maintenance case, not a new implementation. NOVA's rule of thumb: with a current MA a major-version licence upgrade is usually free or heavily discounted and the implementation service is charged separately; without MA, back-payment or repurchase. Claude vetting: general rule, unverified for any specific contract.
- **Delivery ownership (the key ruling):** NOVA says DigiWin TH's delivery lines are T100 and Workflow ERP; DigiWin TH cannot deliver or maintain E10, and E10 8.0 has no ready Thai localisation (VAT, WHT, e-Tax, BOI). An E10 upgrade for a Thai entity needs the China team (remote or on site): high delivery cost and a weak local-support position afterwards. **Never let a Thai SDR quote an E10 upgrade or promise a date before DigiWin China commits to delivery.** Claude vetting: NOVA's claim; confirm with TH delivery before repeating it to anyone outside the deal.
- **累計銷售金額 0 on an E10 site** = DigiWin TH has no direct transaction history; the licence was most likely bought by or through the group's mainland plant from DigiWin China (NOVA's inference). First internal step: the field owner asks DigiWin China (the mainland plant's city unit) and DigiWin TW for the group's contract, modules, MA status and AM.
- **"人手尚未齊備暫緩ERP導入" at a fast-growing plant:** key users are firefighting the volume and cannot do process mapping and testing; confirm whether the paused project was group-driven (one system) or local (BOI, capacity), and which function (finance or production control) could not spare people.
- **Scope:** put a multi-company set-up in the upgrade blueprint when a Thai sister exists (intercompany flows); phase 1 add = customer credit limits / AR ageing when receivables balloon; phase 2 = barcode + simple MES for stock and floor tracking. A customisation assessment of the old version must precede any upgrade date.
- **Risk:** "a few users + software upgrade" is low in value and high in service cost, a loss-making project if DigiWin TH carries the delivery alone.
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA drafted a question to the CFO quoting the plant's own receivables and stock growth from its filings (overridden: filings are raised only as questions about how the plant runs, §43/§55); called the stock rise "爆單導致盲目備料、倉庫失控" (overclaim, not to be said); called the die-casting sister the plant's "upstream or subcontractor" (plausible, unverified); and echoed back our own inputs (the IT contact's Taiwan e-mail domain, the group deciding) as if they were findings.

## §57 — Loss-making colour compounder on SAP where a DigiWin TW unit (鼎華, 台灣東南亞事業部) surveyed and quoted sMES (phase 1: standard + mixing-recipe module + maintenance + 30 users + 35 consulting days) and the customer paused on cost; lead inherited into a colleague's 私田 (NOVA live 2026-10-24 · job ebfc0b45 · CITC ENTERPRISE (THAI) run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Stage:** quote given, paused on cost, no date, no budget, contact not a signatory = **C2 (找權力人士提案)** whatever ACP says (same rule as §45/§51/§55). Three facts restore C1: a quantified must-do reason tied to the owner's money (margin, stock), a power holder engaged (a director or the parent), and a re-agreed budget and restart date.
- **Re-scope before discounting (NOVA's pattern for a thin-margin process plant):** cut named users (a compounder's floor has few key stations: weighing, charging, extrusion, packing) by using shared station accounts and floor boards; phase 1 = weigh-to-recipe error-proofing + production reporting + lot traceability + the basic SAP interface; move maintenance, QMS, SPC and IoT/machine connection to phase 2; consulting days fall with scope. NOVA sized the slimmed phase 1 at roughly 40–45% of the original (its own estimate, currency unknown); a proposal for the field owner, never a price promise.
- **Field questions NOVA proposes (usable, as questions):** material cost of hand-weighing errors (colour deviation, scrap, failed modification) per month; how many paper QC sheets and days it takes to trace a complaint to the raw-material lot and operator; how long each cleaning/changeover takes and what grouping by colour family would free.
- **Division of labour:** the Thai field owner keeps the local contact and builds a diagnostic with the plant manager (framed as fixing loss and stock, not buying a system); the original TW consultant gives the technical backing for the SAP interface; a TW account manager calls high at the Taiwan parent if one exists. First internal step: the TW CRM check for the parent's names.
- **Risks:** the SAP partner offering SAP's own light MES or "SAP can do simple reporting"; the customer's IT building a weighing tool in Excel/Access, which cannot post work-order release, consumption and close to SAP, so floor and books drift apart.
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA proposed **SFT** and subscription/instalments as lighter entries; SFT is not in the TH register (docs/chewie-corpus-index.md rule 1; skill rule "never SFC/SFT"), so in Thailand the light entry is a slimmed eMES phase 1, and subscription or instalments are proposals for TH management, not policy. NOVA **named the parent "中租資源科技, under 中租控股"** from the English name we gave it; no such ownership was found and it is NOT to be repeated. NOVA wrote a pitch to the owner promising that cutting stock to 150 days would "release tens of millions" and a script telling the plant manager the new budget is "only 40% of the original": both overridden (filings are raised only as questions, §43/§55; no price before TH HQ approval and before the quote currency is known). "鼎華 sMES has extremely strong colour-compounding templates" is an overclaim; only a mixing-recipe module line item is evidenced.

## §58 — Pre-revenue Thai plant of a Taiwan-LISTED parent that is an in-family T100 account, quote paused because the Thai plant's output is poor and HQ froze Thai investment "for the next few years", while the plant keeps hiring operators and engineers; lead in a colleague's 私田 (NOVA live 2026-10-24 · job 2aa6da6e · TAISOL ELECTRONICS (THAILAND) run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Stage:** a quote paused by an explicit multi-year capex freeze is **C2 (Pending) / 暫緩觀望**, never C1, even for a long-standing T100 customer: timeline and budget are gone, and a C1 inflates the forecast. Same direction as §40/§41/§57.
- **Freeze vs polite refusal:** NOVA reads continued hiring (operators, process engineers, R&D support) alongside "no investment" as a **CapEx freeze with OpEx continuing** — the plant must keep producing; IT that looks like back-office spend is cut first while output and margin are weak. Not a closure, not a brush-off.
- **Wake-up triggers (watch quarterly):** a Thai finance/accounting head or a materials/production-control manager posted (the plant starts running itself instead of being supported from Taiwan); main revenue above zero in the next DBD filing; a tax or audit event that forces local reports. Add, from the parent's side: the parent announcing that a higher-value line (e.g. liquid cooling) moves to Thailand.
- **What was most likely quoted (NOVA's reading, confirm with the field owner):** T100 multi-site extension + Thai localisation + Thai-site seats + local consulting on the parent's instance (consistent with §43), not a separate system.
- **Division of labour:** the TW AM for the parent leads (call high on the COO / HQ IT; within seven days confirm T100 version and MA status, HQ IT's blueprint for overseas plants, and where the Thai quote stalled); the Thai SDR scouts (DBD changes, JobThai postings, plant status) and brings Thai tax and asset know-how. The Thai plant has no decision power while the parent funds and signs.
- **Risks to watch during the freeze:** (1) the Thai plant settling into Excel or a local accounting firm, a habit that raises the cost of a later rollout; (2) HQ IT opening a Thai organisation inside the Taiwan T100 over VPN without Thai localisation or extra seats, which leaves Thai tax invoices and any BOI records manual. The second is for the AM's internal judgment, never an accusation to the customer.
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA assumed BOI promotion (unverified for this plant) and called Thai e-Tax mandatory (not verified; do not say it). It proposed a "微型 T100 啟動專案" re-using HQ licences with only a small Thai service fee: a pricing and licensing promise that needs TH HQ approval. Its AM opener ("協助規劃 1.3 億資產的合規管理") and SDR script quoted the customer's filing and an unevidenced "equipment arriving" fact at them (filings are raised only as questions, §43/§55), and it used an invented "implementation cost doubles" figure.

## §59 — Sub-gate Thai-Taiwanese fresh-fish TRADER (no BOM, ~฿60M + a same-address sister ~฿75M, net margin 1.4 at best, D/E 3.4, interest-free short-term funding) asking a colleague for an ERP (re-quoted with more custom time) AND a partner AS/RS with four-way shuttles quoted through DigiWin, then silent 81 days: D (Pending); software and hardware split; light cloud trading scope (NOVA live 2026-10-24 · job e463a2c0 · AMAZON INTERNATIONAL run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Grade:** quotes sent, amount 0, the only window (the GM) not answering, no date = **D (需求探詢 / Pending)**, not C1 and not C2: NOVA drops below C2 when the window itself is unreachable. Restore C1 with: (1) the GM reached and confirming the stock pain in his own numbers (space, spoilage, finding lots), (2) a known funding source for ERP and AS/RS (shareholders, bank, related-party loans), (3) a named project owner and a go-live date.
- **Tier and first cut for a no-BOM perishable trader below the iGP Go band:** never T100; Workflow ERP cloud / iGP light, first cut = multi-company books (two same-site companies, shared stock view), stock by lot and expiry (FIFO / first-expiry-first-out), cold-store locations as LOGICAL bins first (no automation hardware yet), sales and shipping documents (invoice, packing list). Not MES, not APS.
- **"Custom programming time increased" on a small trader's quote = the likely cause of silence:** the customer sees cost without an end. Answer = 嚴控客製，回歸標準 — list each custom item against a named flow, move non-essentials to phase 2, aim for a short standard go-live.
- **AS/RS on a ~฿10M-equity balance sheet:** unrealistic on the operating books; any funding comes from shareholders (often as interest-free 股東往來) or a bank. DigiWin = the brain (ERP + WMS); WCS, racking and shuttles contracted by the partner directly with the customer. **Never re-quote partner hardware on DigiWin's paper**: if the hardware fails (more likely in a cold store) the customer withholds the software payments. Consistent with §48 / §50.
- **Contract entity between two same-address sisters:** the one with more equity and the GM's own signature; name the sister in the licence scope (§7).
- **30-day move when the GM stops answering:** stop chasing the price; one value-first touch by LINE/e-mail on lot and expiry control for fish stock; no presales resources until funding is confirmed; no price cuts (silence is "not ready / not decided", not "too expensive").
- **Taiwan angle:** with Taiwanese co-holders, ask the field owner to check through DigiWin TW whether the GM or a related director appears in Taiwan's company registry with a seafood business that is a DigiWin TW account; a lead, not a known link.
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA said the GM "holds the 48" and is "the absolute KDM" (DBD shows two Taiwanese holders, names unknown); called the Thai 52 "nominees" (a legal allegation; do not repeat); said the sister's fixed assets jumped "in one year" (spread over three); asserted the sister's director "certainly" has a Taiwanese seafood parent and told the SDR to visit "the Taiwan HQ" (no parent evidenced); proposed a white paper promising "15 lower stock losses", an opener claiming peers suffered from cold-store mismanagement and "tens of millions" of hardware, a customer question quoting the filings (stock "tripled to 2,000萬") and assuming a "new Thai plant's cold store", a "standard aquatic-wholesale package" and "1-3 months" go-live. None may be used.
