## §60 — Chinese-run Thai BOI plant (heater/fan contract assembly for a US brand, first full year ~฿350M at a gross LOSS, group-funded, 4 BOI certificates, one near 36 months) where KINGDEE is already in a solution PoC and the customer "has not decided to change system"; Thai books on Express; lead in a colleague's 私田 with no opportunity record (NOVA live 2026-10-24 · job 37ae4b3f · BAIYUSHUN TECHNOLOGY (THAILAND) run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Stage:** a competitor's PoC is not the customer's commitment. No quantified must-do reason, no customer project team or named counterpart, no timetable = **C2 (需求探詢 / 尋求內部共識)** whatever ACP says. C1 needs all three: (1) a quantified reason (e.g. BOI raw-material write-off that Express cannot carry, or the group mandating its system), (2) an appointed counterpart (Thai finance or production lead), (3) a decision month and a go-live month.
- **Kingdee at a PRC-group Thai plant — what may be said:** Thai Revenue Department documents in Thai, e-Tax Invoice and e-Receipt integration, BOI machinery and raw-material write-off reports, delivery and support by a team in Thailand, shop-floor depth (moulds, work reporting). **What may not:** that the system files BOI automatically or is "100 percent connected" to government systems (it supplies the report formats; filing stays with the BOI IC system or the broker); that it repairs the past 36 months of manual records. Claude vetting: NOVA's "Kingdee projects in Thailand easily fail" and "Kingdee's Thai localisation is bolt-on and costly" are unverified competitor claims — internal only, never to a customer.
- **BOI 36 months:** NOVA reads it as the deadline to request the full-operation inspection; after it, raw-material formula write-off and stock balance are audited strictly — a must-do pain, to be raised as a question about how they prepare the reports, never as a threat of back-tax.
- **Tier:** NOVA leaned Workflow ERP (iGP) first for a loss-making ~฿350M plant (phase 1: purchasing/sales/stock + manufacturing + basic finance + Thai localisation with BOI reports, replacing Express). Claude vetting: NOVA put 生產排程 in that iGP phase 1 — iGP has no APS (LRP + 異常報表); and KB §4 puts ฿300–500M revenue in the T100 Caution band with capital ฿211M in T100 Go, so the tier stays open for /select-t100-vs-workflow.
- **Investment adviser in the deal (here "江力", unidentified):** advisers who handle BOI and set-up for Chinese investors often hold the owner's ear. NOVA wanted Peter to approach the adviser directly and use it to warn the owner of BOI tax risk; overridden: the field owner owns the account, any adviser contact is agreed with her first, and no fear-selling or "避稅" language.
- **Group check:** ask DigiWin China to search its CRM for the group's Chinese name and credit code; if the group is a DigiWin China account, play group integration; if it runs Kingdee, play the Thai plant's local needs. Claude vetting: NOVA echoed our own guessed Chinese characters for the group as fact; a name match is not a parent link.

## §61 — Thai plant of a PRC private group whose WHOLE GROUP is upgrading to T100 with the DigiWin China city team, the SDR's split being "mainland signed and delivered by the city team, Thai part by DigiWin TH"; small Thai plant (~฿83M, first profit, automotive stamping/CNC under IATF), opportunity at 方案提報 with amount 0, 36 days silent, lead in a colleague's 私田 (NOVA live 2026-10-24 · job ea9ef763 · LUXIN TECHNOLOGY (THAILAND) run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Stage:** a group programme announced by the SDR is not a Thai C1. Without a Thai must-do reason, a Thai project counterpart and a Thai timetable (and with a long reporting gap) = **C2 (找權力人士提案 / 內部未共識)**. C1 = the city AM confirms the mainland contract is signed AND the Thai GM names a Thai counterpart and a Thai rollout date. B = a Thai-localisation gap analysis plus a demo the Thai management accepts.
- **Architecture:** one T100 instance, the Thai entity as its own site (multi-organisation, multi-site); a ~฿80M plant does not justify its own T100, and sharing keeps BOM, material transfer and consolidation in one place.
- **Contract and credit split (NOVA's proposal):** licences bought by the group through the DigiWin China city team (group volume); Thai localisation package (VAT ภ.พ.30, WHT ภ.ง.ด.3/53, BOI reports where promoted) and bilingual consulting contracted by the Thai entity with DigiWin TH; credit split filed jointly by the two field owners. Claude vetting: NOVA named a "跨境聯動單" form and a "software to China, services to Thailand" split rule; neither is verified as current policy; confirm with management before quoting it.
- **Phase 1 (Thai site):** Thai finance and tax (VAT, WHT, AR/AP, GL) + purchase/sales/stock + BOM and work orders (material issue, completion) for basic IATF lot trace. **Red lines:** no die/mould management or eMES in phase 1 at a plant still standardising its processes; never promise a simultaneous go-live; the Thai rollout follows the city's core model by about 3–6 months to avoid rework.
- **Peter's role beside the field owner:** set up an internal group with the field owner and the city AM, and hand the field owner a Thai-rollout pack (localisation + a verified nearby reference) to present; no direct customer contact.
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA put an unverified customer (a large EMS maker in the same estate) into a customer-facing question, quoted the plant's own inventory days back at it, and asserted an "industry standard 90 days" inventory target worth "about ฿15M" of released cash: all overridden (unsourced benchmark; KB §43 says ask, never quote filings; customer link unproven). It also invented a nearby "supply-chain reference" and an example audit year, and echoed the Chinese characters our own question guessed for a director's name.

## §62 — DigiWin-sold AGV already DELIVERED (two units, price-haggled first), then a follow-on quote for 6 barcode PDAs with no software line, ERP unknown, at a certified hygienic-stainless fittings plant (ASME BPE, 3-A, PED; issues EN 10204 3.1 material certificates); lead in a colleague's 私田 (NOVA live 2026-10-24 · job 4f2c3597 · G HWA Industries run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Stage:** a won hardware sale followed by a hardware-only quote (PDAs) with the expected signing date lapsed, no software scope, no customer project owner and ~33 days without a report is **C2 (需求探詢 / 找權力人士提案)** for the software opportunity, whatever ACP says. Three facts restore C1: a quantified must-do reason tied to the AGV go-live (booking and moves not posted in real time once the AGVs run), a named customer project owner for barcode/material flow (with IT or production in the evaluation), and a dated go-live for PDA software alongside the AGV commissioning. Same rule as §45/§47/§50.
- **First question is who owns the PDA software, not which ERP they run.** A PDA on an AGV site usually serves one of three things: calling/dispatching the AGV (WCS), posting stock moves into an existing ERP/WMS barcode module, or replacing old handhelds on an in-house or local-SI app. Behavioural checks the field owner can make without asking "what system do you use": how operators will call the AGVs after installation (wall buttons or PDA app); whether the PDA spec names an OS or a specific app that must connect to their back end; and whether finance books the PDAs as an add-on to the AGV project or as a separate IT/warehouse purchase.
- **First software cut for a plant that issues 3.1 material certificates:** NOVA leads with **heat-number and lot genealogy (eMES)**, not WMS location control, because the compliance pain is matching heat number, process records (heat treatment, leak test, polishing) and the certificate to each piece. Claude vetting: §1/§47 still hold: eMES-first assumes an ERP of record; while the ERP is unknown the order stays open, and barcode receiving of raw tube by heat number is the common first step of either path.
- **Package hardware as one material-flow design, never two hardware tickets:** PDA scan (heat/lot) → system check → AGV move; DigiWin's software is the brain, the AGV and PDA the execution (consistent with §45/§50). Risk NOVA names: if the PDAs are only a device refresh for a local SI's app, planning a big system is a resource mismatch; confirm software ownership at AGV installation.
- **Multinational shareholding with no visible parent (German / Taiwanese / Australian):** the system decision is not readable from the registry. First move for a researcher: identify any Taiwan-side company of the directors and shareholders through DigiWin TW before proposing anything; the field owner asks whether the plant manager or a group/HQ IT reviews the PDA spec.
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA invented a Chinese name for the company ("泰國吉華"; also "吉華/巨華"), read four of five directors as Taiwanese (two have English given names; only two romanisations read Taiwanese) and put an unsourced 90 percent on Taiwanese control although the single largest holder is German (39.49 vs 39.11 for ten Taiwanese holders), called James Lee 總經理 (a 2018 article calls him president), and offered a customer line threatening "百萬貨物退貨" over a certificate error. None may be used.

## §63 — Small ramping Thai plant of a PRC private group that is being ACQUIRED by a PRC-listed company (deal pending exchange review and CSRC registration), group back office run centrally from the parent HQ, colleague's quote to the Thai GM silent ~90 days (NOVA live 2026-10-24 · job 0ec59443 · AGIX Optical Technology (Thailand) run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Stage:** one visit plus a quote sent the same day, then ~90 days without a report, contact = the Thai GM who is not a registry signatory = **D (需求探詢)** per NOVA, below the C2 it gave similar silent quotes (§40, §54): NOVA reads the quote as a one-way seller action, not a customer buying act. Three facts restore C1/B: (1) who approves the Thai plant's ERP purchase at the parent HQ or at the acquirer, and by what process; (2) a quantified must-do reason with a deadline tied to the acquirer's consolidation and internal-control integration (and any profit-commitment period); (3) a budget source (HQ allocation or Thai self-funded) and a go-live date. Claude vetting: the skill's OSINT field may still read C2-candidate on the evidence; record both.
- **A pending acquisition does not freeze the decision, per NOVA; it can make it must-do** (listed-company internal control and consolidation reach every subsidiary). Decision power sits with the acquirer and the target's HQ; the Thai GM has a recommending and cooperating role only. First question to settle: what ERP do the acquirer and the target each run today, and who will set the Thai plant's finance-consolidation and IT-control standard after closing. Claude vetting: until registration the acquirer has no control, so treat "must-do" as a hypothesis to test with the field owner, not a line for the customer.
- **First cut (NOVA):** a stand-alone Thai Workflow ERP with an interface reserved for later integration, covering Thai localisation and BOI, with finance passed back to the parent; never a private cut-price Thai quote before the HQ IT plan is clear. Claude vetting: these two conflict unless sequenced; check the CRM first (is either HQ a DigiWin China account, on what product), and only then shape the Thai offer (same-instance extension per §43 if a DigiWin product runs at HQ, otherwise the stand-alone shape).
- **Route:** internal CRM check on both the target HQ and the acquirer, then the DigiWin China AMs in both HQ cities ask each HQ IT about the Thai plant plan; the field owner calls high with them; Peter supplies the BOI localisation pack and briefing, no customer contact.
- **Never say:** "let's talk after your acquisition closes" (passive waiting) and "Thailand can sign cheaply first" (breaks group pricing before HQ IT is aligned).
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA wrote "否則 100% 掉單" and that waiting hands the deal to "外商或光庫既有友商": both unevidenced (no incumbent at the acquirer is known), not to be repeated. It suggested giving the GM "跨國製造業內控合規案例": no such verified case is in the mirror; case-name hygiene applies.

## §64 — Small single-customer PCBA plant (PRC-majority, Thai plant serving one brand's Thai factory, current volume "not enough to sustain operations") whose NEW customer makes an MES a condition of business; colleague's sMES quote called "too high", project gated on the new customer's orders landing in Thailand; lead in a colleague's 私田 (NOVA live 2026-10-24 · job 821b9241 · HUALIN TECH (THAILAND) run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Stage:** a customer-imposed MES with the project waiting on a third party's order, contact = production manager (not a signatory), approver = an owner not yet met = **D (需求探詢)** per NOVA, whatever ACP says (C1 here). Three facts restore C1: (1) a quantified must-do with a date: the new customer named, its audit checklist and audit date on record; (2) a project owner with authority (the boss or a director engaged); (3) a timetable and a budget the boss accepts. Claude vetting: the skill's OSINT field may still read C2-candidate (a stated, specific need); record both.
- **Wedge = help the plant win the customer, not wait for the order.** Position the MES as the plant's ticket to new business. Minimum phase 1 for a brand customer's PCBA audit (NOVA): barcode feeder/reel loading verification; per-board route tracking (SMT → DIP → test → pack) to stop rejects escaping; key-IC lots and ICT/FCT results bound to the board serial. Everything else (OEE, machine connection, full WMS) is phase 2, framed as funded from the new customer's revenue.
- **Price objection: cut scope first, then talk price.** Never discount first: before the boss's intent is known and the scope is cut, a discount only signals low value, and a loss-making plant still cannot pay.
- **ERP first.** Ask the ERP of record before quoting interfaces. With no ERP, never sell the MES alone (work orders, BOM and item master must come from somewhere): offer a light ERP + sMES package to the owner. If the owner refuses an ERP, NOVA's fallback is an Excel master-data template imported into the MES plus a contract clause excluding ERP integration (a proposal for the field owner, not policy).
- **Owner route:** with a PRC majority and no visible parent, ask the DigiWin China team to trace the parent in the Chinese business registry (romanised name, likely Chinese name, directors' names) and whether it is an account; call high on the parent with the China team. The production manager recommends, the Thai head evaluates, the owner approves.
- **Never say:** "we will talk once you have the new customer's order" (passive waiting); "too expensive, let us try a discount" (before scope and intent); "no ERP, no project" (offer the package or set the boundary).
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA wrote a customer script promising "價格直接降到原來的 XX%" (a price promise; needs TH management), addressed the owner as "林董" on a surname-only input, read the new customer as possibly the existing brand customer (the SDR's report says otherwise), claimed calling high is "有效 10 倍" and that a rival would step in while we wait (both unsourced), and said a PCBA plant "cannot run without an ERP" (its view; small plants run on spreadsheets, §2). It told a non-owner channel head to "order" the stage down; the channel head can only recommend it to the field owner's sales leader. None of these may be repeated.

## §65 — INSTALLED-BASE Workflow account whose only open item is a small seat add-on ("WF增購5U", quote sent, amount 0, 累計銷售金額 0) at a plant whose revenue doubled while gross margin fell to about one point, with a sister plant that shows no system; lead in a colleague's 私田 (NOVA live 2026-10-24 · job 27098273 · Winbest Biotech (Thailand) run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Stage:** a seat add-on is a transactional order, not a project: no quantified must-do, no project team, no go-live date = **C2**, whatever ACP says (C1 here). Same rule as §38/§51/§56. Use the add-on as the door-opener and re-open C1 only on a phase-2 project with its own 立案 items.
- **累計銷售金額 0 on a logo-wall customer (NOVA's two likeliest readings):** the licence was sold and booked by DigiWin TW (OM model, licence assigned to the Thai entity), or it was bought before DigiWin TH's ACP went live and the history was never migrated. Neither is verified for any account.
- **Internal checks before the add-on is invoiced:** (1) the licence serial, module list and MA status in DigiWin TW's records; (2) which consultant implemented it and the close-out report or customisation list; (3) which legal entity the add-on quote is addressed to, so licensing and invoicing match the contract entity.
- **Phase 2 at a revenue-doubling, margin-collapsing plant = item-level actual cost (material, labour and overhead allocation) first,** ahead of lot traceability or SFT/eMES; the owner question is which items lose money as volume grows. Timing: a system health check after the add-on closes, run by the consultant, quoted by the field owner.
- **Licence compliance in a group:** confirm whether the existing contract limits use to one legal entity; watch for sister-company staff logging into the add-on seats.
- **Credit risk:** at a thin or negative margin with large long-term debt, any larger project needs a higher first payment.
- **Channel head (non-owner) role:** do not join the add-on price talk and do not ask for the signatory before the contract history and the pain are known; equip the field owner (cost/stock health-check checklist, help pulling DigiWin TW history) and offer to attend the first senior meeting once the field owner secures it.
- **Conflict with §35 (unresolved):** NOVA here said the ~฿257M sister should be a new T100 / Digiwin ERP opportunity and that Workflow is too inflexible for multi-company, while §35 (one day earlier, same group) ruled Workflow plus iGP/EasyFlow with multi-company in the blueprint and T100 too heavy at a 3 net-margin plant. §4 gates (T100 No-Go below ฿300M revenue or 120 staff) favour §35. Keep the tier open and route it to /select-t100-vs-workflow; never quote either ruling as settled.
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA supplied Chinese characters for both signatories (林明輝, 林宜宇) that appear in no source; the ACP contact record writes 林益宇 for Yi Yu Lin. It assumed a Taiwan parent company that no source shows (the register shows two Taiwanese shareholders only). Its owner script quotes the plant's own filing back at him (revenue, AR days, fixed-asset spend; overridden per §43/§55: filings become questions about how the plant runs). It called the business "虛胖型企業" and the deal "百萬級黃金商機" (unsourced characterisations). None may be repeated.

## §66 — SAP Business One REPLACEMENT referred by a DigiWin China city office: a fast-growing (~฿810M) Chinese-run private-label auto-filter exporter at the centre of a five-company Thai chain (media, carbon, cartons, trading) plus a US plant; SDR estimate "ERP + WMS, 29 + 10 users, 450萬", the only ACP contact a DigiWin China colleague, 36 days silent; lead in a colleague's 私田 (NOVA live 2026-10-24 · job 12e5e6b3 · LESHA (THAILAND) run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Stage:** an SDR estimate with no customer person, no stated pain and no timetable is a one-way sales action, not a buying cycle = **C2**, whatever ACP says (C1 here). Same rule as §40/§42/§61. Three facts restore C1/B: (a) a power holder (the Thai signatory directors or the parent's boss) states support for replacing B1 and names a Thai project owner; (b) the customer's own trigger is known (audit, customer label or traceability demand, cash or costing pain); (c) a budget range and a dated go-live or signing month the customer accepts.
- **Tier:** for a group that buys inputs from its own sister companies and sells through an affiliate abroad, NOVA prefers **T100** (multi-organisation, inter-company trade, consolidation) over Workflow ERP; consistent with §4 (T100 Go: revenue ≥฿500M, ≥200 staff, multi-factory). NOVA reads a 450萬 THB estimate for 29 ERP + 10 WMS users as a Workflow-or-B1-upgrade price and calls it far too low for T100 + WMS (its own range ฿7–10M; unverified, internal only). If the parent's China plant runs E10/T100, the Thai plant follows on T100 for shared item, BOM and customer codes; if the parent runs 易助, the larger Thai plant leads on T100 and the parent follows later. Claude vetting: tier still routes through /select-t100-vs-workflow once the field owner knows scope and entities.
- **Why companies of this size leave B1 (NOVA's priors, to test, not assert):** thin production/MRP depth for an IATF plant; no native WMS, so labels and container checks run on paper or add-ons; multi-company and costing gaps. **Expected counter:** the B1 partner offers a WMS or production add-on instead of a replacement (NOVA's price guess ฿1.5–2M, unverified). Answer: one data base for ERP, WMS and floor barcode, so label-format changes and upgrades do not break interfaces.
- **WMS phase 1 at a private-label exporter:** the main plant only: finished-goods barcode receipt, customer label printing driven by the shipping order, carton and container load scanning. Sister plants go into the ERP blueprint from day one, NOT into WMS phase 1 (scope creep kills the project).
- **Cross-border roles (three-way team):** the DigiWin China colleague in the parent's city calls high at the parent (what it runs, who holds the Thai IT budget, the family's decision chain); the Thai field owner works the plant (finance, IT, plant head; collects B1 pains); the TH channel head coordinates presales on both sides and steers the price toward a scope that can be delivered, without contacting the customer. First step: an internal Teams/WeChat group of the three, and the China colleague's check on the parent's system and budget owner. NOVA's prior that "about 80 percent of budget sits at the China parent" is a prior only; check the register (here 85 percent Thai capital) before assuming it.
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA framed the case as an inventory crisis (stock +50) and promised to cut stock below ฿100M and free ฿40M; inventory days were flat (85 vs 86) and the promise is unsourced; never say it. Its scripts quote the filing back at the owner (overridden per §43/§55). It treated Stellantis as a label-compliance customer and AIAG labels as required; only a Stellantis award is evidenced and the US buyers are aftermarket brands. None may be repeated.

## §67 — INSTALLED-BASE Workflow seat add-on quoted "for budgeting" at a loss-making, NEGATIVE-EQUITY joint-venture plant (Thai industrial group + Taiwanese partner), signing date passed and ~80 days without a report, with a large profitable sister (~฿1.16B) whose system is unknown; lead in a colleague's 私田 (NOVA live 2026-10-24 · job 6e80e511 · Thaimaxwell Solar run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Stage:** a seat add-on quoted only "for the customer to budget", whose expected signing date has passed with no report since, at a plant with negative equity and an external-market shock, grades **D (觀望 / re-qualify)**, below ACP's C2. Contrast §65 (a freshly sent seat quote at a funded plant = C2): the difference is the passed date, the silence and the cash position, which make a budget freeze the likeliest reading.
- **First move (channel head, not field owner):** a case review with the field owner, who calls the IT contact and the GM within about three days to learn whether the add-on is stuck in budget approval or the seats are no longer needed. Then stop selling "N users": reframe around the plant's cash problem (stock ageing and purchase control, then job cost), with the seats folded into that conversation.
- **Phase-2 order at a mixed module-plant + EPC-projects company with a stock spike:** stock control first, project (job) cost second, lot traceability third.
- **Partner that builds its own MES (the Taiwanese JV partner):** short-term partner, mid-term risk. Offer a clean split, ERP (finance, stock, compliance) with DigiWin and shop-floor MES with the partner, joined by API; watch for the partner writing its own stock or finance functions.
- **Group extension:** a large profitable sister sharing directors is worth pursuing, through the GM of the installed plant as sponsor and senior-to-senior with the group's directors, AFTER the add-on question is settled; NOVA leans T100 for a ~฿1.16B transformer maker (complex BOM, design changes, copper/steel price swings), consistent with §4 gates. Tier still routes to /select-t100-vs-workflow.
- **JV decision chain:** the partner-side GM proposes and evaluates; the majority group's directors approve money. Where the registry signing clause forbids named pairs from co-signing, ask early which two eligible signatories are in-country so a contract does not stall.
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA's GM question quoted the filing back at him (overridden per §43/§55); it assumed module exports and an MES already running at the plant (neither evidenced); it framed a Thai-registered sister as a 台商 exchange; it proposed an "AI data-centre compliance and cyber-security" white paper for the group (not a DigiWin offer); it equated the unnamed 55% Thai stake with the group side; and it suggested reclaiming the 私田 if the SDR does not act (a call for the SDR's leader, not the researcher). None may be repeated as fact or used as a script.

## §68 — Thai plant (ramping, pre-revenue in its filings) of a Taiwan-LISTED parent on an OLD TIPTOP GP (5.1x) that is "hard to assess" for T100, where the stated need is Thai e-invoicing the old version reportedly cannot support and the SDR proposes a separate Thai T100; contact on the parent's domain, lead in a colleague's 私田 (NOVA live 2026-10-24 · job 6d599d8f · ITEQ Corporation (Thailand) run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Stage:** a stated need with no quote, no date, no project team and a contact who cannot sign = **C2 (找權力人士提案 / 需求探詢)**, whatever the urgency of the need. Three facts restore C1: HQ IT or finance formally authorises the Thai plant to evaluate its own ERP and names a Thai project contact; a go-live date backed out from the plant's ramp; a must-do reason wider than e-invoicing (Thai tax, multi-unit stock and loss, asset/BOI records).
- **Play = two-tier, not parent-first and not e-invoice-only.** A separate T100 for the Thai plant with Thai localisation, tied back to the parent's old TIPTOP. A parent-first upgrade is too slow for a ramping plant; an e-invoice-only interface "治標不治本". Name three risks up front with their fixes: consolidation (map the Thai chart of accounts to the parent's, send balances back on a schedule), intercompany (parent shipments become Thai receipts automatically and vice versa), item master (parent stays the source of item codes; the Thai plant adds only local consumables and packaging).
- **e-Tax placement:** never the first, standalone order; it is an output of sales and AR in phase 1 (same as §28).
- **Phase 1 for a CCL/prepreg plant:** GL/AP/AR, cash, Thai tax; purchasing, sales, stock with multi-unit conversion (sheets, square metres, weight) and lot control; BOM, work orders, cost; BOI records only if the project is promoted. Claude vetting: TH register uses work orders in phase 1 and eMES for floor actuals in phase 2 (NOVA named SFC).
- **Division of labour:** DigiWin TW's key-account owner for the parent calls high on HQ IT and finance in the parent's city and carries the two-tier argument; the Thai SDR visits the plant's finance lead and build project manager; Thai consultants bring a Thai e-Tax demo and trilingual delivery proof. First question to HQ IT: how they plan to support the Thai plant's Thai tax, e-Tax and (if any) BOI records, and whether a Thai T100 with an interface back has been considered.
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA merged a capacity phase due at year-end with a later second plant; asserted BOI status and an "OOC shipments / BOI 免稅資格" risk without evidence; gave go-live ("6~9 個月") and upgrade ("1~2 年") durations without basis; and proposed "台灣簽約、泰國收款/交付" (contract entity and cross-border withholding are for the field owner and DigiWin TW, §17). None may be repeated as fact. Thai e-Tax Invoice is voluntary as of 2026, so the reason the customer wants it must be asked, not assumed.

## §69 — Profitable Thai plant of a PRC state-owned LISTED tungsten group where group IT told the SDR "ERP no chance (global rollout), but MES/WMS/AS-RS are chosen by each regional subsidiary"; the plant's own job ads describe monthly ERP stock posting, book-vs-floor reconciliation, dead stock and a capacity ramp; Thai GM signs alone below a registry threshold, the parent's GM countersigns above it; lead in a colleague's 私田 (NOVA live 2026-10-24 · job 10448ac4 · Golden Egret Cement Carbide (Thailand) run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Stage:** group-level "ERP closed, plant systems open" plus pain visible only in OSINT (job ads), with amount 0, no Thai-side project contact and ~3 weeks silent = **C2**. Three facts restore C1: a quantified must-do loss from the plant itself (stoppages from shortages, stock-count write-offs, compliance exposure), a named Thai project owner authorised by the GM (warehouse or production-control head), and a selection/go-live date tied to the plant's own ramp.
- **First cut = WMS + barcode, then eMES, then AS/RS.** Reason: the plant's own ads put the pain in the physical stock layer (book vs floor, dead/over-stock, material balance), and a floor still in commissioning and new-product introduction is a poor time for MES. AS/RS is hardware, last, with DigiWin WMS as the brain over the partner's WCS (§50).
- **Cemented-carbide MES specifics NOVA lists (qualitative, use as discovery prompts, not claims):** grade/recipe control of tungsten and cobalt powders; many-to-many links between powder lots, pressing and sintering-furnace runs; green-versus-sintered dimension records to watch shrinkage; continuous grinding/cut-off reporting with wheel wear and inspection rejects (the plant's QE ad names ultrasonic inspection).
- **Order of moves:** DigiWin China's office in the parent's city first (is the group a 鼎捷 account, what it runs for ERP/MES/WMS, who covers it), in parallel the Thai field owner opens with the GM on book-vs-floor and dead stock. The researcher translates OSINT into the field owner's brief; never contacts the customer.
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA proposed sizing phase 1 under the GM's solo-signing threshold so the parent's GM need not countersign; designing a deal around a customer's internal approval rule is not adopted (know who signs; never split to avoid a countersignature). NOVA asserted the group "極大機率" is a 鼎捷 customer with "長期合作歷史" and cited a Zhuzhou cutting-tool maker as a DigiWin benchmark; neither appears in the 662 mirrored TW cases or the Monk cards (grep 2026-10-24), so neither may be said. It gave a shrinkage figure (about 20) without a source, leaned on a BOI-compliance risk although BOI status is unverified, and wrote "自動回傳集團" into a GM script (interface claims follow §22: standard APIs plus per-project mapping, never automatic). None may be used as fact.

## §70 — Sub-gate Thai plant (~฿4M revenue, two loss years, parent-funded) of a Taiwan-OTC parent that is an INSTALLED-BASE Workflow group, parent in a management change (new chairman and GM, board seats changing hands), Thai board replaced, the SDR's finance contact gone and "泰國單獨部署" floated; lead in a colleague's 私田 (NOVA live 2026-10-24 · job 06f37841 · JVE (Thailand) run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Stage:** no window, no budget, no date and a broken contact chain = **D (需求探詢)**, even when ACP shows C2 and the group is a customer. Three facts restore C1 (立案三要項): a quantified must-do (e.g. group consolidation timing or a Thai compliance gap the parent states), a named project window authorised by the parent's GM or IT, and a go-live month signed off by the board or GM. Claude vetting: this departs from §35 (C2 for a zero-contact sister of a customer); the difference is size (sub-gate, §23) and a dead contact chain, so D here, C2 there.
- **Shape of any Thai scope at this size:** extend the group's existing Workflow with a Thai company (multi-company) plus the Thai tax pack; a separate Thai Workflow is not worth it (TCO on a loss-making plant). Phase 1 = Thai VAT/WHT, receiving/shipping and stock, simple BOM with simple work orders (issue and receipt) for costing; no MES. Lighter fallbacks: Thai staff on the Taiwan system over the web, Taiwan finance keying Thai documents, or a DigiWin light cloud option. Consistent with §7 (Workflow multi-company = separate books, manual intercompany, no built-in consolidation) and §43 (same instance, not a separate system, when the parent already runs DigiWin).
- **Re-finding contacts after a management change:** first the DigiWin TW account manager for the parent (contract entity, modules, version, MA, who maintains the system, who the window was, anything the new management has said about IT or overseas plants); never cold-approach the Thai directors, who are parent-appointed and matter at signing. The decision sits with the parent's new GM and IT where the parent funds the plant and set its approval table.
- **Installed-base defence during a change of control:** the TW account manager offers the new GM/IT a health-check visit on the existing Workflow (what it does today, what the parent's own IT/MES plan would need), before anyone else is invited; same sequencing logic as §37. Risks to watch: the new team bringing a familiar outside ERP, and the small plant drifting to a cheap local package (data island, §58 risk 1).
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA's sample question to the new management quoted the plant's fixed-asset figure back at them (filings stay questions, §43/§55); it assumed a new CFO in post and that part of a capital injection was earmarked for IT (the filing says long-term investment, no operating change); it offered "save at least 70 percent of cost and 50 percent of go-live time" (invented, never repeat) and "decision power 100 percent in Taiwan" (overstatement; the direction stands); and it called an outside-ERP switch by the new GM "極可能" with no evidence.

## §71 — Large profitable Thai F&B plant (~฿2B, RTD juice/tea, 100% of a Taiwan-listed food conglomerate that runs its own IT company) on a SELF-BUILT system the users say is failing, the customer's only condition being "unless DigiWin commits people to CO-CREATE"; lead in a colleague's 私田 (NOVA live 2026-10-24 · job 3c7ad201 · Uni-President (Thailand) run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Stage:** one SDR report ("自研的用不好，需求是有的，除非我們有人力投入一起共創"), amount 0, no date, no named decision-maker = **D (需求探詢)** even with ACP at C2 and a strong profile. Three facts restore C1 (立案三要項): a quantified must-do the customer itself agrees to (e.g. cost per SKU, stock/expiry control), a named project owner (plant manager or an HQ IT delegate), and an RFI/RFP or go-live date. Same behaviour-sequence logic as §29/§70.
- **Reading "共創":** treat as a RED FLAG for an open-ended custom project (the customer may see the vendor as a cheap outsourced software shop). Answer = standard product + industry practice + scoped extension, opened through a PAID diagnosis or workshop; never modify or take over the customer's in-house system; never promise free customisation. Claude nuance: a strong in-house IT team may simply want a technical peer (DigiWin TW case 1024, 久津實業 2018: EasyFlow integrated with a self-developed ERP); ask what "co-create" means before reacting.
- **Tier and first cut at ฿2B process F&B with a sister distributor:** T100 (KB §4 thresholds). First cut = T100 core (lot, recipe, cost) + WMS (FEFO/expiry, write-down risk, plant↔distributor stock and reconciliation); plant on full manufacturing and costing, distributor on distribution + WMS, one design. MES/QMS later (KB §1).
- **First move:** DigiWin TW's key-account owner for the parent group (is the group or any sister an account, sister's system status, HQ IT's stance on overseas plants); the researcher is the intelligence bridge, the field owner leads. Proof points to bring: an in-group sister's move from custom to DigiWin (verify currency first) and food process cases.
- **In-group build risk (group IT company or HQ system copied):** answer with Thai localisation (tax, e-Tax, Thai/EN/ZH, local consultants) and food process-manufacturing depth (recipe, line yield, process cost). NOVA's claim that a retail-focused group IT company is weak in manufacturing is unverified; use it as a question, not an assertion.
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA's sample questions quoted the filing back at the customer (net margin, inventory figure; §43); it called 65 receivable days and 74 inventory days "極高" (moderate); it asserted the customer "cannot pass OEM traceability audits" (no evidence); it labelled the group IT company with the retail chain's ticker; "不用錢的最貴，開發完往往是災難的開始" disparages the customer's IT and is not used; "40 years of process-manufacturing experience" is not a verified DigiWin claim; "Workflow cannot support it" overstates (the tier call rests on §4). None may be used.

## §72 — ACP C2 at a large Taiwan-LISTED PCB plant (rigid multilayer, ~฿12B, ~8,000 staff, three loss years, parent recapitalising, revenue ramping on server/notebook boards) where the SDR's contact went silent after one mention of "MES"; the plant's own sustainability report shows an MES implemented in 2020; lead in a colleague's 私田 (NOVA live 2026-10-24 · job 32233de6 · APEX CIRCUIT (THAILAND) run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Stage:** contact lost + one mention of a need + amount 0 + no date = **D (需求探詢)**, whatever ACP says; an ACP contact who is not on the registry and carries a wrong title (e.g. "董事長" from a bought data list) counts as no contact. 立案三要項 restore C1: a quantified must-do (e.g. a customer audit or certification deadline on a new high-layer product), a named owner (the plant COO/EVP, MIS head or a project PM), and an evaluation/go-live date.
- **Re-entry is dual track:** DigiWin TW (鼎華 東南亞事業部 or the TW account owner for the listed parent) checks whether the parent is a 鼎新/鼎華 account and, if so, introduces top-down; in parallel the Thai field owner re-opens with the registry co-signatory who runs the plant (EVP/COO) and the MIS department. The researcher never contacts the customer.
- **Product routing:** for a PCB plant of this scale NOVA routes MES to 鼎華 iMES (PCB industry template) rather than the Thai eMES; treat this as a routing hint to confirm with TH management, not a measured product limit. First cut = traceability and pass-station error-proofing ("audit-ready") on ONE new high-layer/server line, not the whole plant.
- **Value order for a loss-making plant whose recovery depends on new product certifications:** customer certification and audit traceability first (keeps the orders that fund the turnaround), yield and scrap second.
- **If an MES already exists (self-reported 2020 implementation):** the need is an upgrade, replacement or traceability extension; ask what the 2020 system covers and who built it before positioning; over SAP/Oracle ERP the play is the shop-floor layer (§18, §22 interface rule).
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA proposed the first cut on the plant's JV line, but the JV was a separate, Korean-controlled company with no plant assets in its first filing, so its systems will likely follow the Korean partner; NOVA called Samsung and Honeywell "server majors" (customs records show TV/display and fire-system buyers); NOVA cited 欣興 and 健鼎 as 鼎華 references (not in the TW mirror or Monk cards; 欣興 appears only as a 2018 PCB A-Team alliance member); "perfect integration with SAP/Oracle" and "24/7 support" are not verified claims. None may be used.

## §73 — Pre-production Taiwanese rubber-seal greenfield (high-mix: thousands of compounds and part numbers), held 100% by an offshore company, ACP C2 with a quote already sent, ERP owner unnamed, customer saying "the ERP project starts after the production licence"; lead in a colleague's 私田 (NOVA live 2026-10-24 · job a4d14feb · IVY SEALS run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Stage:** a sent quote plus a customer-stated licence date is still **C2** while no ERP owner is in post, no dated production/shipment plan exists and no budget decision is known. C1/B needs three facts: (人) an ERP project owner or PM in post and in contact with us; (事) the first-sample and mass-production month for the plant's main customers, from which go-live is back-calculated; (預) whether the Thai plant has its own budget or the parent approves it. Same rule as §8, §17, §54.
- **Licence ≠ start-up:** for an estate plant, the factory licence may date from the build phase; the "production licence" a customer mentions is often the start-of-operation step. Check the DIW public registry status ("ยังไม่แจ้งประกอบ" = start not yet notified) before treating the customer's date as done; DIW's monthly start lists lag by months.
- **Parent route:** when directors and money look Taiwanese but production was in mainland China, NOVA's call is DigiWin TW first (decision and funds in Taiwan), with the Thai field owner working the plant in parallel.
- **Architecture:** NOVA prefers a stand-alone Thai Workflow ERP with item, recipe and customer codes aligned to the group over stretching a Taiwanese or mainland system across Thai tax and BOI. Claude vetting: if the parent is already on a DigiWin product, the KB precedent (§43, §54) is to extend the group template with Thai localisation; decide after the TW/China account check.
- **Phase-1 scope for a rubber-seal (compound and moulding) plant:** PUR/COP/INV with BOI/bonded handling if promoted; compound BOM with versions; kilo-to-piece dual units (raw compound by weight, seals by count); work orders with subcontracted deflashing (LRP level); GL/AP/AR with Thai VAT and WHT. **Phase 2:** mould management (life, maintenance) and full two-way lot traceability from mixing batch to finished seal. Workflow is the right size for a ~50-worker plant; do not open with T100 on a pre-revenue plant.
- **Value framing NOVA proposes:** origin and batch documentation for US customers plus BOI compliance, ahead of multi-item costing. Claude vetting: keep it as a question about what documents US customers ask for; never assert tariff penalties or customer loss.
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA treated the 10/16 licence as already granted (public records did not show it), framed the plant as a US-China trade-war relocation as fact, asserted that weak origin traceability means "巨額關稅甚至停牌" and BOI fines (BOI unconfirmed), gave a 3–4-month sign-to-go-live estimate (shorter than §17/§46), called Workflow a "零容錯系統", proposed a congratulatory site visit by Thai sales (only the field owner contacts the customer), and read all directors as Taiwanese (one spelling is Malaysian/Singaporean in style). None may be used as stated.


## §74 — Profitable Thai plant (~฿473M, 185 staff, three BOI certificates, two new product lines, gross margin halved) of a PRC private parent in IPO REGISTRATION on SZSE, parent on SAP (2012) and its own MES (2020); the parent vetoed a colleague's eMES quote, and the Thai GM (sole DBD signatory, ex-parent VP) came back asking for APS and proposing that the Thai plant "sign itself"; lead in a colleague's 私田 (NOVA live 2026-10-24 · job 42eef203 · YAOKUN MACHINERY run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Stage:** customer-initiated restart plus a sent APS proposal is still **C2** while the 立案三要項 are open (quantified must-do, a named plant PM, a dated go-live) and the approval path after an HQ veto is unknown.
- **Product for a discrete welded-steel routing on SAP (bending, welding, painting, assembly):** 鼎華 APS (Dinghua tier) with multi-constraint scheduling (painting as the bottleneck with colour/batch grouping; assembly kitting). APS needs from SAP: routings with accurate standard times and work centres, BOMs, calendars; sales orders/forecasts, production orders, open purchase orders; plus floor progress data.
- **Phase 2 when HQ already runs an MES:** SFT as the APS progress-capture layer rather than eMES, so the subsidiary does not appear to build a second MES; eMES only with HQ's agreement.
- **Reading an HQ veto during a listing:** most likely HQ's own MES/architecture standard plus IPO-period control of subsidiary spend; confirm with the field owner whether it was a no to eMES, to spending now, or to DigiWin.
- **Authority:** a sole registry signatory at the subsidiary has legal authority, but a listing parent's subsidiary-management and investment rules decide what he may approve alone; learn the limit and the path, and route any HQ contact through the champion and the field owner. An internal DigiWin China CRM check on the parent (account status, MES vendor, coverage) is safe and comes first.
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA advised keeping HQ in the dark, splitting the contract to stay under the GM's limit, booking it as consulting expense "to avoid the parent's asset audit during the IPO", and telling HQ that SFT "is not another MES". Not adopted: designing a deal around a listing company's internal controls is a compliance and reputational risk and exposes the champion (same rule as §69). NOVA also dated the veto to 2024 (the SDR's notes put it in early 2026), promised to "rescue" the margin and used an invented "3 points of margin" figure; none may be used.


## §75 — Pre-completion Thai DIE-CUT plant of a PRC-listed group (Taiwanese controllers, TWSE-listed grand-parent) whose mainland and Vietnam plants reportedly run DigiWin ERP, Vietnam having SIGNED a Vietnam finance/tax localisation pack a year ago that has not started; lead from a bank-invited event, no TH field owner, contacts = a Thai HR manager + an HQ mailbox (NOVA live 2026-10-24 · job 6cb3cd83 · LONGYOUNG ELECTRONICS (THAILAND) run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Stage:** event attendance + an in-family parent is **C2 (找權力人士提案)**, not C1. Three facts lift it to C1: a quantified must-do (a Thai tax or BOI reporting date that forces compliant Thai statements by a named month); the group formally names an ERP project owner for the Thai plant (HQ IT or Taiwan HQ) who starts working requirements with us; a dated Thai go-live month (e.g. before the own building is handed over).
- **Lead and split:** the DigiWin China AM in the parent's HQ city calls high at HQ (budget and decision sit there); DigiWin TH supports locally: site visits, Thai localisation quote and consulting, timed to HQ's plan. The TH channel head coordinates Thai consulting resources and Thai-localisation technical support; red lines: no Thai-side quote or customisation promise on his own (it undercuts the group negotiation), and no going around HQ IT to the Thai plant staff.
- **A stalled sister localisation pack:** NOVA reads it as HQ IT short of people or wary of overseas roll-outs; do NOT wait for the sister to go live; argue for parallel progress on the Thai plant's own compliance clock. If the stall is raised with the customer, frame it as "lessons from your overseas roll-outs", never as a delay. Claude vetting: ask the sister-country DigiWin team internally first, so the customer never hears our own stalled project from us.
- **Die-cut phase 1:** Thai finance and tax localisation; purchasing, sales, stock; basic work orders and subcontracting; multi-unit conversion (area, length, weight, pieces: roll stock goes wrong without it); lot control. **Phase 2 after the plant is complete:** actual cost with offcut/remnant accounting; formula/R&D for a new material line (e.g. composite copper foil); scheduling and MES. Claude vetting: APS only on a T100/Dinghua-tier instance (iGP = LRP + exception reports); floor actuals = eMES phase 2 in the TH register.
- **First questions:** does the Thai plant follow the Vietnam model (same instance + localisation pack) or get its own system; who decides (HQ IT, the Thai build team, or the chairman); how Thai invoices and tax are handled now while the plant ships small batches from rented space; the latest go-live before the own building is handed over.
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA stated the parent as "已是鼎捷老客戶" and named T100 as the mainland system (the only evidence is one SDR note; product unknown); it read the HQ-mailbox contact "劉總" as HQ management/IT and the Thai director as "傅董" (both unverified); it scripted "過去我們在越南廠有過合作默契" (assumes a relationship the TH side cannot vouch for); it treated BOI promotion as the likely must-do (unverified); and it called lot traceability a strict requirement of electronics/automotive buyers for die-cut parts (an industry prior, not evidence about the plant). None may be said as fact. No control-workaround advice was offered.

## §76 — Just-opened Thai plant (~฿900M assets, first production year, group-loan funded) of a PRC-LISTED pump maker that moved capacity out of China for US customers; the SDR's only note says "T老客" and that BOI planning sits with the parent's DOMESTIC LOGISTICS manager; ACP contact = the parent's CFO; lead in a colleague's 私田 (NOVA live 2026-10-24 · job 73d6bb91 · HUATAI INTELLIGENT TECHNOLOGY (THAILAND) run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Reading "T老客":** in DigiWin usage, an existing T100 or TIPTOP (易拓) customer, most likely the parent. Verify before any use: CRM search on the parent's full Chinese name, then the DigiWin China AM in the parent's city (maintenance contract, modules, consultant and AM names). Claude vetting: an SDR shorthand is a lead, not evidence; an ACP contact Category of 新客 can contradict it (§45), so ask which company and which product it meant.
- **Architecture when the parent is on T100 and supplies the parts:** extend the parent's T100 to a Thai site (multi-organisation, multi-site) for intercompany trade, shared BOM and routings, and consolidation; the parent buys seats and multi-organisation modules through DigiWin China, the Thai entity buys the Thai localisation pack (VAT, WHT, Thai/English) and local consulting from DigiWin TH (same logic as §43, §61).
- **"BOI is planned by HQ logistics":** NOVA reads it as machinery and raw-material duty exemptions (and their BOM-based reconciliation) being controlled from China. The HQ logistics manager is then the domain owner and likely coach; the Thai site is the user. Claude vetting: confirm the BOI certificate exists before using it as the must-do, and never pitch it as a customs-penalty threat.
- **Phase 1 for a newly opened import-dependent assembly plant:** import purchasing and in-transit, stock with BOI and non-BOI material kept apart, basic work orders and material issue on a BOM that matches the BOI filing, Thai AP/AR/GL with VAT and WHT reports, a BOI reconciliation output. No MES or AI-vision work in phase 1.
- **Stage:** one online exchange, a contact who is the parent's CFO (not a Thai signatory), no Thai counterpart, no timetable and a "test" report = **D (需求探詢)**, not C2. C1 needs: a must-do the customer states (Thai tax or BOI reconciliation the current set-up cannot handle), a named Thai project owner working requirements, a dated go-live.
- **Division of labour:** the parent-city AM calls high at HQ (CFO, logistics manager; confirm the installed base and the overseas-plant plan); the Thai SDR approaches the Thai deputy GM or finance lead only after the AM has checked the parent relationship (a premature Thai push risks annoying HQ and the installed-base relationship); the TH channel head packages multi-site T100 + Thai localisation + a verified BOI reference for both, with no customer contact of his own.
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA read the parent's "自筹资金" as the chairman's own money and made him the budget owner by that route (自筹 = the company's own funds; his deciding role is an inference); it gave the CFO a veto without evidence; it scripted the customer confirming a "罰款或停工風險"; it offered a Thai cloud-hosting service and a "BOI 成功案例" not verified in our catalogue or corpus; and it echoed Chinese characters for the Thai deputy GM that our own question guessed. None may be used as fact. No control-workaround advice was offered.

## §77 — ACP C2 whose only daily report is an event invitation ("活動邀約", HR-mailbox contact from a data list), at a ramping, loss-making Thai plant (second sales year ~฿217M, gross margin ~14, net loss, group-funded) of a Taipei-run luxury metal-accessories group (MIM, sintering, CNC, plating/PVD) whose Taiwan parent has a 20 LVMH minority; the plant's own job ads show an ERP and an MES already installed by an outside software house and an MES go-live being pushed; lead in a colleague's 私田 (NOVA live 2026-10-24 · job 3bb6b295 · GBJM TECHNOLOGY (THAILAND) run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Stage:** an invitation with no needs conversation, no project owner and no date = **D (需求探詢)**, whatever ACP shows ("行為序判定": grade by what the customer has done, not by the record). C2 needs a conversation with a plant or parent decision-maker that confirms pain with the current ERP/MES rollout and a willingness to take outside help; C1 needs the 立案三要項 (a quantified must-do, a named project owner such as the IE or systems engineer being hired, a dated go-live).
- **Entry when ERP and MES were just bought:** do not attack the ERP. Offer rollout support where MIM and plating MES projects usually hurt (process logic, reporting discipline), and complements where the installed ERP cannot cost a work order or trace a batch (costing, WMS barcode, sintering-run-to-plating-batch trace). First move is internal: CRM check of the Taiwan parent at DigiWin TW and of the mainland sister at DigiWin China (customer or not, product, AM, overseas-plant plan); if either is a DigiWin account, the Thai plant is in-family and the play is extension.
- **Route:** two tracks, the parent through DigiWin TW (only where a relationship exists) and the Thai plant through the field owner (the IE or MES owner, not HR). Decision for a group-funded, loss-making plant sits mainly with the parent.
- **Industry question themes for MIM plus plating/PVD accessory plants (questions, not claims):** consumable cost per work order (theoretical vs actual) instead of year-end stock-take back-calculation; manual polishing efficiency and how floor reporting is error-proofed; rack life and plating-bath change timing; how fast one brand batch can be traced back to the powder lot, sintering run and plating batch.
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA made "the current system cannot meet LVMH's end-2026 audit" the must-do (no such deadline is evidenced), called LVMH the largest customer (not evidenced), scripted an opening to the parent's chairman that quotes the plant's overhead ratio and sets a 2026 break-even target (filings stay questions, §43/§55; the target is invented), put the parent's decision share at "90" percent (invented number), offered an unverified "plating MES white paper" and a superlative about DigiWin's plating expertise, and treated gold salts, PVD targets and piece-rate pay as facts. None may be used as fact. No control-workaround advice was offered.

## §78 — ACP C2 whose only daily report is "咨询WMS 和MES 正在沟通" from a non-signatory 經理 on personal (163.com) mail, at a year-two PRC-owned CASTING-AND-MACHINING export plant (first full year ~฿140M, gross margin ~32, profitable, group-funded through near-interest-free current liabilities, receivable days ~260) shipping rough castings and machined parts to US OEMs (compressor, generator, robot makers), parent and ERP unknown, ACP record mis-merged with a same-name chemicals company; lead in a colleague's 私田 (NOVA live 2026-10-24 · job 111fb3a0 · WANSHENG TECHNOLOGY run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Stage:** a single "asked about WMS and MES, in discussion" note, 25 days quiet, amount 0, contact not a signatory = **D (需求探詢)**, not C2 (behaviour-sequence rule; same reading as §76). C1 needs the usual three: a quantified must-do the plant states (customer traceability or label audits it cannot meet today), a named project owner (plant head, IT or a director), and a go-live date.
- **First cut when the customer asks for WMS and MES at once:** WMS with barcodes (receiving, FIFO, look-alike part control, customer shipping labels) before MES, because a casting-and-machining MES (melt and charge, pattern and mould life, machine links, many process hops) is long and custom for a plant this size. **Gate before either: confirm the ERP base.** No ERP → ERP + WMS as the first wave; an ERP exists → its brand and openness, then cost the WMS interface (§22 claim rules apply).
- **Tier for ~฿140M:** Workflow (iGP); floor = SFT (native to Workflow) or sMES where barcode boards matter; not eMES in phase 1.
- **Casting-plus-machining discovery themes (questions, not claims):** heat or pour number to finished part traceability and how long an 8D answer takes; reject reasons by process (porosity, shrinkage, machining scrap) and where they are found; keeping look-alike castings apart and labelled per customer; work-order progress reporting to US buyers; whether castings are poured in Thailand or brought in rough.
- **Unknown PRC parent:** DigiWin China searches the registry and its CRM on the directors' names and the Thai address (never on guessed Chinese characters); ask the contact whether the system must connect to HQ's ERP; any HQ contact goes through the field owner with the Thai side's agreement.
- **Mis-merged ACP record (same-sound name, different business):** the field owner splits the contact into its own account and notes it on the record; the research run reports it and writes nothing.
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA framed the must-do as "fines or lost orders if unresolved" (fear-selling; the must-do has to come from the plant), offered an invented example deadline ("2027 annual customer audit"), scripted an opener that names the plant's US buyers from customs data (not in a first conversation), said "US and EU" where only US shipments were evidenced, claimed "many Workflow successes in Southeast Asia" and warned of "cheap Taiwanese and Thai rivals" (unverified, internal only), echoed our romanised name as the company's Chinese name, and wrote the parent's decision power as "absolute". None may be used as fact. No control-workaround advice was offered.

## §79 — ACP C2 at the RAMPING, loss-making Thai plant (first sales year ~฿12.5M, gross loss, stock ~3× sales, group-funded, capital just tripled) of a Taiwan-founded, Kunshan-run stamping and insert-moulding group in Beijing Stock Exchange IPO TUTORING, group on DigiWin TIPTOP (T100 unconfirmed); the SDR's only note: "T老客，内部有在评估单独泰部署/优化，IT负责人是原鼎捷出去的"; contacts on the group domain, none a signatory; lead in a colleague's 私田 (NOVA live 2026-10-24 · job 6bdb37d8 · HUANG YU PRECISION TECHNOLOGY (THAILAND) run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Stage:** group-domain contacts + a customer-stated internal evaluation + an ex-DigiWin IT lead = **C2 (找權力人士提案 / 推動內部共識)**, not D (contrast §76/§78, where one exchange or one vague ask with no evaluation stated read D). C1 = the usual three, with the listing as the clock: a must-do the customer states and sizes (what the sponsor/auditor will require of the Thai subsidiary's stock count, valuation and intercompany records, and when), a Thai project team with a named leader that includes the Thai finance lead and plant head (never the IT lead alone), a go-live backed out from the listing timetable.
- **"单独部署 vs 优化" under a listing:** prefer the group's existing instance with the Thai entity as its own organisation/site plus the Thai localisation pack (VAT, WHT, Thai documents; e-Tax where wanted). A separate Thai system means two item/BOM masters, manual intercompany reconciliation and double maintenance, which a listing review questions. Consistent with §43/§61. Claude vetting: the §68 exception stands: if the group is on an OLD TIPTOP GP that cannot carry Thai requirements, a Thai T100 tied back to the group stays open; the version decides, so ask it first.
- **Phase 1 for a ramping stamping + insert-moulding plant:** purchasing, sales, stock with lot control; Thai GL/AP/AR + Thai tax; simple work orders so finished cartons trace to strip (stamping) and resin (moulding) lots. **Phase 2:** MRP once volume justifies it (spreadsheet planning meanwhile), die/mould life and QMS, barcode/WMS and floor boards. TH register: floor actuals = eMES phase 2 (NOVA said SFC); APS only on a T100/Dinghua-tier instance.
- **Ex-DigiWin IT lead:** use him to bring the Thai finance lead and the director in early and to agree the architecture on standard logic; risks: he holds no budget (deal dies in technical detail and free-customisation haggling if he is the only contact) and he knows DigiWin's pricing and consulting costs (expect it at negotiation).
- **Division of labour:** the DigiWin China AM for the parent calls high at group HQ (version, group IT capacity, listing timetable, the sponsor's plan for the Thai subsidiary); the Thai field owner and consultants work the plant's finance lead and plant head and show the Thai localisation; the researcher briefs, never contacts the customer.
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA scripted the must-do as a warning to the director that the sponsor would block the listing over Thai stock and controls (fear-selling); proposed using the IT lead to write requirements that "排他友商" (shaping the customer's selection to exclude other vendors works around its procurement process: not adopted); suggested asking HQ to "發文" ordering the Thai plant onto the group ERP (the customer's decision, not ours to have imposed); proposed signing in China to "規避" Thai cross-border tax issues (contract entity and withholding are for the field owner and management, never framed as avoidance; §17, §43); cited "SOX" for a Beijing listing; promised "一鍵" consolidation and "自動對帳" (§22: never automatic); called a separate system a "財務舞弊" risk; labelled the US buyer automotive and cited an unverified "車載稽核案例"; asserted a parent/Thai process split without evidence; named DBD as the Thai tax office; treated e-Tax as mandatory (voluntary as of 2026). None may be used. No other control-workaround advice was offered.

## §80 — Ramping Thai plant (first sales year ~฿49M, ~฿400M group-funded plant, interest-free group liabilities, US brand-owner buyers) of a PRC private group that is a DigiWin E10 customer in mainland China, where the SDR's only note says "no Thai tax pack or Thai language pack yet, internal budget limited, undecided"; contact = the GM on the parent's domain (probably a director); lead in a colleague's 私田 (NOVA live 2026-10-24 · job a6192846 · XINGDA (THAILAND) run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Stage:** a customer-stated gap with no budget, no date and no internal consensus = **C2 (找權力人士提案)**, whatever the gap's clarity; same reading as §56/§61. C1 needs a power holder's support, a named Thai project owner and a go-live the customer accepts.
- **E10 Thai pack (NOVA's claim, repeated from §56, still unverified):** "標準的 E10 泰國財稅包與語言包在官方產品地圖中並不存在"; packs SDRs mention for E10 are, in NOVA's reading, mainland-team customisations for specific accounts; DigiWin TH delivers and maintains Workflow, T100 and Digiwin ERP, not E10. Claude vetting: the SDR's wording ("还没有泰国财税包和语言包") implies the packs can be bought for E10; the two statements conflict and neither is confirmed. **Rule: before any Thai quote on an E10 group, the field owner gets the E10 Thai-pack position in writing from TH delivery and the group's DigiWin China AM.**
- **Architecture (NOVA's view for E10 groups):** a separate Thai Workflow ERP with the Thai tax pack, consolidated to the group's E10 by an interface, rather than the Thai company on the mainland E10; reason: Thai compliance and local maintenance. This differs from §43/§61/§79 (same T100/TIPTOP instance plus a Thai pack); the difference rests entirely on the unverified E10 localisation claim, so the version and localisation facts decide, then /select-t100-vs-workflow.
- **"Budget limited" first phase (NOVA):** purchasing, sales, stock + Thai AP/AR/GL and the Thai tax pack; single-level BOM issue; minimum consulting days; MRP/APS later. A legitimate scope cut NOVA offered: the system issues the Thai tax invoices and runs stock while an outside accounting firm keeps the full books until phase 2. Claude vetting: acceptable only as a scope choice with complete books kept by the firm, never as a way to keep records away from audit.
- **Route:** the Thai field owner finds the group's DigiWin China AM first (budget attitude, group IT plan, credit split), then meets the Thai GM on how the plant issues tax invoices and traces lots; the channel head supplies a verified Thai reference and an internal architecture sketch, no customer contact.
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA scripted an opening question on US-China tariffs, customs audits and proving "泰國製造的實質轉型比例" with a Form D reference (not adopted: origin compliance is not an opener, the buyer was named from customs data, and Form D is an ASEAN-China certificate, not a US one); asserted "損失是每年數千萬泰銖" and "直接掉單" (unsourced fear-selling); called Workflow "100% 合規"; required BOI accounting with BOI status unknown; turned the shared founder surname we supplied into "the family's representative who holds the purse" and invented a "集團 IT 總監"; treated the GM as a director (unconfirmed); used "避稅風險" as a theme (tax is never framed as avoidance). None may be used as fact.

## §81 — Pre-production Thai GREENFIELD of a PRC-LISTED connector group whose China plants run SAP + MES + WMS, where the Thai side asked the SDR for "一套轻量合规化的ERP系统" and the group finance director (CPA) is due in Thailand to discuss needs; contact = a parent sales mailbox, both Thai directors are the founder and his son; lead in a colleague's 私田 (NOVA live 2026-10-24 · job ca4735b2 · WCON ELECTRONICS (THAILAND) run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Stage:** one relayed request plus an unconfirmed visit = **C2 (找權力人士提案)**, at risk of sliding back if the visit produced nothing. Three facts restore C1: (a) a first-invoice / first-shipment date from which the ERP go-live is back-calculated; (b) the finance director or a director names a Thai project owner and a working group with DigiWin; (c) a budget range and the approval path.
- **Product for an SAP parent asking for a light Thai system:** Workflow ERP (iGP) deployed for Thailand alone, finance data handed back to SAP (two-tier), not T100, while the plant has no revenue. Phase 1 = GL/AP/AR + Thai tax (VAT, WHT, e-Tax) + **fixed assets** (the plant already carries large PP&E) + purchasing/sales/stock + basic BOM and work orders; no MES, AGV or advanced scheduling in phase 1. Same shape as §17 and §54; here the customer's own word "轻量" puts it in §17's winnable branch.
- **Main threat:** group IT copies SAP (a light SAP product or a cloud two-tier edition) to Thailand for convenience; answer with Thai localisation, speed and Chinese-Thai-English service on both sides, not with claims about SAP's cost.
- **SAP interface promise (restates §22):** standard API and structured exports (daily vouchers, stock summaries, Thai accounts mapped to the group chart) for HQ consolidation; never "seamless real-time two-way sync" without the parent's SAP specification and the parent's SAP side doing its part.
- **Finance-director meeting (CPA at a listed parent):** ask about Thai VAT/WHT/e-Tax controls she plans, how the Thai numbers should reach SAP (chart mapping vs document level) and how many days after month-end, how the plant's assets are recorded and depreciated against group policy (and BOI, if promoted); show Thai tax returns and e-Tax produced from the system plus the account-mapping output, not generic features.
- **Roles:** DigiWin China AM in the parent's city calls high at HQ (finance director, the operations-director son) and owns HQ terms; DigiWin TH shows the Thai localisation and supports the site; the SDR keeps both in sync. Red lines: no Thai-side commercial or architecture promise before the AM is aligned; no price talk with a relay contact.
- **Connector new-plant pain priors (discovery prompts only):** outside plating issued and received in quantities that do not reconcile; ECN/BOM changes from HQ reaching the Thai floor late, leaving obsolete terminals; IATF lot trace from finished connector back to copper strip lot and plating batch; Thai-language UI for operators.
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA gave unsourced comparisons (Workflow TCO "30-40" of SAP, "3 個月" go-live, SAP "6-9 個月", WHT format changes costing "數萬人民幣"), an invented budget example (100萬-150萬泰銖) and an invented plating loss example, called SAP's Thai localisation "出了名的昂貴", assumed BOI, misnamed the chairman and gave the finance director signing power she does not hold on the registry. None may be used. No control-workaround advice was offered.

## §82 — Cold ACP C2 at a JAPANESE-owned batch plating-chemicals plant (~฿481M, +31%, net margin 1–3 points, 100% Japanese capital, four Japanese directors, one on the Osaka parent's board) whose only daily report says "the Japanese boss said they had a need, then could not be reached by phone"; the need is not recorded, the contact is a staff-level 專員, no system evidence at all; lead in a colleague's 私田 (NOVA live 2026-10-24 · job 51cf3810 · OKUNO (THAILAND) run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Stage:** an unrecorded need + an unreachable staff contact + no decision-maker reached = **D/E, not C2**, whatever ACP says. Restoring it needs direct contact with someone of decision weight (the Japanese MD, a director or the plant manager) AND a confirmed pain with willingness to act.
- **Wake-up path (two weeks, then E and marketing nurture):** (1) internal first: the field owner recovers who said "need", when and about what; (2) one value-first touch: a formal Japanese-language note to the Japanese MD (copying the existing contact), framed on the plant's growth and cost control and on carrying the parent's production-management DX into Thailand, never as a product pitch; (3) no real conversation in 14 days → E.
- **Japanese-parent positioning (restates §21):** never fight a parent Japanese ERP head-on; DigiWin = the local operations and Thai-compliance engine (formula production, Thai tax, BOI if any) that rolls finance and stock up to the parent's system. Japanese buyers weigh trust, certainty and formal courtesy; a Japanese-speaking colleague helps with the call-high.
- **First cut for a batch-chemicals maker on thin gross margin (~11–12):** ERP first (formula/BOM versions, lot and expiry, actual batch cost, Thai tax), because cost truth comes before shop-floor data; eMES batch records and WMS later. NOVA leaned iGP for ~฿480M; KB §4 puts that revenue in T100's caution band, so the tier stays with /select-t100-vs-workflow.
- **Roles (inferred):** evaluators = plant manager and finance/accounting head; signer = the resident Japanese MD or a parent-seconded director, with Osaka approval for a system that touches group standards.
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA restated a 2021 capacity-doubling plan as current, assumed an existing ERP and BOI, asserted that manual recipes and paper lot records "必定" cause late deliveries and yield loss, said accumulated losses give a tax "refund" (they carry forward), proposed opening on a competitor's new plant in the same estate, offered a DigiWin "Japan Desk" not verified, and named 南寶樹脂 and 長興材料 as chemical references although neither appears in the mirrored case library or the Monk cards; and it credited 三晃 (case 999) with lot-tracing and CoA gains that the page does not state. None adopted. A verified plating-chemicals reference exists: case 780 (鈞澤科技, page 2018).

## §83 — Ramping Thai BOI greenfield (first sales year ~฿4.7M, ฿100M paid in, no debt) of a TAIWAN-founded, DONGGUAN-run compressor group whose Thai plant runs on the group's mainland ERP (brand unknown) and whose HQ "wants one system, will not change", while the Thai plant manager says the new BOI certificate is a headache; contacts are managers, not directors; 75 days without a report; lead in a colleague's 私田 (NOVA live 2026-10-24 · job 83a64567 · ALL FIRST MACHINERY & ELECTRIC (THAILAND) run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Stage:** a stated local pain (BOI) with no project team, no budget, no date and a HQ that controls the system = **C2 (找權力人士提案)**; re-engage within about 15 days or drop to D (需求探詢) / Pending. Event attendance is the re-engagement hook.
- **"Will not change system" is not a dead end, it is a branch:** first check, internally, whether the group's mainland system is a DigiWin China product (CRM search on the PRC operating company's name through the HQ-city DigiWin China office: brand, version, AM). In-family (易飛 / E10 / T100 / TIPTOP) → Thai localisation + BOI module on the same instance, eMT and RMTS reports from the existing stock data (same logic as §43, §61, §79; E10 Thai packs still unconfirmed per §56, §80). Non-DigiWin → NOVA proposes a non-intrusive BOI reconciliation layer fed by exports from the group ERP (BOM, import entries, export entries, material issues; API or Excel) that compares them with the BOI filing and outputs RMTS formats. Claude vetting: NOVA called it "BOI Helper / 鼎捷 BOI 數據對帳平台"; no such product is documented in the KB, corpus or repo; confirm with DigiWin TH delivery before anyone mentions it.
- **Roles and split:** HQ (owner, IT, finance) decides; the Thai plant manager is the user and potential champion; import-export staff are daily users. The HQ-city DigiWin China AM calls high; DigiWin TH helps the plant manager on BOI through the field owner and asks him to bring HQ IT or finance in.
- **Three first-visit questions (reworded):** when do the first duty-free machines or materials arrive; can the group ERP's stock and BOM be matched to the BOI-approved BOM and the import/export entries, and who does it now; would the plant manager bring HQ IT or finance into a look at a BOI option that leaves the group ERP untouched.
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA framed the offer as helping the plant "避稅" and treated our unverified guess that the plant exists to avoid US tariffs as fact (both not adopted: tax and customs obligations are never framed as avoidance, and the tariff motive is never raised); it translated the pain into "百萬泰銖" penalties and warned that the BOI certificate could be revoked (fear-selling, not adopted); it gave BOI deadlines of "24-30 個月", "1-2 年", "投產後 2-3 年" and a "20%~30%" back-duty figure (unsourced, not adopted; only §15's earlier "~3 years from the certificate" stays as a prior); it called the directors Taiwanese (DBD records Taiwanese capital, not the directors' nationality); and it named 泰金寶 Cal-Comp, First Rubber, Thai Summit and 浙江開山 as references, none of which is a verified case in the mirror or the Monk cards (Cal-Comp appears only on a logo wall). A verified compressor reference exists: case 794 (東正鐵工廠, SWAN, page 2018).

## §84 — Ramping Thai BOI plant (~฿139M sales, 2.4 gross margin, ฿600M paid in, ฿850M PP&E, stock ×6.8 in a year) of a PRC-LISTED small-engine and farm-machinery group, running the parent's mainland KINGDEE Cloud Galaxy (金蝶云星空) with "no Thai language pack, no Thai tax", own books, three BOI certificates "not yet inspected"; the contact offered to book the GM, "GM busy", 75 days silent; lead in a colleague's 私田 (NOVA live 2026-10-24 · job b50758e4 · NOVUS POWER EQUIPMENT run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Stage:** a relayed, customer-stated gap (Thai tax, Thai language, BOI) with no project team, no budget, no date and a GM who keeps postponing = **C2 (cold)**; re-engage within about 14 days, then D (需求探詢). C1 needs a quantified must-do (an inspection date or a Thai filing the current set-up cannot produce), a named Thai owner (finance manager or IT) authorised by the GM, and a go-live month. Same reading as §60, §80, §83.
- **Kingdee incumbent at the Thai plant of a PRC-listed parent:** coexistence first, not displacement: Thai localisation (VAT, WHT P.N.D. 3/53, e-Tax, Thai-language documents), BOI machinery and raw-material reconciliation, barcode WMS; displacement of Kingdee at the Thai entity only if HQ lets Thailand run its own system. NOVA called Thai tax and WMS standard and BOI reconciliation plus any Kingdee GL link "to confirm". Claude vetting: selling Thai tax as a layer beside Kingdee (rather than a second Workflow ERP finance book) is not documented here; confirm the shape with DigiWin TH delivery before offering it. Kingdee is active in Thailand (§60), so its own Thai pack is the likeliest rival.
- **Route:** DigiWin China's office in the parent's city checks its CRM first (account, past evaluation, HQ systems, IT and finance owners); the Thai side, through the field owner, works the finance manager and BOI officer with a Thai-tax and BOI walkthrough; findings go back to the China AM, who calls high at HQ. Roles: chairman = ultimate decision maker for large Thai budgets; overseas-division head = influencer; Thai GM = sponsor; Thai sole director = signatory.
- **First-visit questions (reworded as open questions):** which inspection is still open on each BOI certificate and by when, and who prepares the machinery and raw-material reports from which records; how Thai VAT, withholding and statements are produced today and how many days a Thai month-end takes; how Thai stock is counted and matched to what HQ sees in Kingdee (never quote the customer's filings at them).
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA framed the opening as a compliance diagnosis around "cancelled tax exemption or heavy fines" and wanted HQ told about "稅務處罰與免稅期延後的風險" (fear-selling, not adopted); asked about past Revenue Department penalties (dropped); claimed the 6-year CIT holiday cannot take effect before the full-operation inspection (contradicted by the parent's report showing the BOI rate applied in the year before inspection; not used); gave "30 months" for machinery imports and "36 months" for the full-operation inspection (not adopted as fact; §15's "~3 years from the certificate" stays the prior); called Kingdee's Thai service "極為薄弱" (unverified competitor claim, internal only); named the parent's chairman wrongly ("夏重陽"; the annual report names another person); invented an "IT 總監"; and named 宗申動力, 隆鑫通用 and 神馳機電 as DigiWin ERP/MES/PLM references, none of which is in the TW mirror or the Monk cards, and one public search found no DigiWin case for any of them. Verified product-adjacent references: TW cases 1689 (大井泵浦, water pumps, page 2018) and 568 (芳晟機電, submersible pumps, page 2018).

## §85 — Thai CORRUGATED-BOX greenfield (฿852M BOI plant, opening Oct 2026, pre-revenue in its filings) of a PRC-LISTED, family-controlled carton group whose HQ builds its own digital platforms (industrial-internet platform, AI design SaaS) and whose ERP brand is not public; the SDR's notes say "Thai ERP project pending confirmation" and "opens 15 Oct, starts production"; ACP contacts = the Thai GM (not a director) and the group's IT head; lead in a colleague's 私田 (NOVA live 2026-10-24 · job 64626aaa · GREAT SHENGDA TECHNOLOGY DEVELOPMENT (THAILAND) run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Stage:** a pending ERP project with amount 0, no budget, no RFP and no go-live date = **C2 (找權力人士提案)**, even with the plant opening. Three facts lift it to C1/B: a budget and a named Thai ERP project owner (the Thai GM or the group IT head); an RFP date or a dated go-live month; HQ IT confirming the Thai plant's IT route (own system with a hand-back to HQ, or the group's). The SDR's first move after an opening is a congratulation call that asks how commissioning and first orders are going and when the system must follow.
- **Route for a self-building HQ with an unknown ERP:** NOVA prefers a stand-alone Thai Workflow ERP (Thai buy/sell/stock, tax, BOI, Thai-language use) with finance passed back to HQ, reasoning that an HQ that builds its own platforms will not take an outside ERP's production module and that a group template will not carry Thai tax and BOI.
