Claude vetting: hold this as the working hypothesis until the DigiWin China office in the parent's city has checked its CRM (account, past evaluation, HQ systems) and the field owner has learned what HQ IT plans; same logic as §17 and §54, and §71 warns that strong in-house IT may want co-creation, so ask first.
- **Corrugated-box phase split (NOVA's, not evidence about any one plant):** phase 1 = quotation and costing by board grade, flute and size; multi-unit conversion (m², weight, pieces); buy/sell/stock and purchasing; Thai tax and BOI; basic plate and cutting-die records. Phase 2 = corrugator order combination (scheduling: T100/Dinghua tier only; iGP answer = LRP + exception reports), small-batch production control, plate/die life with barcodes, MES and floor boards (eMES phase 2 in the TH register). Add fixed assets (and eMT) to phase 1 when the plant carries large new PP&E (§39, §54).
- **BOI for a box plant:** eMT (imported machinery) in phase 1. RMTS only matters if imported paper or materials are claimed duty-free; NOVA wanted RMTS in phase 1 regardless, but a plant that plans local paper buying may need little RMTS; confirm the certificate's raw-material items. A CIT holiday counted from the start of operations needs promoted and non-promoted income separated from the first invoice; do not date the holiday from an opening ceremony.
- **Roles (family-controlled PRC-listed parent):** the parent president who is also the Thai legal representative and signatory = 核決者; a parent VP director and the Thai GM = 決策者; the group IT head = 守門員 with a likely technical veto on group IT rules and data hand-back; a minority investor's director = influencer.
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA told the team to "極力顯化不合規的稅務處罰風險" to win over the IT head and said a late ERP means the books "無法通過泰國會計師審計" (fear-selling, not adopted); it dated the 3-year holiday from the opening day (not published); it called 吉宏股份 "目前仍高度穩定使用中" and named 达成包装 "或 正隆紙業（泰國/越南廠）" running a "紙器行業專用模組" in South-East Asia (currency unverified, 正隆 in neither the TW mirror nor the Monk cards, no such module documented); it placed the group IT head in the Thai plant (filings place him at HQ); it scripted a GM question quoting the parent's sales target back at him (reworded). Verified paper-packaging references: TW cases 1027 (久德, paper boxes, Vietnam, Workflow ERP, page 2018) and 1541 (統奕包裝, paper cups, Workflow ERP, page 2018); DigiWin China's 2025 packaging deck lists 吉宏股份 (T100 + MES, signed 2017), 达成包装 (corrugated, signed 2016), 艺虹, 众品鑫 (history only).

## §86 — Ramping Thai JIT parts plant (~฿876M first full year, net margin 0.3, ฿1.3B PP&E, BOI) of a BSE-LISTED PRC appliance-parts maker that supplies Midea's Thai plant, with a sister Thai company at the same registered address and a shared director (the parent chairman); customer says "no local Thai ERP" and plans shop-floor dashboards after a phase-2 plant; parent-sent finance head attended an online event but declined the SDR's contact request; 74 days silent; lead in a colleague's 私田 (NOVA live 2026-10-24 · job 7f61b384 · HONGHAI TECHNOLOGY (THAILAND) run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Stage:** a future dashboard plan with no budget or date, contacts not engaged and no signatory met = **D (需求探詢)** per NOVA, whatever ACP says (C2 here). Three facts restore C1: a quantified must-do with a date, a named project owner (finance head or plant head) with a team, a phase-2 timetable with a parent-approved budget range. The skill's OSINT field may still read C2-candidate on the evidence; record both (same reading as §64, §76).
- **First cut = ERP, dashboards as the visible output.** "看板是結果，進銷存與工單是骨架": Thai Workflow ERP (finance, Thai tax, BOI accounts, buy/sell/stock, work orders) first, the shop-floor screens as the deliverable the plant can show; never sell dashboards on a plant with no system of record (consistent with §1, §64).
- **Plays by parent system:** parent on DigiWin = one group design and shared reporting (same-instance logic, §43); parent on Kingdee/Yonyou = Thai localisation and local service with data passed back; parent on SAP = two-tier, Thai Workflow ERP feeding SAP (§22, §81). Put BOTH Thai companies in one blueprint when they share an address and a chairman (§40).
- **Route:** DigiWin China in the parent's city calls high on the chairman/GM or group finance director, framed on listed-company reporting and the Thai monthly report; asks HQ to introduce the Thai finance head; the Thai field owner follows. Never complain to the customer about an unaccepted contact request; no price talk at D; do not tell the plant its workers are slow (say how other Chinese-run plants made output visible).
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA told the channel head to message the finance head himself on LinkedIn/WeChat/LINE (not adopted: colleague's account, channel head stays behind the field owner, §52, §76); its script claimed DigiWin "recently helped several PRC BOI plants in Chonburi" with JIT auto-reconciliation and BOI write-off (unverified), set an invented "對帳效率提升 80" target, promised Midea-portal auto-reconciliation (no integration verified), used BOI fear framing, said Kingdee/Yonyou have no strong Thai team (contradicts §60), called SAP "extremely costly" in Thailand and gave DigiWin "20+ years" in Thailand (unsourced), named 正航/天心 as rivals on no evidence and called the finance head newly appointed (unknown). None may be used. No control-workaround advice was offered.

## §87 — ACP C2 whose only daily reports are courtesy touches ("客情维系", "节日问候"), at a year-two Thai plant (~฿125M sales, −17, gross margin ~32, operating break-even, stock ×18 to ~276 days of COGS, PP&E doubled, no debt) of a PRC PRIVATE IATF auto-parts maker (hose clamps, Tianjin; Chinese OEM customers) whose ERP is unknown; contacts = a 張總 on the parent's domain (not confirmed as a director) and two import-export managers on personal mail who registered for DigiWin events; lead in a colleague's 私田 (NOVA live 2026-10-24 · job 0b2b391c · KAINUO INDUSTRY (THAILAND) run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Stage:** courtesy reports plus event registrations, with no needs conversation = **D (需求探詢)**; "報名活動 ≠ C2" (same 行為序判定 as §77). Back to C2 = a conversation with a Thai decision-maker (the plant head or the 張總 contact) that confirms a will to fix stock, BOI or traceability; C1 = the 立案三要項 (quantified must-do, named project owner, dated go-live).
- **Entry when the only contacts sit in import-export:** use their own subject (BOI raw-material reconciliation, customs records) as the door, then move to stock and lot traceability, which is where the money is for a standardised, batch-sensitive auto part; confirm the BOI certificate exists before using the BOI door at all (§15, §40). Phase 1 stays ERP-first (§1).
- **Plays by parent system** (same as §86): DigiWin = same-source extension and fastest group reporting; Kingdee/Yonyou = Thai localisation and local service (do not claim they lack a Thai team, §60); SAP = two-tier, Thai ERP feeding the parent.
- **Route:** DigiWin China's office in the parent's city calls high at HQ (finance director or IT head); the Thai field owner follows up the event registrants with a value-first touch and asks for the decision-maker. Never say the plant's sales fell or its stock ballooned; no demo before pain, BOI status and the decision chain are known; never run down the parent's incumbent.
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA scripted the opener around "many Rayong peers were fined because import-export and stock did not match" and "customs and OEM audits are very strict this year" (unsourced fear-selling: not adopted); offered a "BOI and IATF compliance white paper for Chinese auto-parts plants in Thailand" (no such asset verified); claimed a "phase-2 expansion" (not evidenced); suggested citing "peer auto-parts cases in Rayong" (none verified, case-name hygiene); put HQ's share of the decision at "80" percent and sized the prize as cutting stock to an "industry-average 90 days" to free ~฿42M (invented benchmark); said Kingdee/Yonyou lack a Thai service team (contradicts §60) and SAP is "extremely costly" (unsourced); quoted the customer's own stock figure in a first-meeting question (keep filings as our questions, §43/§84). No control-workaround advice was offered.

## §88 — ACP C2 whose only daily report says "泰国没有权限，都是国内做主", at a fast-ramping Thai export plant (~฿786M second sales year, ×2.7, profitable, zero income tax, receivables ~207 days, stock ~158 days of COGS, current ratio below 1) of a PRC A+H-LISTED giant (refrigeration and auto thermal components, ~RMB 31B), shipping to the group's OWN US subsidiary; the data-list contact is the HQ division GM who is also the Thai sole signatory for investments; group ERP brand not public; lead in a colleague's 私田 (NOVA live 2026-10-24 · job fb5b3cff · SANHUA INDUSTRY (THAILAND) run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Stage:** one report, a data-list contact, no need, interest or pain expressed = **E (公田 / 長期經營)**, even though the SDR filed C2 (行為序判定). Back to C2 = a real conversation with the Thai on-site head (deputy GM) or his finance, planning or IT lead that acknowledges an operating pain and accepts that DigiWin may help; C1 = the 立案三要項 (quantified must-do, named Thai project owner, dated go-live).
- **What DigiWin TH can sell when "Thailand has no authority":** the Thai layer and the edge, never the core first. Order: (1) Thai localisation and compliance (Thai tax, and BOI material/machinery accounting only once a certificate is confirmed); (2) WMS and barcode at the newer site plus lot trace for export shipments; (3) group ERP only if the HQ relationship allows. Same logic as §52 and §69.
- **Route:** first ask DigiWin China's office in the parent's city whether the listed parent or the division is an account and what it runs; if it is an account, the HQ AM or CSM calls high at the division GM or group CFO; if not, learn the HQ ERP before anything is designed. The Thai field owner works the on-site deputy GM and the plant's IT, finance and planning leads; never ask a Thai manager "do you have authority, can we go to your HQ boss" (it closes the door), and never quote the plant's filings (stock days, current ratio) at a first meeting.
- **Ask internally:** does the HQ-city DigiWin team have any deal record with the group; are the Thai BOI and tax books kept on local software or by an accounting firm.
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA called Thai BOI and tax "the dead spot of SAP/Oracle or any China HQ ERP" (overclaim), read zero income tax as "heavy dependence on BOI" and threatened "巨額補稅" if reconciliation fails (BOI unverified; fear-selling), said HQ "usually delegates" Thai localisation purchases to the plant (unsourced, and contradicted by the SDR's report), called WMS a "剛需" because the US buyer demands lot trace (the US consignee is the group's own subsidiary; requirement not evidenced), dated the newer site's start to 2026–2027 (not evidenced; the site already appears in current job ads), scripted a first question that asserts the plant "enjoys BOI" and mentions tax audits, named the US consignee and praised the "×2.7 revenue" in the customer's presence (filings and customs records stay our questions, §43/§84), offered unnamed "Thai Chinese-manufacturer success cases" (none verified, case-name hygiene) and an interface "with any HQ brand" (§22: standard APIs plus per-project mapping only). None may be used. No control-workaround advice was offered, and nothing had the channel head contacting the customer.

## §89 — Sub-gate Taiwanese-Thai telecom CONTRACTOR and equipment trader (~฿28M after a ฿93M peak, two loss years, owner-funded, receivables ~161 days, registry = wholesale, no BOM) asking for "进销存+生产" in Thailand with FINANCE LEFT OUT because the group's mainland Kingdee "does not fit Thailand"; one contact (probably the director's family), C2 with amount 0; lead in a colleague's 私田 (NOVA live 2026-10-24 · job c20fdebc · THAI TE TECHNOLOGY (THAILAND) run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Grade:** C2 (意向溝通, internal consensus not reached) on the stated need, but **no presales (no demo) until two things are clear: what "生產" means and where the finance boundary sits**; treat as D / watch-list until then. C1 = a quantified must-do (stock loss or job-cost overrun per month), a project owner authorised by the signatory with finance or the director taking part, and a dated selection with a non-zero budget.
- **"生產" at an installer is usually not manufacturing:** for a telecom, solar or CCTV contractor it most likely means site material issue and return, subcontractor progress and project cost (PJT), not MRP/BOM. First clarifying question (through the field owner): fixed assembly with a BOM and a line, or job-site dispatch and material control?
- **Finance left out is workable only with a written boundary:** the system covers logistics and project progress; Thai tax invoices, WHT certificates, VAT/WHT filing and the official books stay with the customer's accountant (often an outside firm or a Thai package); no automatic link between the two systems is promised. The scope-creep risk is a later demand that the system print Thai tax invoices or WHT certificates. Ask who files Thai tax today and how documents reach them before scoping.
- **Kingdee in the group (mainland):** position as the Thai operations front end, never as a rival for group finance; no price fight, and walk away if it becomes one (a cut-price Thai customisation for a loss-making contractor cannot be delivered profitably). Note: Kingdee launched a Thailand finance-and-tax pack (LocalKits, 2026-06-30), so finance is where Kingdee now has a Thai answer.
- **Route and window test:** DigiWin TW / China check CRM and registries for the director and the foreign holders, the related parent, its Kingdee edition and implementer. Test the contact, through the field owner: can the Thai director decide alone or is group sign-off needed; will a Thai PM or site lead join the evaluation. No introduction upward = information gatherer, high project risk.
- **Deal guard at this size:** standard light cloud trading scope only, no free customisation, subscription paid in advance (KB §23/§32).
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA scripted a director-level question quoting the customer's receivable days from the filings (not adopted; filings stay our questions), gave a past example date ("2026/Q2"), called the sole director "董事長" and assumed a Taiwan HQ (not evidenced), called Kingdee in Thailand "mostly resellers, weak engineering delivery" (unverified, internal only), said data could flow back to HQ Kingdee "by hand or a simple API" (KB §22: standard APIs plus per-project mapping only), and proposed a personal joint guarantee from the director or parent, a 50-40-10 milestone schedule and a "15 days overdue, system stops" clause (disproportionate or unverified contract terms; not adopted). No control-workaround advice; all customer contact stays with the field owner.

## §90 — Ramping Thai GREENFIELD medical-device plant (home-care thermometers and BP monitors; ฿388M equity, ฿288M PP&E, pre-revenue in its FY2025 filings, first US shipment 2026) of a TAIWAN-OTC parent that is an in-family TIPTOP account by the SDR's note ("TT老客，想做泰国财税。但预算较低"), only contact a non-director whose mailbox is on ANOTHER company's same-name domain; lead in a colleague's 私田 (NOVA live 2026-10-24 · job 26c80b58 · AVITA CORP (THAILAND) run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Grade:** an unverified contact (title unknown, not a director, identity in doubt) + "wants Thai finance and tax, budget low" + no date = **D (需求探詢)**, down from ACP C2. C1 needs: the contact's role confirmed or a direct line to the parent's IT or finance owner; a dated clock (next tax, audit or customer-audit point); the budget source known (parent capex vs Thai opex). Claude vetting: consistent with §70/§76/§78 (one vague ask, broken or unverified contact = D) and stricter than §80 (C2 where the contact was the GM).
- **Architecture for a TIPTOP group's new Thai plant:** (a) the Thai company as its own organisation on the parent's TIPTOP plus Thai localisation (VAT, WHT, Thai documents; e-Tax only if wanted) first; (b) a separate light Thai system tied back only if the version is too old or MA has lapsed. Deciding facts: TIPTOP version and MA, whether Thai key users can work in Chinese or English, BOI status. Same as §43/§79, with the §68 exception.
- **"Budget low" at a funded greenfield:** reads as "HQ will not approve much IT capex for a plant that does not earn yet; wants the minimum to be compliant", not inability to pay. MVP = purchasing, sales, stock + Thai tax pack. Claude vetting (TH register): lot fields on receipt, issue and shipment belong in phase 1 on their merits for a medical-device plant; full DHR and floor actuals stay eMES phase 2.
- **Route:** the DigiWin TW account manager for the parent leads (installed-base courtesy call on the parent's IT and finance owners); the Thai SDR confirms the contact's job and e-mail; the channel head supplies research, a verified Thai reference and an internal architecture sketch, no customer contact. Neutral IT-head question: would opening the Thai company inside the current TIPTOP be the most efficient route for permissions, shared item/BOM masters and maintenance, and who supports the Thai localisation locally?
- **Identity check (Claude, not NOVA):** when an ACP contact's mail domain belongs to a different same-name company (here a Hong Kong laptop brand vs a Taiwanese medical group) and ACP's industry tag points the same way, confirm the contact's employer before any presales; if wrong, the record is mis-keyed (§34).
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA asserted "DigiWin TH has a mature TIPTOP GP Thai localisation kit including e-Tax" and a "GP 3.0 or below" cut-off (unverified; §68 records an old GP 5.1x reportedly unable to do Thai e-invoicing; get TH delivery's written position for the exact version before any quote); stated "a ฿300M plant certainly has BOI" and built BOI-cancellation and back-tax risk on it (BOI not found); scripted a chairman opener that quotes the customer's customs-data shipment and asks for a DHR "in 30 minutes… 以規避關稅與合規風險" (fear-based, framed as tariff avoidance); called traceability "一票否決, 輕則退貨、重則停工" as a selling line; gave a duty formula with "5%-20%" and "整批退貨（貨值百萬起跳）" (unsourced); quoted Thai filing deadlines without a source; and offered a "台商泰國建廠財稅與 BOI 合規白皮書" not known to exist. None may be used. No control-workaround advice was adopted; all customer contact stays with the field owner.

## §91 — Profitable year-two Thai COATINGS plant (~฿205M, +39, no bank debt, stock ~106 days, full-rate tax) of a PRC coatings group whose parent is mid-IPO on the BSE (review suspended for stale financials) and runs SAP + CRM/SRM/MES/APS at HQ; the SDR's only report says "泰国这边用的express+sap，没有单独买erp，下周拜访", then 24 days of silence; contact = a group-mailbox employee-platform partner, not a director; lead in a colleague's 私田 (NOVA live 2026-10-24 · job e90e3aa2 · ZHANCHEN NEW MATERIALS (THAILAND) run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Grade:** a system-facts-only report with an unconfirmed visit and no stated pain = **E (長期經營)**, down from ACP C2 (same behaviour-sequence rule as §88; stricter than §81, where the customer had actually asked for a light ERP). Back to C2 once the field owner confirms a conversation in which the Thai GM, finance or the contact acknowledges a pain; C1 = the 立案三要項 (quantified must-do, a named Thai or HQ project owner, a dated go-live).
- **Reading "Express + SAP, no ERP bought":** most likely Thai VAT/WHT and statutory books in Express (often with an accounting firm), operations either keyed into HQ SAP as an overseas company code or kept in Excel and summarised for SAP. Confirm through the field owner before designing anything.
- **First ticket under an entrenched parent SAP:** DigiWin ERP as the Thai sub-ERP in a two-tier set-up (purchasing, sales, stock, batch work orders with lot and expiry, Thai finance with VAT/WHT/e-Tax) with structured exports to HQ, and only with HQ's agreement. Do NOT lead with batch-trace eMES at a small Thai plant that would need deep SAP integration and has no local IT. Same shape as §81.
- **Coatings first-visit prompts:** how recipe changes from HQ reach the Thai floor; how tint leftovers and returns are booked; lot and shelf life of resins and solvents; how many days after month-end Thai numbers reach HQ.
- **Route:** internal check with DigiWin China's office in the parent's city first; brief the field owner; his meeting is about how Thai numbers reach SAP, not about product.
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA built the C1 must-do on "brokers and auditors will certainly scrutinise the Express + SAP split" (architecture unconfirmed, audit claim unsourced), dated the IPO re-filing to 2027 (not public), sized stock relief with an unsourced "industry-reasonable 70 days", scripted HQ-finance questions that quote the IPO and the plant's filings at the customer, told the SDR to sell "a guarantee of passing the BSE IPO audit" (fear-based and undeliverable), promised "two-way reconciliation" with SAP (§22: standard APIs plus per-project mapping), offered an "IPO compliance white paper" and "e-Tax/BOI cases" not known to exist (BOI not evidenced: the plant pays full-rate tax), and had the channel head accompany the SDR or "call high" at HQ finance or the Thai GM. None may be used; all customer contact stays with the field owner. No control-workaround advice was offered.

## §92 — Pre-production Thai GREENFIELD (registry: furniture plus automotive power tailgates and power seats; ฿245M capital raised in seven steps, ฿122M PP&E, zero revenue and stock) held 100% from Singapore by an UNIDENTIFIED parent, whose ERP is already BOUGHT in China (Kingdee) and whose SDR's only report reads "MES跟WMS有机会跟进。还没有投产"; contact = a non-director GM; lead in a colleague's 私田 (NOVA live 2026-10-24 · job b7027d4d · LANTSEA INDUSTRIAL run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Grade:** the SDR's own reading of an opportunity (not a customer-stated MES/WMS evaluation), pre-production, amount 0, 24 days quiet = **D (需求探詢)**, down from ACP C2 (same behaviour-sequence rule as §78). C1 = a must-do the plant states (a production-start date plus a customer or quality requirement it cannot meet without floor or warehouse control), a named Thai project owner, and a dated go-live with a budget.
- **ERP already chosen (Kingdee) → complement play, WMS first:** barcode receiving and issue, stock accuracy and location control while routings are still unstable; floor reporting later. Claude vetting: NOVA named SFT as the follow-on, but SFT is native to Workflow ERP; on a Kingdee ERP the floor option is sMES or eMES as a phase 2, and Kingdee's own MES/WMS modules are the default rival.
- **Kingdee interface claim:** standard API specification and intermediate tables (items, BOMs, receipts and issues) plus per-project mapping, with the Kingdee side's work agreed between the customer and Kingdee (§22). Never "real-time two-way sync" and never a Kingdee integration track record until DigiWin TH delivery confirms a named, citable case.
- **Route:** DigiWin China CRM check on the contact's name and mail domain first (never on guessed Chinese characters); the researcher briefs the field owner and never contacts the customer.
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA built the must-do on "if the automaker audit fails, no shipments" (fear framing; whether the plant supplies automakers or holds IATF 16949 is not evidenced), scripted a "one-hour" lot-trace requirement (invented), assumed imported high-value hardware and electronics, offered a "泰國汽配建廠稽核規範與 WMS/MES 規劃白皮書" not known to exist, suggested asking Kingdee's channel about the group's Kingdee edition (ask the customer through the field owner instead), and treated the contact as the decision-maker although he is not on the registry. None may be used as fact. No control-workaround advice was offered.

## §93 — ACP C2 filed on a DORMANT shell (฿2M Thai–PRC wholesale company, zero revenue two years, sole director) whose brand-twin is a small fast-ramping Taiwanese-owned coil plant on the Myanmar border with no registry link; SDR's angle "泰國單獨上ERP的必要性", only contact a non-signatory IT manager; lead in a colleague's 私田 (NOVA live 2026-10-24 · job 9ba28a5c · WISUNO ELECTRONIC PASSIVE run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Grade:** five elements empty, contact only receiving event invitations, no power holder reached, 54 days quiet = **D (需求探詢)**, down from ACP C2. C2 needs the customer to know the problem is solvable and internal consensus not yet reached.
- **Wrong entity on the record:** recommend that the field owner add or re-point the ACP record to the operating plant, because a zero-revenue shell can never be 立案 or closed. Before re-pointing confirm three things: the contact's employer (mail domain, phone or office; with personal webmail, ask where he sits), whether the foreign holders have a parent where decisions sit, and whether the contact acts for the Thai plant or collects material for a head office. Claude vetting: brand plus product category is not a registry link; keep "possible, unverified" until the field owner confirms.
- **"Thailand needs its own ERP" is the wrong opener at ~฿50M:** a separate mid-size ERP with local IT is a TCO the owner will refuse. Order: (1) dual-track multi-organisation (parent system plus the Thai plant, light web or barcode entry on site) when the parent runs DigiWin; (2) otherwise a light local cloud ERP aimed at lot and barcode needs. Reframe as "a low-cost set-up headquarters can see and the plant can run". Consistent with §43 and §70.
- **ERP before MES at this size:** without item, BOM, routing, stock and lot data, MES output cannot become cost; keep an MES event as vision and follow up with lot control plus barcode as the foundation.
- **Coil / magnetics plant pains (hypotheses to ask):** copper wire issued by the reel against a per-unit BOM (returns and remnants distort stock and cost); winding machines as the bottleneck; lot traceability of wire and core lots for export customers' audits. Ask generically; never name the customer or quote customs records.
- **Champion test:** a champion shares concrete pain and helps book the head; an information gatherer asks for decks and prices and deflects who decides and when. Ask DigiWin TW to check the holders and directors (Thai script only, never guessed characters) for a parent and any Workflow/T100 account; if found, the TW AM calls high at the parent, routed through the field owner.
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA claimed calling high in Taiwan is "快 10 倍" (invented), pushed a BOI angle with no certificate evidenced, offered "財務由台灣控管，當地稅務外包" as a design (an option to ask about, not a recommendation), scripted "我們注意到許多外銷美國的被動元件廠…" (unsourced peer claim) and a five-element prompt naming the US buyer (customs records stay our questions, §43/§84/§88), and echoed the question's framing about the workforce as if it were evidence. No control-workaround advice; contact stays with the field owner.

## §94 — Year-one Thai EXPORT plant (registered 2024, ฿130M PP&E group-funded through interest-free current liabilities, pre-revenue in its FY2025 filing, first US bills of lading June 2026) of a PRC private exhaust-parts group, running KINGDEE Cloud Galaxy for operations plus PEAK (Thai cloud accounting) for the Thai books, SDR's only report "没有办法支持业财一体化", staff met, on-site interview planned, contacts = purchasing, finance and production staff (no director); lead in a colleague's 私田 (NOVA live 2026-10-24 · job f75b2bae · JIAWEN AUTO PARTS (THAILAND) run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Grade:** staff-level complaint, none of the 立案三要項, 36 days quiet = **D (需求探詢)**, down from ACP C2. Back to C2 = the interview is held and a director or plant head accepts that DigiWin may propose; C1 = a quantified must-do, a Thai PM appointed with a director's authority, a dated go-live with a budget range.
- **Kingdee (operations) + PEAK (Thai books) "業財不一體":** the usual reality is stock movements in Kingdee that create no vouchers in PEAK, Thai tax documents (VAT return, WHT certificates, tax-invoice formats) produced outside Kingdee and keyed into PEAK, and three currencies (THB, USD sales, RMB group) reconciled by hand. Play = replace both AT THE PLANT with one Thai system (Workflow ERP: purchasing, sales, stock, work orders and issue, Thai AP/AR/GL with VAT and WHT) and hand vouchers or the trial balance back to the group's Kingdee (§22: standard export plus per-project mapping). Respect Kingdee's group role; never disparage it.
- **Main threat:** Kingdee's own Thai pack (LocalKits, 2026-06-30, §89) closes the same gap inside Kingdee and drops PEAK; a PEAK partner may build a bridge. Answer with Thai delivery, Thai-language floor screens and the plant's own kit costing, not with claims about Kingdee's Thai capability.
- **Stainless exhaust plant interview prompts (hypotheses):** WIP and reporting at the bending, welding and polishing bottlenecks; kit BOMs per vehicle fitment and kit completeness at packing (missing parts are claimed by the buyer); stainless tube yield and scrap accounting; lot trace from tube lot to finished part; days to close the month and where Kingdee and PEAK disagree. Phase 1 = purchasing, sales, stock + Thai AP/AR/GL/VAT/WHT + basic work orders and issue; per-worker actuals later (eMES), no APS on iGP.
- **Tier:** Workflow ERP (iGP), not T100, for a year-one plant of this size.
- **Route:** DigiWin China offices in the group's cities check whether the group is an account and what it runs; the field owner plays the interview findings back to a director or the plant head at a second meeting.
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA made BOI a must ("必須碰", bonded-material balance, "免稅額度被扣罰") with no certificate evidenced; framed the must-do and the director invitation around fines, BOI and tax-penalty risk (fear-selling); said Kingdee "has no Thai local team and cannot produce WHT/VAT/tax-invoice formats" (unverified, contradicted by the 2026 Thai pack; internal only); scripted an "IATF 16949 audit by MBRP/Borla" with a "2 hours" lot-trace test (no certificate or audit requirement evidenced; customs buyers never named to the customer, §88); offered "四大罰款風險" cases and a tax-compliance clinic not known to exist; claimed a mature BOI module and a 3–4 month go-live for Workflow (unverified). None may be used as fact. No control-workaround advice; nothing had the channel head contacting the customer.

## §95 — Profitable Taiwanese-run Thai BEVERAGE co-packer (~฿970M, net margin ~3, US private-label buyers who audit the plant) whose own job posts point to SAP Business One, where the SDR's only report is "初步需求在BPM，HR"; lead in a colleague's 私田 (NOVA live 2026-10-24 · job 7a06fd0a · First Canned Food (Thai) run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Stage:** one report, a single first-name contact on a personal mailbox with no title and not a signatory, 36 days quiet = **D (需求探詢)** even at ACP C2. Back to C2 / up to C1 on three facts: a quantified must-do (hours spent checking time, OT and leave and building the wage report for HQ; Thai-Taiwan purchase-approval lead time), a named owner (often a newly hired admin or accounting manager) and the HQ chairman's role as approver, and a date tied to an operational event.
- **First ticket:** BPM (EasyFlow / DigiWin BPM) on top of SAP Business One for cross-border approvals (purchase requisitions, formula or process changes, customer-audit corrective actions, OT and leave), with HR handled as attendance and approval flows. **Do not open an ERP replacement** while the customer is hiring accountants with B1 experience; revisit T100 / Workflow only after BPM has earned the data flows.
- **HR honesty line:** per NOVA, DigiWin's HR offer in Thailand does not itself compute Thai payroll, social security or withholding (50 ทวิ); those stay in a Thai payroll package and are integrated, with BPM carrying attendance approvals and the payroll result for sign-off. Verify with DigiWin TH delivery in writing before any customer claim; never promise "100 compliant".
- **Positioning:** cross-functional flow layer (BPM) above both the Thai payroll package and the B1 ledger; do not assert competitors' weaknesses (license cost of B1 approval add-ons, local HR packages' limits) as fact.
- **Interview prompts (customer words, no filing figures):** formula/process change approval lead time from Taiwan R&D to the Thai plant and the audit trail buyers ask for; how plant purchase requests are approved by HQ (mail, paper, system) and how budget is controlled; how attendance and OT for the workforce are checked and turned into the wage report HQ receives.
- **Route:** first an internal DigiWin TW check on whether the Taiwanese HQ company is an account; the field owner keeps all customer contact; the channel head supplies material and cases, never contacts the account.
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA's purchase question quoted the filing back at the customer (interest growth, inventory value; §43) and is reworded; "SAP B1 HR/attendance is extremely weak", "B1 approval add-ons are very expensive" and "local HR systems cannot handle non-HR cross-border approvals" are unverified and not used as claims; "100 percent compliant with Thai labour law" is an overclaim; its proposed white paper citing "many Taiwanese Thai food co-packers" as success cases is not backed by a verified case list (only TW 1024 久津實業 2018 EasyFlow on a self-developed ERP, VN 3237 萬得福 2022 and VN 3343 豆鮮 2023 Workflow ERP are verified, as history); calling the new hires "內線" is reworded as possible project owners. No control-workaround advice; nothing had the channel head contacting the customer.

## §96 — Micro Thai spring plant (~฿10M sales, near-zero profit, negative equity, interest-free owner loans, round-number stock above three years of cost of sales) of 100 percent Chinese capital with no public parent, supplying Delta Electronics Thailand per the GM, books kept by hand, GM (not the sole signatory) asking for an ERP and a quote while the factory moves; SDR also sent an MES event invitation; lead in a colleague's 私田 (NOVA live 2026-10-24 · job e617e1e9 · EISEI Metal Products (Thailand) run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Stage:** oral intent from a non-signatory GM, no budget, no date, no project owner, mid-move, 39 days quiet = **D (需求探詢)**, whatever ACP shows. C1 needs the 立案三要項: a dated go-live the customer commits to (for example within three months of the move), a quantified must-do (for example a customer such as Delta actually demanding lot traceability), and a named project owner with a budget range.
- **Product at a fifth of the iGP floor:** never iGP or a heavy local ERP; a light cloud subscription ERP or minimal Workflow modules sold as a subscription. Phase 1 = item master, simple BOM, work orders (progress and material use), stock. Not in phase 1: Thai tax and the ledger, which stay with the outside bookkeeper (system data exported to them; write the boundary down, same logic as §89). Stop pushing MES at a plant with almost no fixed assets and no ERP base.
- **Factory move timing (reusable):** plan and code items/BOMs before the move (after signing), train key users during the production stop, go live one to two months after the move has settled, using the new plant's first full stock count as the opening balance. Never go live in the middle of the move.
- **Finding an unknown mainland parent:** the shipper on the plant's wire imports, Chinese registry searches on the director's name, then a DigiWin China CRM check once a Chinese company name exists; if it is an account, the China AM gives the decision chain.
- **Deal guard:** standard scope only, a capped number of consulting days, zero customisation; NOVA proposed 50/30/20 milestones with licence keys released after each payment.
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA's sample question invented a "within 1 hour" Delta trace demand (Delta's requirements are not evidenced; reworded without it); it treated the wire shipper as "very likely" the parent (a lead, not proof; Thai customs data is paid); it called the GM the director (the sole DBD director is a different person with the same surname); "Delta requires traceability or order share suffers" is a hypothesis to test, not a fact; for a negative-equity plant the KB's prepaid-subscription rule (§23, §32, §89) is safer than milestones, and payment terms belong to the field owner and DigiWin's standard. No control-workaround advice: ledger and Thai tax filing stay with the customer's bookkeeper; nothing had the channel head contacting the customer.

## §97 — Pre-production Thai SEALANT and ADHESIVE greenfield (registry: silicone sealant, adhesives, grout; ฿45M capital from Hong Kong 99 / Singapore 1, parent UNRESOLVED, ฿1M PP&E, zero stock) whose group runs KINGDEE on the mainland and finds its purchasing, sales, stock and production "unfit for Thailand", asking for an ERP with finance left out "for now"; contact = a non-director 特助 on personal mail, 36 days quiet; lead in a colleague's 私田 (NOVA live 2026-10-24 · job 808aa8a3 · RUI TAI NEW MATERIALS run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Stage:** a relayed need with a stated scope, but no signatory reached, no budget, no date and a month of silence = **D (需求探詢)**, whatever ACP shows. C1 needs all three: (a) a production or first-shipment deadline from which go-live is back-calculated; (b) an authorised project owner (the contact formally mandated, or a director or plant head joining) and a working group; (c) a budget range with a decision month.
- **Finance-less scope (reusable):** Workflow ERP can run purchasing, sales, stock and production without the general ledger, but in Thailand the tax invoice must tie to the shipment; without the AR side the Thai staff re-key every sale into the outside books for ภ.พ.30, and (per NOVA) DigiWin's e-Tax integration needs the AR module. Ask first where Thai bookkeeping and VAT filing live (accounting firm, Express, the mainland Kingdee) and why finance is out (a group consolidation rule, or no Thai finance staff yet); minimum sensible scope = operations plus AR with Thai localisation, GL later. Confirm the e-Tax dependency with DigiWin TH delivery before telling a customer.
- **Silicone sealant / adhesive plant discovery themes (NOVA's industry priors, questions only, never findings):** one batch recipe filled into several pack sizes (cartridge, sausage, pail) and the fill loss per size; lot, shelf life and FIFO for raw materials and finished sealant, and near-expiry returns; recovered or reworked material blended back and how it is costed; dosing accuracy for catalysts and curing agents (a shop-floor scale/eMES topic, not phase-1 ERP); purchase by weight, issue by volume, sale by piece or carton (unit conversion per item).
- **Unknown mainland parent:** get the group's Chinese name from the customer through the field owner (or the paid บอจ.5 shareholder list), then a DigiWin China CRM check on that name; never search CRM on guessed characters (here "瑞泰" would hit an unrelated listed 江蘇瑞泰).
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA told the visitor to bring "鼎新在泰國當地的中資化工/膠水業成功案例" (no such verified Thai case; only 2018 Taiwanese adhesive-film and resin pages exist: 603 宏櫂, 1059 德亞); suggested opening on BOI filing and tax-audit risk and a related-party tax question aimed at "the decision-maker's biggest fear" (BOI not evidenced; fear framing); translated dosing errors into "X 萬泰銖 scrap risk if you do not buy an ERP with dosing error-proofing" (fear-selling; ERP does not do dosing control); offered automatic temperature/density unit conversion as a capability; and proposed the regional manager join through a "協同拜訪" (customer contact stays with the field owner). None adopted. No control-workaround advice was offered.

## §98 — Fitting-out Thai GREENFIELD (฿800M capital, ฿401M PP&E group-funded, ฿0.5M first sales) of a PRC-LISTED gear-motor and reducer group whose China side runs Kingdee + Weaver OA + 思普 PLM + 中之杰 MES/WMS and says the data "do not connect well", whose Thai automated warehouse (立庫) is being built by the same 中之杰 for a year-end start, and whose only contact (a non-director 總經理/生產 on a Ningbo line) relayed interest in "a separate Thai system"; lead in a colleague's 私田 (NOVA live 2026-10-24 · job 869f9041 · ZHONGDA LEADER MOTION CONTROL (THAILAND) run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Grade:** relayed interest, no signatory reached, no budget, no date, 24 days quiet = **D (需求探詢)**, whatever ACP shows (same rule as §92, §94, §97). C1 needs a dated must-do (system go-live back-calculated from the production start), a Thai project owner named with a director's authority, and a budget range with a decision date.
- **Partner-built AS/RS from the group's own smart-factory vendor:** ERP = business and finance brain (items, BOMs, receiving notices, issue plans, stock ledger); the vendor's WMS/WCS = execution (slots, put-away, equipment), returning actual quantities and lots. Offer DigiWin WMS only if the vendor supplies no WMS layer (consistent with §50, where DigiWin WMS is the brain over a partner WCS). Interface wording per §22: standard API specification plus per-project mapping, the other vendor's side agreed between the customer and that vendor.
- **Customer says its China systems "do not connect":** use it as a blank-sheet argument (define master-data ownership and the interface list once, before the plant starts), never as criticism of the incumbent vendors.
- **Tier:** NOVA leaned T100 (listed parent, ฿800M capital, precision manufacturing) with APS in phase 1. Claude vetting: §17/§81 keep APS out of phase 1 and put a pre-revenue plant on iGP first; the tier is decided by /select-t100-vs-workflow once the plant's volumes and the group's intent are known.
- **Route:** DigiWin China's office in the parent's city checks CRM and, with the field owner's agreement, takes the conversation to the controllers (who are very likely the Thai directors); the Thai side works the plant lead through the field owner. The researcher never contacts the customer.
- **Reducer / gear-motor new-plant discovery prompts (NOVA's, questions only):** how BOMs and engineering changes will reach the plant from the group PLM; how the AS/RS will receive its orders and return movements; first-year ramp targets set by the parent; Thai-language screens and operating habits on the floor and in the warehouse.
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA claimed Workflow "struggles" with multi-level BOMs and consolidation, that T100 has "many" Chinese-plant references in Thailand, that Kingdee in Thailand relies on agents and lacks floor and BOI depth, and that DigiWin TH has a mature BOI raw-material solution (all unverified; Kingdee launched a Thai pack in 2026, §89); promised "high" automatic PLM-to-ERP sync and "real-time" warehouse returns (beyond §22); built the must-do and the call-high topic on BOI compliance risk with no BOI certificate evidenced (fear framing); suggested citing "other successful Chinese cases in the same industrial zone" (none verified). None adopted. No control-workaround advice was offered; nothing had the channel head contacting the customer.

## §99 — ACP C2 whose only daily report says the customer "thinks its BOI is already well run, but is fairly open", at a LARGE, fast-growing Thai export plant (~฿6.4B, +38, stock ×3.3, phase 3 opening, BOI clerks being hired for formula / release / write-off / scrap) of a PRC-LISTED, Taiwanese-founded group with STRONG in-house IT (34 systems, a self-built manufacturing platform, a sister company that sells injection MES), ERP brand unknown; contacts = a group-IT manager and a buyer; lead in a colleague's 私田 (NOVA live 2026-10-24 · job 2867c690 · RUNNER INDUSTRY (THAILAND) run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Stage:** social openness plus a stated "BOI is fine", no agreed pain, no budget, no project team, no signatory reached, a month silent = **D (需求探詢)** whatever ACP says. Back to C2 = the plant head, finance lead or BOI desk acknowledges that "fine" is held up by heavy manual work worth looking at. C1 = the 立案三要項: a quantified must-do tied to the volume jump or the new phase, a named evaluation team (IT plus finance/BOI), a target date before the new phase starts.
- **First cut against strong group IT = the Thai-only BOI raw-material layer** (formula and scrap-rate set-up, duty-free release and write-off reports, scrap), fed from whatever ERP the plant runs. Reason: a group IT team builds for its home country and is unlikely to build and maintain Thai BOI logic for one site; the plant's own BOI-staff job post is the evidence of manual work. **Do not touch** ERP replacement, MES, SRM or APS when the group runs its own platform and a sister sells MES. Interface wording per §22/§60: report formats and upload files plus per-project mapping; filing stays with the BOI desk or adviser.
- **Answering "our BOI is fine":** questions only, never a threat: how formula filings, releases and write-offs are prepared today and how long each quarter's reconciliation takes now that volume has jumped; how floor scrap records and the BOI material balance are matched, and how often. Objection "our group IT will build it": a Thai-supported product keeps the group's developers on their own platform instead of maintaining one country's BOI rules.
- **Route:** first ask the field owner who said "BOI is fine", whether the IT contact reports to the Thai plant or to group IT, and which ERP the plant runs; then DigiWin China's office in the parent's city checks ERP brand, any past MES/ERP relationship and its decision-maker, and whether HQ lets the overseas plant buy local systems. The channel head coaches the SDR (scripts, translating answers into a workload case) and never contacts the customer.
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA proposed a "BOI compliance-risk white paper" for the customer and BOI "success cases" 富強鑫 and 台達電 (富強鑫 is in neither the TW mirror nor the Monk cards; 台達電 appears in the internal BOI playbook as a penalty case, not a customer); it said Thai BOI rules and customs interfaces change "every 2-3 years" (unsourced) and used back-tax, fine and shipment-delay risk as must-do reasons and in a customer question (fear framing); it assumed the BOI desk works in Excel and called a job-post standard an IATF certification; it named the parent's MES "鼎捷電子組裝 MES" from a slide that names no product; it suggested the SDR record customer calls. None adopted. No control-workaround advice was offered; nothing had the channel head contacting the customer.

## §100 — Ramping Thai GREENFIELD PAIR of a US family-run brand (toilet maker; a plastic-moulding company and an assembly/import-export company registered the same day next door, same two American directors = the parent's CEO and EVP by name, ฿52M registered across the pair, pre-revenue in FY2025) where a non-director IT manager on a QQ mailbox took a 10-user Workflow quote, said the floor price is over his RMB 100–200K perpetual budget, compares Kingdee or Yonyou SaaS, and parked the ERP visit until a finance manager is hired; lead in a colleague's 私田 (NOVA live 2026-10-24 · job 0207c23e · NIAGARA CONSERVATION FINISHING (THAILAND) run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Stage:** a quote, a stated budget and a named shortlist, but no finance lead, no signatory involved and no date = **C2 (需求探詢 / 找權力人士提案)**, not D: unlike the relayed-interest greenfields (§92, §94, §97, §98), the customer has a number and a comparison. C1 needs: (a) the parent's first-shipment deadline from Thailand, from which go-live is back-calculated; (b) the finance lead in place and a selection group with the IT contact; (c) a dated go-live month tied to production.
- **Price gap against cloud rivals (reusable):** no price war. Offer the Workflow subscription model to lower the first-year outlay, and size phase 1 to what the plant runs now: purchasing, sales, stock plus the finance needed for Thai tax and equipment purchases; production and costing before full output. 10 users is reasonable for phase 1. Price and terms stay the field owner's call.
- **Two Thai companies that will trade with each other (reusable):** one Workflow database with both companies (multi-company), so the moulding-to-assembly trade is booked once on each side; never two separate systems. Ask early whether the moulding company sells parts to the assembly company or works for it on a fee, and in which currency the two companies and the overseas buyer settle.
- **"Wait until finance is hired" (reusable):** do not push; ask which accounting firm keeps the Thai books now, and bring the finance lead into the selection when hired. Same family as §55 (no finance person) but here the hire is under way, so the play is patience plus preparation, not a remote-HQ-finance design.
- **Foreign (non-Chinese) signatories, Mandarin-speaking plant team:** the SDR helps the IT contact carry an English or bilingual evaluation pack to the overseas signatories (scope, Thai localisation, multi-company handling, references with their years), so DigiWin reaches the signatories through the contact, not around him. Ask how the Thai numbers must reach the parent's books (currency, chart, timing), not which accounting standard "compliance" requires.
- **Toilet-parts moulding + assembly discovery themes (NOVA's, questions only):** first-shipment date to the parent; moulds new or moved from China, and how mould life and maintenance will be tracked; intercompany flow and settlement currency.
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA called Kingdee and Yonyou agent-run with "very low" Thai tax and BOI support (unverified; Kingdee has a 2026 Thai pack, §89); said buying wrong costs "tens of times" the licence fee (unsourced; fear framing); assumed BOI and bonded imports for both companies and built questions on losing BOI privileges (no BOI evidenced; fear framing); invented an "OBU 轉單" flow, a Thai tax/BOI guide PDF and a "Compliance Risk Assessment" pack (no such assets verified); proposed opening with a "100 percent" compliance guarantee and amplifying the risk of Chinese-vendor service failures (overclaim; disparaging rivals); gave a past example go-live date. None adopted. No control-workaround advice was offered; nothing had the channel head contacting the customer.

## §101 — Equipped, pre-revenue Thai SHAFT/CRANKSHAFT greenfield (฿326M capital, ฿262M PP&E, Singapore 100) very likely the first overseas base of a large PRC private shaft group (compressor eccentric shafts, motorcycle and car crankshafts), where the SDR's only report is "客戶告知會有需求，需要再次確認，客戶最近在參展" with no contact recorded; lead in a colleague's 私田 (NOVA live 2026-10-24 · job 71867819 · GANGYANG INDUSTRIES (THAILAND) run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Grade:** a one-line "there will be a need" with no stated pain, no must-do, no named contact and no consensus = **D (需求探詢)**, whatever ACP shows (same behaviour-sequence rule as §77, §78, §92, §94, §97, §98). C1 = the 立案三要項: a must-do with a date (a production start the system must precede, or a customer requirement the plant must meet), a named Thai owner or project team, a dated system timetable.
- **First move for a big PRC parent:** DigiWin China's business unit covering the parent's city checks CRM on the parent's published name. Parent on a DigiWin product → template rollout (same product line; the Thai team delivers Thai language, tax and localisation; version decides instance vs tie-back, §43/§61/§68/§79). Parent not on DigiWin → treat as a new PRC overseas greenfield. A locally invisible plant (no website, job post or BOI item) is best read at HQ, with the field owner's agreement, never around the Thai contact.
- **Tier:** Workflow ERP (iGP) phase 1 for an equipped, pre-production plant (purchasing, sales, stock, finance with Thai tax, basic material issue; fixed assets per §54 vetting). T100 only if (a) the parent requires group consolidation and multi-organisation control on its own T100/TIPTOP, or (b) first customers require process-level trace and fine costing from trial production.
- **SDR re-contact themes (after a trade show):** production and system go-live dates; how Thai tax invoices and VAT filing are handled today; who leads the system evaluation.
- **Crankshaft / eccentric-shaft plant discovery themes (NOVA's, questions only):** unit cost including depreciation and outside processing while volumes are low; heat treatment or surface treatment in-house or outside, and how outside work, loss and fees are tracked; batch trace from finished shaft to material and inspection records when a customer asks; grinding-wheel and tool life; long batches vs frequent changeovers and WIP bottlenecks.
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA used an IATF 16949 audit by named buyers as the example must-do and named those buyers (from the parent's customs data) in a customer question (not evidenced for the Thai plant; buyers never named, §88); opened a re-contact question on "strict BOI tax audits for new foreign plants" and put BOI in phase 1 (BOI not evidenced; fear framing); addressed the unknown contact as a director; offered "free plant-planning consulting" (a commercial term, not the researcher's to promise); quoted the plant's filed equipment value back at it (filings stay our questions, §43/§95). None adopted. No control-workaround advice was offered; nothing had the channel head contacting the customer.

## §102 — ACP C2 at a profitable Hong Kong-held Thai company (~฿99M revenue, ~24 net margin, ~฿360M assets mostly liability-funded, D/E ~3.4) whose REGISTRY says factory-rental landlord at registration and engineering R&D in the latest filing (services group), while its name says "precision manufacturing" and ACP says auto parts; the only report is "客户说有ERP需求，后面联系他" from a non-signatory 總經理 on a QQ mailbox; parent and products unknown, no web footprint; lead in a colleague's 私田 (NOVA live 2026-10-24 · job 4b306fea · TAURI PRECISION MANUFACTURING run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Grade:** a one-line "has an ERP need" with none of the 立案三要項 = **D (需求探詢)** (NOVA mapped it to CRM A5), whatever ACP shows; same behaviour-sequence rule as §77, §78, §93, §101. C1 = a quantified must-do, a named Thai project contact besides the GM, a dated timetable.
- **Entity check before any product talk (registry business ≠ name):** three questions through the field owner: (1) what does the Thai company ship, and does it have its own production floor and stock or does a related company produce; (2) are the people on site mostly operators or engineers and lab staff; (3) which legal entity would sign and use the ERP (this company, another Thai company, or the Hong Kong / mainland parent). A rental objective "to client companies or affiliates" plus an R&D filing code is one signal of a landlord or group-service vehicle, never proof; the name and the GM's need are the counter-signal.
- **Tier if it is a ~฿100M parts plant:** Workflow ERP (iGP) first (fast, low-risk go-live). T100 only if the parent is a DigiWin T100 account that requires the Thai plant inside its multi-organisation set-up.
- **If it is a landlord or R&D company (no BOM):** do not push production or MRP. Workflow finance plus purchasing and stock (R&D material issue, asset purchases); fixed assets and lease contracts against rental income; project hours and cost allocation for R&D; multi-currency reporting to the Hong Kong / mainland side. Lead with low total price, compliance and a quick go-live.
- **Parent check:** NOVA: trace the Hong Kong holders through the Hong Kong registry and have DigiWin China check its CRM. Claude: the holders' names come only from the paid บอจ.5 (recommend, never order, from the research run); CRM checks use published names only.
- **SDR next-contact themes:** why an ERP now (a customer requirement or audit, or stock and production pain); planned go-live; who in Thailand would be the project contact; what the parent runs and whether it sets the system for Thailand. Then a short online needs survey before any demo.
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA invented a Chinese company name ("泰國托里精密") and guessed Chinese characters for the Thai-script directors' names for a CRM search; offered invented example must-dos (a Tier-1 audit "next Q1", a "2027 H1" start); read a company with ฿99M revenue as being in its "plant-build phase"; suggested opening on "passing car-maker audits" and citing named Chinese OEM supply chains as references (no OEM link evidenced); speculated that the company is a "白手套" set up to avoid tariffs on related-party trade and may only be pricing to satisfy a parent audit (speculation about the customer's tax conduct; never repeated); used a fear-framed daily idle-capacity loss naming Toyota and BYD (unsourced); converted baht to USD without a sourced rate. None adopted. No control-workaround advice was given; the GM call is the SDR's, not the channel head's.

## §103 — ACP record keyed to a SAME-SOUND WRONG registration number (a Bangkok Thai-family tool retailer) while the SDR's report ("北柳还在装修中，预计2027年6月开始投产。还没有ERP") describes a pre-production Chinese-owned casting-machinery company in Chachoengsao (฿202.56M registered, ~฿54M paid in, ฿55M property, no stock, no sales), very likely the first Thai base of a Baoding foundry-equipment group; contact = a non-director 總經理 on the group's mail domain; lead in a colleague's 私田 (NOVA live 2026-10-24 · job e4fa6363 · YONGHONG FANDA INDUSTRIAL (THAILAND) run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Grade:** a plant timeline plus "no ERP" with no project owner, budget or procurement timetable = **D (需求探詢)**, whatever ACP shows (behaviour-sequence rule, same as §78, §92, §101). C1 = the 立案三要項: a dated must-do (the production start, from which go-live is back-calculated), a named project owner or team, a dated selection-to-signature timetable.
- **Wrong number on the record:** the field owner files the correction in ACP/CRM (number, address, capital) after confirming the legal entity with the contact, and links the parent only once confirmed; the research run writes nothing (same as §78 mis-merge, §93 shell).
- **Greenfield timing (NOVA's back-calculation):** go-live in the trial-run period, 2–3 months before production; 5–6 months of implementation; kick-off about 7–8 months before production; signature about 8 months before production. KB §17's 9-month rule gives an earlier date; read both as "this quarter".
- **Two plays by plant type, decided by discovery, not assumed:** (a) the plant ASSEMBLES foundry equipment (ETO/MTO) = BOM and drawing hand-over from HQ, long-lead project purchasing, cost per machine, Thai VAT/WHT (§21 logic, lighter); (b) the plant POURS castings or makes auto parts = heat/lot trace with barcodes, pattern and mould life, melt and scrap reasons (§78 logic). Phase 1 on Workflow ERP (iGP) for a pre-production plant (§17, §54, §101); T100 only if the group demands consolidation or first customers demand process-level trace.
- **Route:** DigiWin China's unit covering the parent's province checks CRM on the parent's published name and, with the field owner's agreement, visits HQ (NOVA named the North China business unit for Hebei; unverified). Follow-ups NOVA asked for: the parent's ERP brand, the contact's urgency, and whether Thailand or HQ approves and pays.
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA rated T100 "highly recommended" for a foundry plant (contradicts §17/§54/§78/§101 for a pre-production plant); put BOI asset management in phase 1 and offered BOI or IATF deadlines as the must-do (neither evidenced); warned that waiting means "manual off-system books, a disaster" (fear framing); supplied Chinese characters for Thai-script directors and stated one director "holds group decision power" from a single 2024 name match (§35 hygiene). None adopted. No control-workaround advice was offered; nothing had the channel head contacting the customer.

## §104 — ACP D whose only daily report says "窗口不肯引荐领导，用的sap", at the RAMPING Thai plant (opened end-2025, volume from July 2026, LEO-satellite and server boards for "non-China" capacity) of a Taiwan-LISTED HDI PCB parent that holds it 100% (฿3.5B invested); the plant's OWN job ad hires four developers to "maintain factory applications (MES, SAP, BPM, Web systems)" with HQ; the parent sits on a 鼎華 iMES PCB customer slide (2020) and an older NOVA answer called it a TIPTOP/T100 account (unverified); lead in a colleague's 私田 (NOVA live 2026-10-24 · job 619e289d · UNITECH PCB (THAILAND) run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Stage:** a gatekeeper who will not refer upward, a named incumbent ERP (SAP), an MES already running, amount 0 and no pain stated = **E (公田 / 長期經營)**, not D; the SDR's D is recorded, not changed. Back to C2 = DigiWin learns who really decides for the Thai plant (the Taiwan-seconded plant head or HQ IT) AND hears that person's own worry about the ramp or Thai-side compliance, through the TW AM or our own senior contact.
- **Route = HQ first ("總部包圍分公司"):** before any Thai-side pitch, DigiWin TW / 鼎華東南亞事業部 checks whether the listed parent is a 鼎新 ERP or 鼎華 iMES account under maintenance and who its TW AM is. If it is: the Thai plant is an executing unit with no say over architecture; DigiWin TH positions as the local delivery partner for HQ's template and never pushes ERP at the Thai contact. If it is not: approach HQ IT or a senior VP through TW relationships (industry association, the 2018 PCB A-Team ties).
- **Edges DigiWin TH may explore once HQ is aligned (questions, not claims):** Thai localisation around SAP (Thai tax documents, withholding tax; BOI records only once a certificate is confirmed); a light WMS + barcode for laminate and chemical lots (FIFO, shelf life) beside SAP; machine data collection (EAP/IoT) for the ramp. All interface wording follows §22.
- **Gatekeeper rule:** never press the contact to introduce the boss. Make him the beneficiary: offer something useful he can carry to his manager himself (e.g. a short note on Thai-side compliance and new-plant IT pitfalls), so DigiWin is his resource, not a threat.
- **Wake-up triggers:** Thai-side hires for a warehouse manager, tax accountant or BOI officer (local operations taking shape); yield or utilisation trouble after volume starts; a further capital increase or phase-2 announcement by the parent; a local IT manager arriving.
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA asserted the Thai SAP and MES are "99%" an HQ template (inference, unsourced); called Thai localisation for a foreign SAP "very expensive and slow" and SAP WMS "too heavy for floor staff" (unverified claims, not to be said); offered BOI filing help although BOI promotion is not confirmed (only a 2024 application is public); described "a mature DigiWin TH localisation add-on for SAP" (needs TH product confirmation before any promise); dated the plant opening to end-2026 (it opened end-2025/January 2026) and suggested watching "2025 build progress" (stale). Its gatekeeper script refers to a "white paper" that does not exist. None of these may be used as fact. No control-workaround, direct-contact or recording advice was offered.

## §105 — ACP D whose only daily report says the plant "is applying for BOI" and that "the current 易飞 version cannot do it" (a DigiWin TH consultant explained BOI handling online), at the year-one Thai plant (~฿63M first sales year, gross LOSS, stock ~254 days of COGS, receivables about equal to a year's sales, interest-free current liabilities, ฿170M paid in) of a PRC optical-cable maker that self-describes as 100% owned by a Shanghai JV whose listed majority owner signed to buy out the foreign partner (closing pending); parent group on a DigiWin China logo wall; contact a non-signatory 經理; 157 days silent; lead in a colleague's 私田 (NOVA live 2026-10-24 · job bc969f4c · SKYLAR OPTICAL FIBRE & CABLE run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Stage:** a need voiced once (BOI) plus the admission that the in-family mainland system cannot meet it, then 157 days without a touch, no signatory reached, no team, no date = **D (需求探詢)**. Back to C2 = a DBD director or the Thai plant head agrees the BOI-to-ERP gap must be solved and accepts a DigiWin walk-through. C1 = the 立案三要項 (a must-do with a date, a named BOI/ERP owner, a go-live target).
- **Play when the Thai plant runs DigiWin China's 易飞 and needs Thai BOI:** NOVA prefers (a) a Thai BOI raw-material layer plus Thai tax localisation BESIDE 易飞, sold and delivered by DigiWin TH, over (b) replacing the Thai entity's ERP with Workflow/iGP (group IT likely to refuse a second ERP brand) or (c) waiting (the plant is ramping now). Delivery split: DigiWin China keeps 易飞 maintenance; DigiWin TH implements the BOI layer fed from 易飞 stock and BOM data. Claude vetting: the data feed follows §22/§99 wording (report formats, upload files, per-project mapping); no live API to 易飞 is promised until TH delivery and DigiWin China confirm one exists.
- **Ask DigiWin China first (three questions):** which ERP the listed group HQ runs and whether it plans to pull subsidiaries onto it after consolidation; whether the Shanghai parent's 易飞 maintenance contract is current and who its AM and IT owner are; whether 易飞 already carries bonded / processing-trade (加工貿易手冊核銷, 保稅倉) functions whose data could feed Thai BOI records.
- **Route:** Peter talks to the field owner first (why 157 days silent; whether a joint 聯營 arrangement suits them); the field owner, not Peter, re-opens with the 經理, e.g. by offering help to the new import/export hire with BOI records.
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA stated the buy-out had CLOSED in July 2026 (only signed; closing pending) and treated the Thai company's self-described parent as fact; used "巨額補稅與罰款" as the must-do and in customer questions (fear-selling, BOI not even granted; not adopted); asserted HQ "has very high expectations" of the Thai plant (invented); offered a "中資出海 BOI 成功案例白皮書" (does not exist); and framed the add-on as a way to "排除集團干預" (positioning a purchase to avoid the customer's group IT governance is NOT adopted: any Thai purchase must be visible to and approved by the parent's IT owner). No recording advice was offered.

## §106 — ACP D with a BLANK daily report at a large SET-LISTED Thai tyre-and-tube plant (~฿2.7B, two plants, 1,814 staff, four BOI certificates, profit down about two thirds since 2021) that is 55-odd per cent owned by a Taiwan-LISTED tyre parent sharing the same chairman, where DigiWin's OWN 鼎華 台灣東南亞事業部 had already drawn a two-plant tyre MES plan (sMES + sAPS + sWMS + sQMS, 50 reporting points, seven months) the year before and nothing followed; the ERP exists but is unnamed; the ACP contact is a 副總 who came back from the parent's production side; lead in a colleague's 私田 (NOVA live 2026-10-24 · job 8f48e3e0 · HWA FONG RUBBER (THAILAND) run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Stage:** blank report + amount 0 + no project team + no date = **D (需求探詢)**, even with a strong profile and an in-family plan on file. C2 = the contact agrees to re-evaluate the earlier MES plan and gives a route to the signatory or the parent; C1 = 立案三要項 (a quantified must-do such as a customer traceability or audit requirement, a named owner or team, a selection date).
- **When another DigiWin unit already proposed:** the first move is internal and within 24 hours: the TW CRM check (is the listed parent a 鼎新 or 鼎華 account, on what system, under maintenance) and a call with the 鼎華 author on why the plan stalled (budget, parent stance, people change). NOVA's split: DigiWin TH (the field owner) keeps the local relationship and leads; 鼎華 is the second-line process expert and product owner; both managers agree credit in writing before anyone approaches the customer (a proposal for TH management, not policy).
- **Product route:** MES/WMS first; if the parent runs 鼎華 MES, extend that template to the Thai plant ("copy the parent's working setup, lowest risk") instead of introducing Thai eMES; ERP only later and only on evidence about the incumbent. Start where the money is in a tyre plant: compound weighing/recipe at mixing and curing-press time and mould life, phased one plant first.
- **Questions for the 副總 (through the field owner):** his comparison of floor reporting and WIP control with the parent's plant; where the earlier MES plan stands inside the plant and whether the chairman's automation plan has a timetable; whether the current ERP can feed the floor data and whether the parent's systems would simply be extended.
- **Risks:** a capex-sensitive board after a profit fall (scope small first); decision chain above the contact (signatory and parent), so plan the call-high through the field owner early.
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA read a fully amortised ฿8.9M software asset as proof the ERP is "extremely old" and "must be replaced" (an amortised cost proves nothing about the system; not adopted) and proposed later "exposing the risk that the old ERP cannot support intelligent decisions" to the chairman (fear framing on an unknown system; not adopted); suggested preparing "the parent's case" and "鼎新/鼎華 tyre success data" (the parent's account status is unverified and no tyre MES case is published; not adopted); addressed "田主（楊慧芳、Peter）" as if the channel head may call the contact (Peter does not contact a colleague's account; not adopted); its "split the project to lower the first-phase signing threshold" is adopted ONLY as a value-first scope choice, never as a way to stay under the customer's approval limits or away from its CFO or parent review; its Decathlon-audit example is a question, not a fact. No recording advice was offered.

## §107 — PRE-CONSTRUCTION Thai plant of a Taiwanese private family OEM (two unlisted sister companies in one Taiwan city, same officers) held ~100 per cent through an offshore company; ACP D whose only daily report is "factory not built yet, wants product information", asked by a Thai director on the parent's generic mailbox; lead in a colleague's 私田 (NOVA live 2026-10-24 · job b2d1cc81 · DA YI INTERNATIONAL run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Stage:** an information request while the plant is unbuilt is **D (需求探詢)**, not C2, even when the asker is a registry signatory and capital has been raised tenfold. Three facts lift it: (a) a dated build and trial-production schedule (machines in, trial run); (b) a named Thai project owner and the Taiwan-Thailand split of the decision; (c) a budget range and a date for the formal system selection. Consistent with §8 (false-C2) and §17 (立案三要項).
- **Who decides:** for a family OEM's first overseas plant the system is chosen at the Taiwan HQ (owner, finance or IT); the Thai side builds and gathers requirements. First move is internal: ask DigiWin TW (the parent's city) whether either Taiwan company is an account, on which product, who leads overseas IT, and whether TW CRM already logs the Thai expansion. Identify the Taiwan companies from the Taiwan registry (GCIS open data: names, officers, shareholdings) and match the Thai directors by transliteration before asking.
- **Product-information stage:** give a framework and history-tense cases, never a specification sheet or a rough price (both get used for comparison and set expectations before the parent's system is known). Sign about **4-6 months before machine installation or trial production** (NOVA live), within §46's 3-6 months; the date to discover is the trial-production month.
- **Phase 1 for an injection-moulded eyewear (or similar consumer-safety) plant:** buy/sell/stock with lot control, BOM and work orders before trial production, finance and fixed assets from the build phase; BOI eMT first and RMTS before trial production only if promoted (§39). NOVA proposed QM in phase 1 for lot traceability; Claude vetting: lot control in stock and work orders covers day one, QC/eMES traceability later (§17 "simplify phase 1").
- **Industry pain priors NOVA gave for injection-moulded safety eyewear (priors, not evidence about any plant):** mould life, maintenance and changeover gate capacity; outsourced hard-coat or anti-fog coating (issue, return, yield, subcontract cost); lot traceability from finished carton back to resin lot; regrind (runner) reuse and its cost in the BOM.
- **Channel head's boundary:** coach the SDR, supply internal intel (TW CRM through the field owner), prepare a generic new-plant deck with presales; never contact the asker directly and never give a price or spec before the parent's situation is known.
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA invented a Chinese name for the Thai company ("大奕"), gave an unsourced "80 per cent of decision power in Taiwan" and an unsourced "100 per cent traceability" industry requirement, framed a trade-flow question around OBU triangular trade (touches the customer's tax structure; not used), and suggested the channel head himself convene a Taiwan-Thailand meeting and have the TW branch visit the parent (a call on a colleague's account; left to the field owner). None adopted.

## §108 — Year-one SMALL operating company (first sales year ~฿26M, gross margin ~17, net loss, negative equity, ~฿2M paid in, ~฿80M interest-free owner loans, receivables ≈ a full year of sales) with a SAME-BRAND ฿100M company registered at the next-door address months later (different director, different holders), Australian 99.99 / Hong Kong 1 holders, ACP D whose only report is "plant not built yet, relationship-building", contact = nickname on a mainland personal mailbox, not a director; lead in a colleague's 私田 (NOVA live 2026-10-24 · job 5801e86c · HENGYUAN SCIENCE AND TECHNOLOGY (THAILAND) run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Grade:** relationship-building with no pain, date, owner or budget = **D (需求探詢)**; if the next contact brings nothing, **E**. C1 needs a quantified must-do with a date (the production start), a named project owner or team, and a timetable plus budget (same as §103, §107).
- **Which company is the deal:** NOVA's instinct is the bigger new company ("the real investment"). Claude vetting: a same brand plus an adjacent address is not a registry link; the field owner first asks the contact (1) whether the two companies are one group, (2) which one will run the new plant and its system, (3) the contact's role in each, and only then re-points or adds the ACP record (§93, §103). The research run writes nothing to ACP.
- **Where decisions sit:** offshore holders (Australia, Hong Kong) plus a Chinese-named director and a mainland personal mailbox point to owners abroad, probably mainland China (inference). Ask the contact for the head office's name first; only then check DigiWin China's CRM, through the field owner. Never guess Chinese characters for Thai-script names to search 企查查/天眼查 or CRM.
- **Phase 1 for a small machinery plant before its new site opens:** Workflow ERP unless a group mandates otherwise; purchasing, sales, stock, AP/AR and simple production (BOM, work orders, subcontracting). Likely first pain: build-phase equipment, hardware and consumables received without reconciliation to the books; ask whether products are standard or made to order (ETO/MTO decides the BOM and design-change scope; §103).
- **Weak balance sheet = payment terms are part of qualification:** deposit-first (NOVA: a higher first payment, project starts only once it is paid) or subscription (consistent with §23). Ask who approves and pays in neutral words ("is a system purchase approved here or planned by head office?"), never by flattering the size of the investment.
- **Channel head's boundary:** coach the SDR's question list, prepare internal checks, never contact the customer, never change ACP status, never give a price or product scope before the decision chain is known.
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA gave an unsourced "over 90 per cent of decision power in China", guessed Chinese characters for both directors, called the small company a possible "paper window" for related-party purchases (one hypothesis; it had real PP&E, stock and cost of sales), proposed that the Hong Kong or mainland parent sign and pay through a three-party contract (touches the customer's cross-border contracting and tax structure; not a sales script), used an invented "car-maker certification" must-do, scripted the Thai build as "very large" (it is ฿100M), and had Peter, addressed as a southern-region head, contact DigiWin China himself. None adopted.

## §109 — Built-but-not-producing Thai plant (registered 2023, ฿100M paid in, PP&E up ~฿258M in 2025, two years of sales BELOW cost, receivables ~2× a year's sales, ~฿330M interest-free current liabilities) of a PRC private EXPORT fuel-pump maker (self-described 77 export, North America ~38) whose 2023 brochure calls Thailand its "third factory" and lists ERP/MES/WMS/PLM as "now doing"; ACP D whose only report is "大陆T100老客，目前还未投产"; contact = a 經理 on the parent's corporate domain, name close to (not proven as) the sole director; lead in a colleague's 私田 (NOVA live 2026-10-24 · job c7a034f3 · ZOREN AUTOMOTIVE ELECTRIC (THAILAND) run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Stage:** D (需求探詢), not C2, while the Thai buying cycle has not started and the report is one line. Lift facts: (1) who decides and who runs the project (Thai director or HQ), (2) a dated mass-production month and ERP go-live, (3) HQ's decision on the Thai plant's system (an organisation on the parent's T100 or a separate system) and who pays.
- **Sales before "production":** NOVA reads gross-loss sales at a plant the SDR calls not yet producing as resale of parent-made goods before local output (transfer prices, freight, duty); trial output is the other reading. Ask the contact neutrally; never quote the filing at him.
- **Architecture with a T100 parent:** path A = a Thai organisation on the parent's T100 plus DigiWin TH's Thai localisation and local consulting (same logic as §43, §61, §76); path B = a lighter local system feeding the parent, only if HQ judges T100 too heavy. Phase 1 on path A: purchasing, sales, stock, work orders, Thai AP/AR/GL with VAT, quality and lot traceability; MES and WMS in phase 2 once the line is stable.
- **Route:** confirm what the SDR's "T老客" meant, then the field owner has DigiWin China check the parent (product, maintenance, AM, any Thai-plant project, other vendors). The channel head supplies Thai localisation material and briefs through the field owner; no quote before the architecture is known; nobody goes around the China account owner to HQ.
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA guessed Chinese characters for the director, supplied a PRC credit code no public source confirmed, gave an unsourced "90 per cent of IT decisions at HQ", estimated mass production for 2027 Q1–Q2 with "3 to 6 months of customer certification", asserted a Thai tax-filing problem and an IATF mandate for North American exports (neither evidenced; the parent's US consignees look like aftermarket buyers), and had Peter convene a co-selling meeting with DigiWin China and settle the credit split himself. None adopted.

## §110 — Ramping Thai plant of a TAIWAN-LISTED (KY) parent whose operating company is in mainland China, set up "at the customer's request" to ship lamp assemblies to a US OEM (GM) under customer certification, ERP brand unknown at group level (the mainland plant shows an SMT MES, vendor unnamed); ACP D whose only report is an event invitation "有回复未参加"; contact = a Thai nickname on the mainland plant's mail domain; lead in a colleague's 私田 (NOVA live 2026-10-24 · job a539e7ba · E-LAN MOTOR ELECTRONIC (THAILAND) run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Stage:** D (需求探詢). A reply to an invitation without attending is not buying behaviour (same 行為序判定 as §77, §87). Lift facts: (1) the contact's real role and where the Thai plant's system decision sits (Taiwan listed parent, mainland operating company, or the Thai plant); (2) a must-do the customer states in his own words (customer-audit traceability, stock and material records breaking under the ramp); (3) a named project owner and a go-live timetable.
