- **CRM check, both sides:** the field owner asks DigiWin China (the office covering the mainland operating company's city) AND DigiWin TW (listed parent) to search the mainland operating company, the listed parent and its officers: customer or not, which ERP/MES, who owns IT, any Thai-plant plan. Neither is approached by the channel head himself.
- **Plays by group system (same shape as §86/§87):** DigiWin = extension (group reporting, shared master data, Thai localisation and local service by DigiWin TH); Kingdee/Yonyou = Thai localisation and local service first, data back to HQ (do not claim they lack a Thai team, §60); SAP/Oracle = two-tier, Thai operations feeding the parent's ledger.
- **Phase split NOVA proposed for a customer-certified auto-lighting ramp:** phase 1 = Thai tax localisation (BOI only if a certificate exists), lot traceability with barcode, purchasing/sales/stock; phase 2 = actual cost per part, MES link to the mainland SMT MES. Claude vetting: work-order costing basics sit in any ERP phase 1 (§17); the split is NOVA's, not policy.
- **Timing:** NOVA reads the first shipping quarters of a ramp as the pain peak, not a missed window; if the plant already runs the parent's system, the entry is a Thai localisation or barcode add-on, never an attack; if it runs Thai accounting software, the upgrade path (§2). Both are inferences until the customer confirms.
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA asserted that Kingdee/Yonyou are "extremely weak" on Thai support (contradicts §60), that SAP is "extremely costly" in Thailand and "買錯比買貴更貴" (unsourced slogans), that simple accounting software "will certainly fail" the OEM's ongoing supplier audits (fear-selling, unsourced), a "10×" capacity jump (an FX-dependent comparison it took from our own packet; not for customer use), an IATF audit at the Thai plant (only the parent's IATF is evidenced), proposed a "how to pass GM audits" white paper as bait (no such asset verified), had the channel head coordinate DigiWin China's office managers and a cross-border revenue split himself, and had him front the parent's spokesperson as an executive sponsor. None adopted: internal routes run through the field owner; no control-workaround advice was offered.

## §111 — Large CAPTIVE cost-plus Thai plant (first sales year ~฿1.57B, gross margin ~0.14, profit from other income, ~฿1.37B group liabilities, receivables ~119 days) of a PRC-LISTED appliance ODM that moved all US-bound output to Thailand in 2025; the parent's exchange filing names the Thai company as a site of its raised-fund "smart digital-factory upgrade" project; the Thai accountant's job ad asks for ERP (cash/AP/AR/FA) experience and work "with the Chinese finance team" while an outside firm keeps the Thai books; ACP D whose only report is "T100老客，预约拜访但负责人不在泰国"; contact = a data-list 廠長, not a director; lead in a colleague's 私田 (NOVA live 2026-10-24 · job 28164b94 · KINGCLEAN ELECTRIC (THAILAND) run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Stage:** a booked visit with no real conversation and no stated need = **E (公田/長期經營)**, even with an installed-base note and an ACP D. C2 = the plant manager or the finance lead says the Thai plant has a problem (Thai tax and the group close, or floor traceability) and agrees to talk; C1 = the 立案三要項 (quantified must-do, named Thai project owner, dated evaluation and go-live). Same logic as §88.
- **Reading "T100老客" at a PRC-listed group:** most likely the HQ runs T100 (or TopGP) and the Thai plant is, or will be, a site in it. The field owner checks with DigiWin China's account manager for the group: system and version, whether Thai-site licences exist, who owns overseas-plant IT planning (HQ IT or the plant), and whether HQ IT would introduce the Thai team.
- **Positioning when the parent has written the Thai plant into its own digital-factory programme:** DigiWin TH as "HQ IT's extended hands in Thailand", helping HQ land its own specification (T100, MES) with Thai staff and Thai tax, protecting HQ IT's work; never "your system does not fit Thailand". Sell order: Thai finance and tax localisation with consulting, then SMT/assembly floor data (barcode traceability, mistake-proofing) and WMS. PGI-type sister plants in another industry: share intelligence, sell as a separate project.
- **First visit while the plant manager is abroad:** the Chinese finance manager (two-country close, Thai VAT/WHT) or the SMT/quality lead (audits, traceability); neither signs, so the visit is for pain and facts. Never mention the plant's margin or transfer pricing; never say the group system does not fit; never claim an arrangement with the absent manager.
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA used the parent's raised-fund completion date (2025-12, already past) as a C1 must-do; asserted IATF 16949/QML audit pressure from the US appliance brand and scripted a question naming the brand and an "upcoming IATF certification" (IATF is automotive; the posting only lists it; the brand name comes from customs data and stays ours); called Thai tax plus BOI a "剛需" (BOI unconfirmed, no need stated); and told the field owner to bring the DBD and filing intelligence to the visit (filings are never quoted at the customer, §43). None adopted. No control-workaround advice was offered, and nothing had the channel head contacting the customer or DigiWin China himself.

## §112 — JUST-BUILT Thai site (registered 2024, ฿80M capital, ~฿30M paid in, PP&E only ~฿1.6M and no stock at the FY2025 close, pre-revenue) of a TAIWANESE private die-and-mould STANDARD-COMPONENTS maker (catalogue of 8,000+ guide pillars, bushings, ejector pins, punches; main production in Suzhou; Taiwan HQ), Taiwanese holders 100, sole director; ACP D whose only report is "新厂刚建好，正在预约拜访"; contact on the Thai brand domain, no title, not a director; lead in a colleague's 私田 (NOVA live 2026-10-24 · job 44e403d0 · TAIMON TECHNOLOGY PRECISION (THAILAND) run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Stage:** a visit being booked with no conversation yet = **D (需求探詢)**: not E because a new site is an explicit build event, not C2 because no power holder has been reached and nothing is shared. Lift to C1 = the 立案三要項: a dated operating start (first Thai invoice or first shipment) with the reason it must run on a system; a named Thai project owner plus the Taiwan or Suzhou IT/finance person who co-evaluates; a go-live date and a budget range. Contrast §111 (a booked visit at a large operating T100 plant read E): the difference NOVA draws is the build event.
- **Site role is the first unknown:** ฿1.6M of PP&E buys desks, PCs and racking, not grinders, CNC or heat-treatment furnaces, so the first phase is quite possibly a warehouse, light-processing and sales hub fed from the group's China plant (NOVA's inference). Claude vetting: the FY2025 balance sheet predates the "plant just built" report by nine months, so 2026 equipment would not show; ask, do not assume.
- **Product:** Workflow ERP (iGP) for a small new site; T100 only if the group demands it. Industry priors NOVA gave for die-and-mould standard parts (priors, not evidence about any plant): item-master explosion (catalogue sizes times customer variants; slow, error-prone coding and quoting); frequent outsourced heat treatment and surface finishing (where the parts are, processing-fee matching, return quality and dates); rush "fire-fighting" orders when a customer's die breaks, so safety stock must cover next-day delivery without dead stock.
- **First visit (field owner):** confirm (1) the site's role now and any later phase or BOI plan, (2) whether the system decision sits with the Thai director or with the Taiwan/Suzhou parent, (3) what runs today (Excel, or remote use of the parent's system). Bring dated, history-tense cases and the Thai trilingual service story; no price, no specification.
- **Route:** the field owner asks DigiWin TW (Taiwan parent) and DigiWin China (Suzhou plant) whether either is an account, on which product and version, maintenance status and AM.
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA had the channel head coordinate the TW/China CRM checks himself and "Call High" alongside the field owner (Peter does not work a colleague's account or contact DigiWin China directly; both go through the field owner); framed the must-do as "without a system, customs filing, consolidation or shipping collapse" and asked the customer to price "penalties and lost trust" (fear framing); quoted a "98 per cent on-time delivery" from unnamed Taiwanese parts makers (unsourced); called 易飛 a CRM; offered "2027 Q1" as a sample date. None adopted. Its competitor list for a Taiwanese SME (copy the Suzhou system, 正航, SAP B1, Express) is a prior to test, not evidence. No control-workaround or customer-recording advice was offered.

## §113 — BUILT-and-stocking Thai GREENFIELD (registered 2024, ฿202M capital ~94 per cent paid in, PP&E ~฿200M and the first ~฿0.7M of stock at the FY2025 close, pre-revenue) of an UNIDENTIFIED group of 12 Taiwanese holders (founder-named, two sole-signing directors, no website, no posts, no parent found) making car, motorcycle and exercise-equipment metal parts; ACP D whose only report is "预约10月拜访" with no visit logged 25 days later; contact on a personal Taiwanese ISP mailbox, no title, not a director; lead in a colleague's 私田 (NOVA live 2026-10-24 · job 0ccc48e9 · CHANG YONG TECHNOLOGY run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Stage:** a booked-but-unlogged visit with nothing known about pain or decision chain = **D (需求探詢)**; never C2 before the visit. Lift to C1 = a dated operating start with the reason it must run on a system, a named Thai project owner, and a go-live month (consistent with §8, §17, §107, §112).
- **Product:** Workflow ERP first for an S-size Taiwanese plant with no visible group IT; T100 only if the owners demand consolidation. Implementation takes about 4–6 months, so at a built-and-stocking plant a system started now will most likely go live after trial output; the visit is still the right entry point.
- **Industry priors NOVA gave for vehicle and fitness-equipment metal parts (priors, not evidence about any plant):** multi-level BOMs (tube/sheet → stamping, bending, welding → assembly) with frequent engineering change in a new plant; outsourced plating and painting (issue, return, loss, WIP reconciliation); vehicle customers' lot-traceability, label and EDI demands; high-mix fitness orders beside volume vehicle parts causing frequent changeovers (on Workflow the answer is LRP plus exception reports; APS is T100/Dinghua tier only).
- **First visit (field owner):** confirm (1) the decision chain (are the directors resident in Thailand, the contact's real role, who leads the build, who approves the system), (2) time pressure (mass-production month, sample orders, customer audit dates), (3) budget autonomy (Thai budget or the Taiwanese owners' decision). Bring dated, history-tense parts-maker cases and a new-plant IT checklist; no price, no specification.
- **Route when the parent is unknown:** get the Taiwanese company's name or the directors' Chinese characters from the customer first, then the field owner asks DigiWin TW whether it is an account. Do not search CRM on guessed characters or on a consumer mail domain.
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA framed the must-do as "without a system the plant cannot pass big-customer audits and cannot ship" and "suspension or heavy fines" (fear framing); guessed Chinese characters for both directors and proposed CRM searches on them and on the contact's consumer ISP domain; guessed the owners come from central Taiwan's metal-parts cluster and named two Taiwanese fitness brands as likely customers (unsourced); proposed unverified "Thai stamping and metal-parts references"; and had the channel head use his position to drive the Taiwan-Thailand CRM alignment and secure Taiwanese consultants himself (goes through the field owner). None adopted. No control-workaround or customer-recording advice was offered.

## §114 — ACP D whose only daily report is "陌拜开发" (a cold visit, no contact named), at a RAMPING Thai sheet-metal plant (server chassis now, painted racks from the next year; ฿1.3B registered, ฿900M paid in, pre-revenue in its filings, small-volume output started) held 100% by a TAIWAN-LISTED server-chassis and rack maker whose ERP brand is not public (annual report, sustainability report and job ads all silent); ACP industry and region mis-filed; lead in a colleague's 私田 (NOVA live 2026-10-25 · job 938079ff · CHENMING ELECTRONIC TECHNOLOGY (THAILAND) run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Account status:** NOVA said plainly it does not know whether the listed parent or its China plants are DigiWin accounts. Treat that as the first internal question for DigiWin TW through the field owner, not as a negative.
- **Grade:** a single cold visit with no named contact, pain or intent = **E (長期經營)**, below the SDR's D (recorded, not changed). C2 needs all three: a management-level contact at the Thai plant (finance, plant head or IT), a stated operating pain from that person, and their willingness to look at a solution (consistent with §8, §17, §107).
- **First cut for a sheet-metal chassis/rack plant ("雙軌"):** door opener = Thai localisation (VAT, withholding tax, e-Tax documents) plus BOI records only once a certificate is confirmed; value expander = barcode traceability per rack (sheet lot, paint batch, key-part serials) timed with the paint line and rack assembly. Both framed as complements to whatever HQ runs, never a fight with the parent's ERP.
- **Channel head's boundary:** coach the field owner's questions and supply internal intel; never contact the customer; the parent's system and the Thai decision chain come first.
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA asserted the plant "100% 享有 BOI" (unconfirmed), used fear framing ("天天面臨罰款風險", "無法通過美系大廠驗廠，拿不到訂單"), proposed a customer question that quotes the registered capital back (KB §43), suggested Peter supply a 《美系驗廠 MES 檢核表》 (no such document known), offered case packaging with invented percentages (80 per cent less reconciliation time, 100 per cent audit pass), named Amazon as an end customer (unsourced), and had the SDR approach the Thai finance or IT lead directly by LinkedIn or phone to "避開總部 IT 的排斥感" (works around the customer's group IT governance; the route stays HQ-first and with HQ IT's knowledge). None adopted. No customer-recording advice was offered.

## §115 — PRE-BUILD Thai company (registered 2025, ฿40M → ฿50M capital, ฿10M paid in, NO PP&E, stock or sales at the FY2025 close, ~฿10.6M interest-free current liabilities) of four Taiwanese holders (three directors share one surname), registry objective electronic parts for computers, audio and appliances but first filing coded fabricated metal products; ACP D whose only report is "电话开发，十月去拜访" with no visit logged 26 days later; the contact matches a sole-signing director by name but uses a mailbox on an unrelated-looking mainland group's domain; lead in a colleague's 私田 (NOVA live 2026-10-25 · job 810667d0 · HUA YA TECH (THAILAND) run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Grade:** phone prospecting plus a planned visit, with no needs conversation, = **E** in NOVA's live reading (below the SDR's D; recorded, not changed). This is one notch stricter than §112/§113 (booked visit at a BUILT site = D): with no machine on the books there is not yet a build event to anchor D. Lift facts: dated build milestones (machines in, trial run, mass production), a named power person in Thailand, and a system line in the build budget with an evaluator.
- **Product and phasing:** Workflow ERP for a ฿50M pre-revenue plant; T100 only if an owning group demands consolidation. For electronic/appliance METAL STAMPING (priors, not evidence about any plant): BEFORE trial production = stock-to-book consistency with customer lot traceability, and outsourced plating/painting control (issue, stock, WIP); AFTER mass production = die life and coil yield (phase 2, cost optimisation).
- **Signing rule (NOVA live):** sign about **five months before mass production or the first customer audit** (Workflow ~3–4 months to implement plus ~1 month parallel run). Consistent with §107 (4–6 months before trial production) and inside §17's 6–9 months before go-live.
- **First visit:** build milestones; whether Thailand decides alone or must link to a parent's system (and what it runs); who has power in Thailand (is a family member resident). Bring a generic new-plant IT checklist and a de-identified stamping story; no price, no specification. Ask what the build team uses today for money and build spend.
- **Contact identity method (Claude, this run):** the ACP 聯絡人 record can carry the contact's Chinese name; matching it syllable by syllable against the DBD Thai-script director names (e.g. 秦素華 = ฉิน สู หัว) identifies a signatory without guessing characters. A mailbox on another company's domain is ONE signal toward a group link and never proves it; DBD nationality of the holders shows whether that company can be a direct shareholder.
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA used fear framing ("without a system it is very hard to pass a big customer's audit, which decides orders"), embellished a published case in its suggested script (claimed the reference was helped to BUILD its plant and "passed a Japanese audit at the first attempt"; neither is on the case page), guessed Chinese characters for three directors, proposed an SDR opener promising a "group cooperation discount" to draw out the parent's name (a price promise before scope), and proposed cross-strait CRM fuzzy searches on guessed names and a surname by TW/China CRM administrators (goes through the field owner, on names the customer gives). None adopted. Its suggestion that the channel head role-play the visit with the SDR is optional coaching within the boundary. No control-workaround or customer-recording advice was offered.

## §116 — Profitable 16-year-old Thai SHOE-MOULD job shop (~฿85M FY2025, +35, net margin ~13, gross ~24), Thai 51 / Chinese-nationality 49 with Taiwanese-looking director romanisation, receivables ~260 days (81 of assets) carried by ~฿26M INTEREST-FREE long-term liabilities, cash ~฿2.7M, near-zero PP&E for four years then ~฿7.8M of first machines in FY2025; ACP D whose only daily report is BLANK; contact = a probable director on personal webmail; no web footprint; lead in a colleague's 私田 (NOVA live 2026-10-25 · job 1691a935 · HOMDA MOLD MANUFACTURING run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Stage:** blank report + amount 0 + new pass = **E** (no customer behaviour yet). C2 = a real talk with the director-contact in which he names a management problem (cost after an expansion, lead times) and agrees to meet DigiWin; C1 = 立案三要項 (quantified must-do, named window, committed date).
- **Product fit:** shoe-mould shops are classic MTO/ETO job shops (one mould set per style and size run; CNC, EDM, texturing and sandblasting often outsourced): Workflow ERP (iGP) order, work-order, job-cost and subcontract functions are "very suitable". **Never promise:** automatic sync from 3D design changes to production (PLM/CAD integration; ERP holds the BOM/ECN flow); fully automatic scheduling (mould scheduling is too dynamic; ERP tracks progress); shorter receivable days (terms are commercial; the system gives overdue alerts and reconciliation).
- **Long receivables + interest-free owner money:** a payment-design risk (milestone billing; look at who stands behind the company) and a question, not a pitch. Ask softly about the buyers' terms and whether finance can separate in-terms from overdue money (KB §12 mechanics). KB §12's "long DSO alone = C2-candidate" is weaker when the gap is visibly owner-funded: read it as structure until the visit shows pain (Claude's ruling this run).
- **First-visit checks (by the SDR):** the director-contact's real role and attitude to systems; what the new machines are and what hurts most now (lead time, subcontract reconciliation or unknown job cost); what tools they use today (Excel or a Thai package). No early demo without a diagnosed need; use the seven-step first visit.
- **Channel head's boundary:** internal analysis and call-high planning through the field owner only; never bypass the field owner, never promise price, free customisation or consultant days.
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA built a C1 example on "depreciation and loss pressure" from new machines at a profitable company (fear framing; not adopted); phrased its buyer-terms opener as a statement about big footwear groups (kept only as an open question); named large footwear groups as typical buyers and four footwear-sector customers as references that are not in the published case mirror (identity and currency unchecked; not used); and suggested the channel head coordinate Taiwan or Vietnam experts across regions (goes through the field owner, not adopted). No control-workaround or customer-recording advice was offered.

## §117 — RAMPING two-plant Thai subsidiary (฿5.67B registered, ฿4.58B paid in, pre-revenue in its last filing but both plants running since 2025, mass production 2026-07, US$234M phase II approved for 2026–2028) of a TWSE-LISTED HON HAI group semiconductor-equipment parts maker (chambers, valves, modules for etch, thin-film, CMP; ISO 27001 at the parent), parent ERP brand not public; BOI confirmed; ACP D whose only report is a cold visit that "found the factory not yet built" (stale); ACP industry mis-filed; lead in a colleague's 私田 (NOVA live 2026-10-25 · job 4fb05c7d · UNIEQ INTEGRATED TECHNOLOGY run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Grade:** keep **D** when one contact sits on DigiWin event lists, but drop to **E** if the next contact finds no pain. C2 needs: a named Thai project owner (plant head, IT or finance), a stated must-fix pain (ramp or customer-audit traceability, BOI records), and that person's willingness to hear a proposal (consistent with §8, §114).
- **Account status:** NOVA does not know whether the listed parent or its China plants are DigiWin accounts; its "core ERP very likely SAP" is a guess. First internal question goes through the field owner's manager: CRM check in TW and China on registry or filing names only, and the multinational or Hon Hai/FII key-account route for what the group runs and who group IT is.
- **Entry for a group-system plant that is already live:** never fight the group ERP. Complement order: BOI eMT (machinery, phase II wave) and RMTS, Thai tax layer only if the group's system lacks it; then semiconductor MES (鼎華) or WMS where group MES does not reach the Thai site. Ramp-phase pains for semiconductor-equipment parts (use as discovery prompts): two-way genealogy material → machining → welding → surface treatment → cleanroom assembly incl. CMM data; copy-exact change control of BOM, routing and tooling with an audit trail; BOI duty-free quota and eMT/RMTS records; mistake-proofing for new staff on high-mix, low-volume work.
- **DigiWin-FII link (FII, a Hon Hai company, is DigiWin's largest shareholder):** a trust fact, not a lever; never imply privileged access to group IT.
- **Channel head's boundary:** no contact with the parent, the customer or DigiWin TW/China by Peter; no promises, price or free POC before a quantified pain and a decision path.
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA's sample line promised "與集團 SAP 完美對接" and large integration savings (group ERP unconfirmed; interfaces per §22), called SAP's BOI support "既慢又貴" (unsourced), used fear framing (停工, 高額補稅), built a question on a stale "qualification before year-end" premise the parent's own deck had overtaken (mass production 2026-07), proposed a CRM search on a guessed entity name, recommended a BOI guide handout that is not known to exist, and had Peter coordinate TW/China key-account teams and join visits himself. None adopted. No control-workaround or customer-recording advice was offered.

## §118 — RAMPING Thai plastic-injection plant (registered 2023, ฿700M registered, ฿500M paid in by FY2025, first sales year ~฿87M at ~26 gross margin and an ~฿11M net loss with ~฿10M interest, PP&E ~฿895M, IATF 16949 + ISO 13485 per the parent's call summary) held 100% through a Samoa company by a TAIWAN-OTC moulding group the SDR calls an in-family T100 account, with a brand-new SAME-ADDRESS sister for the parent's freshly acquired US metal-stamping group (registered 2026-09, shared Thai director, land purchase authorised); ACP D whose only report says "陌拜进不去, 厂长是许总的朋友, 产值不是很好, 今年没有机会推财税包"; lead in a colleague's 私田 (NOVA live 2026-10-25 · job eb6ea25f · DONPON PRECISION (THAILAND) run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Grade:** a report that says "no chance this year" with no IT evaluation, a cold visit refused and a name-only contact = **E (公田/長期經營)**, below the SDR's D, even for an in-family group. C2 needs the plant manager or the parent's IT/finance owner to admit a concrete bottleneck (two Thai entities on one site, or audit traceability) and agree to hear DigiWin. C1 needs the three filing items. Consistent with §58 (freeze → wait) and §90 (vague ask, unverified contact → D at best).
- **Architecture is a hypothesis to test, not a fact:** NOVA's prior here is that the Thai plant keeps its books on a local package or an accounting firm, decoupled from the parent's T100; §43/§58 give the other branch (a Thai organisation inside the parent's instance). Three checks before any 財稅包 talk: BOI status (none found publicly), who keeps the Thai books (in-house or outsourced), and the parent's month-end close deadline for the Thai entity.
- **A second legal entity on the same site is the live trigger:** a sister company for metal stamping beside an injection plant raises multi-entity transactions, cross-process (stamping + moulding) coordination and a second set of Thai books. Wake-up signals: the sister's land purchase and plant build approved (may already have fired), the first Western automotive or medical new model in trial at the plant, a tax or customer audit.
- **Route (unchanged from §58/§90):** the DigiWin TW account manager for the parent calls high on the parent's IT/CFO, using the group's own e-signing history as history, not as a present-tense reference; the Thai sales leader uses his personal tie to the plant manager for an informal, no-product conversation about passing automotive and medical customer audits. The channel head supplies research, verified Thai references and presales planning through the field owner; no direct contact, no custom or discount promise.
- **First cut beyond the tax pack (discovery prompts):** PPAP and lot traceability for IATF/ISO 13485 audits; mould life, maintenance history and shot counts; machine cycle time, drying time and first-pass yield (eMES phase 2).
- **Never promise:** that the parent's e-signing platform covers Thai tax filing and Thai-language forms seamlessly with no consulting or customisation; full-Chinese on-site consulting in Thailand with no expatriate fees.
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA framed BOI tax-exemption filing and BOI audits as a compliance clock (BOI not found for this plant; kept only as a question); asserted that a two-entity site "已超出當地小系統的負荷極限" (no evidence; not used); named Express as the likely local package (a prior, not evidence); and offered "Thai auto/medical Taiwanese moulder cases" without names (any case must be verified and reference-approved first). No control-workaround or customer-recording advice was offered.

## §119 — JUST-OPENED Thai precision-machining GREENFIELD (registered 2023-12, ฿620M registered in ten steps, ฿500M paid in by FY2025, PP&E ~฿416M, pre-revenue, no debt; park opened 2026-10-20 after trial output from Q3, the group's single largest site, quality system "being certified") held 100% by a TWSE-LISTED machining group (auto safety/drivetrain, industrial instruments, semiconductor equipment, AI liquid-cooling parts, aerospace) that the SDR calls a Taiwan T100 account, with a SAME-VILLAGE aviation sister (same two directors = the parent's chairman and founder-president) holding only land for a 2027 casting line; ACP D whose two identical reports say "陌拜認識財務經理，在台灣是T100老客。目前工廠準備竣工.長期追蹤"; contact = a Thai given name on the group's mail domain; lead in a colleague's 私田 (NOVA live 2026-10-25 · job 477b0ea4 · GLOBAL TEK GROUP (THAI) run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Grade:** a cold visit that reached the finance manager, an in-family parent and an opened plant = **D (需求探詢)**, not E (contact exists) and not C (no project team, no system timeline). C2 needs a decision-maker reached through DigiWin TW (the parent's IT head, CFO or the Thai plant GM) and an answer to who owns the Thai ERP decision (Taiwan or Thailand). C1 needs the three filing items (quantified must-do reason such as a dated certification audit, a named PM, a YYYY-MM go-live with HQ budget).
- **Architecture prior:** a T100 group with plants in Taiwan and China most likely extends its T100 multi-organisation set-up to Thailand rather than letting Thailand run a small local system (group consolidation and supply-chain coordination). Three checks: HQ IT policy (one global T100? hosting), Thai tax and any BOI obligations (verify BOI first; do not assume it), and the intercompany pattern with Taiwan and China (transfers, triangular trade). A second Thai entity with the same directors pulls multi-company accounting to day one (capital calls, shared costs).
- **Route:** the Thai sales leader asks the DigiWin TW AM for the parent's T100 status and customisation list and HQ's plan for Thailand; the TW AM calls high on HQ IT/CFO; the Thai SDR keeps the finance manager warm on Thai filing and local service. The channel head supplies presales planning and Thai material through the field owner only.
- **First cut beyond the tax pack:** lot and serial traceability for IATF 16949 / AS9100 (material lots, machining, heat treatment, FAI/PPAP records); the incoming Thai quality manager is the natural supporter. Acceleration signals: that QA manager in post, a customer trial-run contract or audit date, the casting line starting early.
- **Never promise:** that the system guarantees IATF/AS9100 certification; that the parent's T100 customisations move to Thailand intact and free.
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA asserted the Thai plant "enjoys BOI" and named BOI duty-free equipment and a bonded warehouse as a rigid pain (no BOI found publicly; kept only as a question); assumed land-asset transfers between the two Thai companies (not evidenced); told Peter to convene a TW-AM/Thai-team alignment meeting himself and to settle the revenue split (not adopted: the field owner engages DigiWin TW; Peter does not contact TW or a colleague's account directly). No control-workaround or customer-recording advice was offered.

## §120 — Ramping Thai audio plant (registered 2023-09, ฿780.99M registered since 2025-02, ~฿237M paid in by FY2025, PP&E ~฿624M, first sales year ~฿25.7M at ~2.7 gross margin and a ~฿22.5M loss, no interest, current ratio ~0.26 on group balances, stock ~333 days of COGS) held 99 from Singapore by what is very likely a PRC PRIVATE pro/car/home audio OEM (Ningbo, IATF 16949, US musical-instrument retail and car-audio brand buyers), parent ERP brand not public; ACP D whose only report is an event invitation "邀请参加活动，未回复"; contact = a non-director 總經理 on the parent's mail domain; lead in a colleague's 私田 (NOVA live 2026-10-25 · job 461a699a · TONWEL AUDIO (THAILAND) run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Grade:** an unanswered event invitation is not buying-cycle behaviour = **E (公田/長期經營)**, one notch below §110 (where the contact at least replied). C2 needs: a first real conversation with the Thai GM or plant manager in which he agrees to discuss the Thai plant's system plan; a pain the customer admits (stock, multi-workshop control, Thai tax); and a known decision path (Thai plant or Ningbo HQ).
- **Architecture prior (NOVA, unverified):** at this stage the plant most likely runs on parent-sent cadres plus a Thai accounting firm for the books and tax, with Excel or a simple stock tool on the floor; the parent has not yet copied its own system to Thailand. Three checks: who holds the Thai system decision (HQ IT or the Thai GM), what the parent runs (SAP, Oracle, DigiWin or other: extension versus new purchase), and whether a BOI certificate exists (do not assume one).
- **Route:** the field owner first asks DigiWin China to check whether the Ningbo parent is a customer; if it is, the China AM introduces the Thai GM or IT from HQ (the fastest door). SDR switches from generic event invitations to a topic the GM cares about (setting up a Chinese plant in Thailand: Thai finance and tax compliance, stock control). The channel head supplies verified Thai references and internal coaching through the field owner and never contacts the customer.
- **First cut:** Thai tax compliance plus basic BOM and stock control (first-year volume is too small for MES or scheduling talk); BOI and duty modules only if a certificate exists. Restart signals: JobThai posts for production manager, PC/MC, QC, IT or finance head; phase-2 (drivers and hardware workshops) groundbreaking or equipment move-in; HQ starting an IT evaluation for Thailand.
- **Never promise:** changing core processes in Thailand without the parent IT's agreement; a 100 per cent seamless link to the parent's unnamed in-house data-collection system; very short go-lives or rock-bottom package prices for a cross-border project.
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA read the plant as "一期剛奠基、二期未建" (wrong: ground was broken 2024-12, PP&E reached ~฿624M and first sales were booked in FY2025, so phase 1 is built and producing at low volume); proposed sending a "white paper" (no such asset verified; only verified, reference-approved cases may be used); suggested the channel head line up Thai BOI consultants and accountants (not adopted beyond internal reference material through the field owner); tied duty and tax modules to BOI as if BOI existed (kept as a question). No control-workaround or customer-recording advice was offered.

## §121 — ACP D whose only daily report is "台湾老客，建联中" (existing Taiwan customer, relationship still being built; contact unnamed, amount 0) at the RAMPING Thai HDI plant of a Taiwan-LISTED PCB/IC-substrate giant (registered 2023, ฿10.36B registered, BOI-promoted, first sales year ~฿328M at ~5 gross margin with a ~฿563M loss, land for up to five plants); the plant's own job ads show ERP, MES, BPM and HR systems maintained by one local engineer (vendors unnamed); the group sits on a 鼎華 APS PCB logo wall with a "跨厂代工排程&新开厂外包" rule; lead in a colleague's 私田 (NOVA live 2026-10-25 · job 9ed77952 · UNIMICRON (THAILAND) run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Stage:** "D 級 (需求探詢) 偏向 E 級 (長期經營)" — no named contact, amount 0 and no stated pain is not a working opportunity, whatever ACP says. C2 needs all three: the key roles identified (Thai plant IT and finance heads, the HQ IT owner); a first pain confirmed (cross-border management or Thai tax output from the current systems); executive intent raised (HQ or the Thai head sees the current set-up will not carry the multi-plant build-out).
- **Reading "台湾老客":** NOVA's inference is that the Taiwan HQ or specific plants are 鼎華 APS/MES maintenance customers rather than 鼎新 ERP customers; NOVA cannot query the CRM. Claude vetting: an SDR shorthand is a lead, not evidence (same as §76); the field owner must say which company and product she meant.
- **Entry order for a ramping, loss-making, HQ-run Thai plant (hypotheses to test through HQ, not claims):** (1) extend the group's 鼎華 APS to the Thai plant for cross-plant loading, but only if HQ is confirmed live on it (T100/鼎華 tier; never promise auto-scheduling on iGP); (2) Thai-side peripheral localisation (tax output, approval flows) around whatever ERP HQ chose; (3) WMS and barcode for laminate and chemical lots. Never start by challenging the plant's existing in-house .NET/SQL systems.
- **Route:** "台灣攻頂（鼎華 + 台灣 AM），泰國在地交付" — Taiwan calls high at HQ on group-wide scheduling standards; the Thai side supplies local tax know-how, floor-pain discovery and the IT-head relationship. Claude vetting: carried through the field owner's line; Peter does not contact DigiWin TW or the colleague's account himself.
- **Wake-up signals:** a new high-end customer qualification (server, next-generation console) that loads the plant; changes in the plant's IT postings (HQ systems rolling in); gross margin turning positive (local discretionary budget appears).
- **Correction to §72:** 欣興 is NOT only a 2018 A-Team alliance member in our corpus: Monk card b961221db7 (鼎華 APS deck, undated) shows "欣兴集团 (Unimicron)" on the PCB APS logo wall with three contributed rules. Its currency is unknown (case-currency rule).
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA asserted the HQ and German core ERP are "預期為 SAP" (only Unimicron Germany is evidenced, 2019); called SAP's Thai local tax "非常難用且調整成本高" and said DigiWin TH's local tax and BPM "可完美與 SAP 介接" (unverified product and interface claims; the Thai plant's ERP is not known to be SAP; interface wording follows §22); scripted an HQ question that presents 鼎華 APS as currently "非常高效" in use (case-currency rule); premised a Thai question on e-tax rules changing "頻繁" (unverified); and offered a pain translation with "OEE 損失達 XX%" and "數千萬泰銖" stock (placeholders, no evidence). Its internal next step had the researcher look up and brief the Taiwan AM directly (not adopted; routed through the field owner). None may be used as fact. No control-workaround or customer-recording advice was offered.

## §122 — ACP D whose only daily report is "日常建联" (routine relationship building; contact = a sole-signing director from a data list, amount 0) at a small PROFITABLE Thai PVD vacuum-plating company (year two ~฿68M, ×3.65, gross ~48, net ~20; stock ~229 days of COGS, receivables ~153 days, ~฿20M new interest-free long-term money), 100 Taiwanese-held, registered at the SAME ADDRESS as its group's loss-making assembly plant (shared director, the plant already on an outside ERP/MES with an MES go-live push in a "plating plant"), whose mainland sister of the same name runs a SELF-BUILT ERP on a low-code platform (明道雲 HAP private deployment) plus a custom .NET system; lead in a colleague's 私田 (NOVA live 2026-10-25 · job 7ed42fa3 · XINWEI METAL SURFACE TREATMENT (THAILAND) run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Stage:** a content-free "日常建联" note = **E (公田/長期經營)**, even with ACP at D (behaviour-sequence rule, same as §87/§111). C2 = a real conversation with the director-contact in which he names a specific management problem (stock, reconciliation with the sister, Thai filing) and shows interest in outside help.
- **Two group companies on one site:** link them as related accounts but keep **separate opportunities** (different systems, size, pain). Enter through the entity whose contact is a director and which makes money; keep one site story with the sister's play (same field owner).
- **Group self-build at a mainland sister (low-code plus custom):** read as a preference for control and specific floor needs (racks, batch loading, trace), possibly maintained by mainland IT. Play per §71: standard product plus industry practice plus scoped extension; Thai VAT, withholding tax, Thai-language users and local support are the gaps to TEST with questions, never claims that the low-code build "cannot" do them; never offer to modify or take over the in-house build. First internal check: DigiWin China CRM for the mainland sister and DigiWin TW for the Taiwan parent, through the field owner.
- **Question themes for in-group PVD/vacuum toll plating (questions, not claims):** per-batch reconciliation of customer-supplied parts (received, in process, plated, rejected, stripped and re-plated) with the in-group customer; chamber loading against urgent orders (LRP plus exception reports on Workflow, APS only at T100/鼎華 tier); strip-and-replate cost per batch and its cause (target life, process drift); what the stock actually is (customers' parts, blanks, targets and gases, WIP).
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA declared a "營運資金卡死危機" and told the field owner to "繞過其自建系統的防線" (adversarial framing; stock and receivable days stay questions); its opening script quoted the filings at the customer (3.65 times growth), promised "釋放數千萬泰銖的流動資金" (invented), called a Taiwanese company "中資", signed as NOVA and fixed a meeting time (§43); it asserted that a low-code build "極難應對" Thai tax, VAT and GAAP and that its multi-language rights "極易崩潰" (unverified product claims), tied the self-built system to the receivable days (no evidence), gave the chairman an LVMH-compliance motive and the contact a cash-stuck motive as facts (inference only), and told the team to call DigiWin China "today" and reach the contact by WeChat (routed through the field owner; the channel head contacts no one). None adopted as fact. No control-workaround or customer-recording advice was offered.

## §123 — ACP D whose only daily report is "日常建联" (contact = a Thai staff member with no title on the PARENT's corporate domain, from a data list, NOT a director), at a small YEAR-TWO Thai auto-parts plant (heat-shield assemblies, stampings, clamp assemblies; registered 2024, ฿80M paid in, no bank debt; FY2025 sales ~฿19M at ~44 gross margin but SG&A ~135 of sales, net loss ~฿17M two years running; PP&E falling, no new equipment; self-described address in a WHA LOGISTICS PARK, i.e. most likely leased space) of a PRIVATE Shanghai SME parent (UN Global Compact participant; Chinese name and ERP not public); lead in a colleague's 私田 (NOVA live 2026-10-25 · job 617fc34b · ZHILIN AUTO PARTS (THAILAND) run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Stage:** a content-free "日常建联" with a non-director contact = **E (公田/長期經營)**, below the SDR's D (same behaviour-sequence rule as §87/§111/§122). Lift facts: (1) a customer-stated, quantified must-do (an OEM or tier-one audit, a Thai tax filing problem, a traceability claim); (2) the contact's real role plus a link to the sole director or a budget owner; (3) a dated evaluation or go-live schedule.
- **Reading a small plant with high gross margin, overheads above sales and falling PP&E in leased logistics-park space (inference):** most likely a finishing / assembly / inspection / dispatch hub for parent-made parts rather than full stamping and welding; SG&A = rent, seconded staff, start-up costs; high receivable days = a new supplier's weak terms or open balances with the parent. Ask, never assert: plant role (full process or assembly and dispatch), main car makers or tier-one customers, IATF or customer-audit requirement.
- **First system question when the parent's ERP is unknown:** what ERP/MES the HQ runs, and whether HQ wants the Thai company independent, interfaced to HQ, or on the group system. Phase 1 at this size: Workflow ERP standard scope plus Thai tax localisation; no big project. Question themes (questions, not claims): Thai VAT and WHT filing, lot traceability from steel strip and shift to the shipped clamp or shield, reconciliation of subcontracted surface treatment.
- **Channel head's boundary:** coach the field owner's questions; never contact the customer, never promise terms, never change CRM status; the parent's DigiWin China check runs through the field owner.
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA gave a Chinese parent name it said it "confirmed on 企查查" (no public hit; not adopted, kept out of the dossier; the field owner gets the name from the contact and checks gsxt.gov.cn); told Peter to contact DigiWin China managers himself and to introduce chambers, BOI consultants or accounting firms (not adopted: Peter does not contact DigiWin China or work a colleague's account); used fear framing ("小廠每月人工申報極易出錯被罰", "2 小時內追溯否則巨額索賠"; unsourced, kept as questions); named "Toyota, Ford" as example customers (no evidence); and scripted a DigiWin team "beside the WHA park", a peer case of "Rayong auto-parts plants passing OEM audits in three months" and a free "泰國車廠稽核系統準備清單" (none verified; case-name hygiene). None adopted. No control-workaround or customer-recording advice was offered.

## §124 — PRE-PRODUCTION Thai plant under construction (registered 2024-11, ฿5M → ฿80M in 2025, ฿20M paid in, PP&E ~฿68M, no stock or sales, ~฿83M interest-free current liabilities, a ~฿32M receivables-type line before any sale) held 100 by two Singapore-nationality holders; registry TSIC changed from auto parts to office machinery, ACP says machinery; the only contact matches a signing director by name and writes from the mail domain of a PRC-LISTED woodworking-machinery maker whose own consolidation lists show NO Thai or Singapore company; ACP D whose only report is "工厂还没竣工，长期跟进"; lead in a colleague's 私田 (NOVA live 2026-10-25 · job 79c44fb7 · NANXING TECHNOLOGY (THAILAND) run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Grade:** an unbuilt plant with one "long-term follow-up" note stays **D (需求探詢)** in NOVA's live reading even when the contact is a signatory: a build creates a real need, but with no quantified must-do reason, no timeline and no project team (立案三要項) it is not C2. Consistent with §107 (D) and one notch above §115 (E, where nothing was on the books yet). Lift facts: a direct conversation in which the signatory (or the build lead he names) agrees to evaluate the Thai plant's system; and whether the Thai ERP is chosen in Thailand or by the Chinese side.
- **Unverified listed-parent pointer (Claude method, this run):** a contact on a listed company's domain is a pointer, not a parent. Check the listed company's latest annual and interim consolidation lists (企业集团的构成) and its announcements and investor Q&A for the country before writing "subsidiary"; if absent, record "possible, unverified" and recommend บอจ.5 for the Singapore or offshore holders. Internal checks run through the field owner with the DigiWin China office in the candidate's city: is it an account, does its CRM know of the Thai plant, does the budget come from China or Thailand, must the Thai plant connect to the parent's own systems.
- **First cut for a pre-production plant:** Thai tax compliance, fixed assets from the build phase and build-phase VAT and advances (a large receivables-type line before any sale is the usual sign; ask, do not assume); BOI machine duty only if a certificate exists. Against a parent with its own software teams: position as complement (Thai tax, books, basic stock), never as replacement.
- **Never promise:** free, unlimited custom links to the parent's own systems; real-time cross-border database sync or two-way integration before the parent's IT has agreed.
- **Timing:** sign about 3–4 months before completion or machine move-in (about three months for implementation plus Thai tax set-up; live before the trial run), inside §107's 4–6 months before trial production. Restart signals: JobThai or LinkedIn posts for a finance manager, accountant or warehouse supervisor; a further capital call on DBD; senior visitors from the parent (only if the SDR hears of it).
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA guessed the holding sits outside the listed company "to avoid China's approval of a listed company's overseas investment" (unsourced; imputes a regulatory motive; not adopted); told the channel head to sound out BOI consultants, accountants and builders about the plant and to "coordinate cross-border resources" (not adopted: no approaches to third parties about a colleague's account; coordination is the field owner's call); scripted an opener that assumes BOI equipment exemption (kept as a question); and proposed promising connection to the parent's PLM and WMS (a scope promise; not adopted). No control-workaround or customer-recording advice was offered.

## §125 — PRE-PRODUCTION Thai PCB plant of a HONG KONG-LISTED laminate-to-PCB conglomerate (registered 2022-12, ฿960M registered, ~฿433M paid in, PP&E ~฿1.51B, a ~฿950M near-interest-free group liability, no stock or sales; the group's own filings moved phase I from "2026" to "completed in 2027"), registered at the SAME ADDRESS and with the SAME sole director as the group's 28-year-old, profitable Thai LAMINATE company (~฿4.2B revenue); group ERP since 2003 brand unknown, Lotus Notes workflow; ACP D whose only report is "工厂还未竣工，预计十月再去陌拜一次"; data-list contact on the group's mail domain whose name matches the listed parent's company secretary (unverified); lead in a colleague's 私田 (NOVA live 2026-10-25 · job 67e21089 · TECHWISE CIRCUITS (THAILAND) run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Grade:** an unfinished plant of a listed group whose phase I slipped a year, with a non-signatory contact, stays **D (需求探詢)**, ready to fall to E; consistent with §107 and §124. Three lift facts to C2: (a) a dated start of the Thai system evaluation; (b) a named Thai project or IT owner reached directly, not only a head-office mailbox; (c) a must-localise Thai need (Thai tax, BOI if certified) that the group's mainland system cannot cover.
- **Same-group sister next door (new prior):** when an established Thai sister in a DIFFERENT process (laminate vs PCB) shares the address and the director, expect its production system NOT to carry over (lamination/cutting vs drilling, plating, WIP tracking), but expect its Thai finance, tax and BOI team and set-up to be shared or copied for the new company. Discovery: "will the sister's finance team keep the new company's books and Thai tax, or will the plant hire its own?" The intercompany flow between the two Thai companies (in-group material purchases) is a real multi-entity question to ask, not a claim.
- **Let-go and wake-up signals:** let go (to E) if the site is closed with no one on it, or the answer is "all IT is decided by head office and Thailand may not talk to vendors"; wake up when a resident build or plant head arrives in Thailand, or the customer shows interest in Thai tax or BOI handling. Watch the listed parent's next results for a firmer phase-I date.
- **Risks to name:** the 空降兵 risk (months spent with a registration-era or head-office contact who does not run the build) and 總部一條鞭 (central IT with no Thai say).
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA guessed the group's core ERP is "高機率 SAP 或 Oracle" (unsourced); positioned DigiWin as able to "無縫對接 (API)" the group ERP and as having "20+ 年泰國在地稅務與 BOI 輔導經驗" (interface promise and unverified claim; §22 wording applies); quantified pain with "每月 5-7 天" reconciliation and BOI fines "數百萬泰銖" (unsourced); premised the first cut and questions on BOI equipment exemption with no certificate found (kept as a question); scripted an opener claiming many Rojana customers, a fear story about other PCB plants' BOI penalties and a BOI "白皮書" to send (unverified; no such asset known); suggested asking the sister company's IT directly (a side door around the account's contact; left to the field owner) and "將戰線拉回中國大陸鼎捷對接" (routing is the field owner's call; Peter does not contact DigiWin China himself); and set a "2026 Q2–Q3" golden window premised on machines arriving in H2 2026, which the 2027 phase-I date does not support (§107/§124 timing used instead). None adopted. No control-workaround or customer-recording advice was offered.

## §126 — Ramping Thai connector/harness plant (first sales year ~฿79M, profitable, stock ~326 days of cost at year-end, parent-reported H1 loss) held 80% through Hong Kong by a Taiwan-LISTED wire-and-connector group whose OTHER segment is IT services (its own SI subsidiary); no system named anywhere; one SDR report "visit in October"; lead in a colleague's 私田 (NOVA live 2026-10-25 · job 4f334be5 · WELLTREND TECHNOLOGY run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Stage:** a planned visit with no customer contact yet = **E (長期經營)** as the working grade, even with ACP at D and visible pain indicators; the OSINT field can still read C2-candidate. Rise on the 立案三要項: a quantified must-do the customer agrees to (e.g. stock days, cost per part), a named Thai project owner or team, and a dated go-live.
- **Group with an in-house SI:** the core-system brand is decided in Taipei (parent IT / the IT-services division); the Thai plant holds local-requirements and user-acceptance say. Expect "we can build and maintain it ourselves"; never pitch a general ERP against the group's own IT. Lead with what HQ IT would rather not maintain: Thai tax localisation (VAT, WHT, Thai documents) with a Thai implementation team; second, stock and lot traceability for imported terminals, wire and housings. Same direction as §48 and §71.
- **Tier:** Workflow at ~฿80M (KB §4 caution band), with data handed to the parent's chart for consolidation; T100 only if HQ demands group integration.
- **Researcher role:** brief the field owner privately (pre-visit brief); the field owner checks the CRM for every group entity name (parent, China plants, sister Thai plant); never contact the customer, never move ownership.
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA put BOI in the first cut without evidence of promotion (keep as a question); its sample questions quoted the plant's stock figure and the parent's loss back at the customer (§43/§55); called the stock level "致命傷" from one year-end snapshot of a ramp; wrote "易飛/易拓泰國版" (易拓 = TIPTOP; the call is Workflow); suggested a 協同備忘 in the colleague's ACP record (the field owner's or Peter's call, not the researcher's). Its check of its own records for the group returned "不確定" with no record found; never treat that as proof of non-customer.

## §127 — JUST-BUILT, pre-revenue Thai plant (registered 2024-11, ฿125M fully paid, PP&E ~฿137M, zero stock and sales at the FY2025 close, ~฿110M interest-free current liabilities, a ~฿89M receivables-type line before any sale) held 99 from Hong Kong and 1 by a Chinese individual, parent UNRESOLVED, no public footprint; registry scope mixes machinery, precision hardware, electronics, moulds and a surface-treatment spray; the only contact is a non-director 經理 whose mailbox is on the domain of an UNRELATED-industry PRC company (refinery chemicals); SDR's only report "展会认识，工厂刚建好。十月去拜访"; lead in a colleague's 私田 (NOVA live 2026-10-25 · job d3dd2dad · SUN STRONG TECHNOLOGY (THAILAND) run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Grade:** a trade-show contact at a built-but-not-producing plant with a visit planned and no needs conversation = **D (需求探詢)**, same behaviour-sequence rule as §92, §101, §124. C2 = the SDR reaches a director or a plant head the directors authorise AND the customer acknowledges a system need in a substantive discussion. C1 = the 立案三要項 (a dated must-do such as a production start the system must precede; a formally named project owner; a YYYY-MM evaluation and go-live timetable).
- **Contact on another company's domain (reusable readings, hypotheses only):** (a) the owner also invests in that company and the new plant borrows its mail system until its own exists; (b) the contact works for a build consultant or registration agent, not the plant; (c) the address was mis-copied at the trade show. Until resolved, treat the contact as a gatekeeper and promise nothing deep before a Thai director or plant head is met. Identify the parent by asking neutrally which mainland company invested and whether a mainland finance or IT team will work with the plant; never quote the contact's mail domain back at her (Claude vetting; KB §43), never check CRM on a guessed name (§97).
- **Window and scope:** sign 3–6 months before production; never wait until after start-up. Phase 1 (build and preparation) = GL, AR, AP, fixed assets with depreciation under Thai VAT and WHT, plus purchasing, receiving and stock; phase 2 (trial to mass production) = work orders, issue and return, sales and shipping; cost accounting only after about three stable months. Refuse a big-bang "everything on day one" scope.
- **SDR record after the first visit (five facts):** the parent group; where the final decision-maker sits (Thailand or China); trial and mass-production dates; the system project owner; any competing system or budget.
- **Visit questions by branch (NOVA's, questions only):** ETO machines vs standard assembly and whether the parent's routings and BOMs can be reused; moulds made for own use or sold, and how mould maintenance and life will be tracked; coating-formula confidentiality and chemical batch and shelf-life trace; what floor rules (receiving, issue, reporting) must exist before the line starts; Thai VAT/WHT and books kept locally or by the parent's finance.
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA scripted the SDR quoting the contact's mail domain back to her and offered a Chinese name "信佳" for the name-alike Hong Kong group (found in no source; not adopted); its C1 example said that without a system the plant "cannot ship, clear customs or comply with Thai tax law" (fear framing; kept only as "a dated production start the system must precede"). No control-workaround, recording, or direct-contact advice was offered.

## §128 — PRE-BUILD Thai plant (registered 2024-12, ฿500M, no DBD statement yet, registered office = the industrial estate's service office) that a TAIWAN-LISTED parent names as a 100 per cent subsidiary in its own audited statements (land bought from the estate for ~฿1.04B, parent loans up to ~NT$1.4B at 3.5, stated operation end-2026 to 2027), DBD coding both holders "other country"; ACP D whose only report is "工厂还未建好，长期跟进"; the only contact has no title and a personal mailbox, from a data list; lead in a colleague's 私田 (NOVA live 2026-10-25 · job 5f629b14 · TOPO TECHNOLOGY (THAILAND) run, parent Catcher Technology · 南高-商機助手)
- **Grade:** money and land in place do not count as buying behaviour. An unbuilt plant with one "long-term follow-up" note and a contact who has no title = **E (長期經營/公田)** in NOVA's live reading (one notch below the SDR's D; recorded, not changed). Compare §124 (D: the contact was a signing director) and §115 (E: nothing on the books). Lift to C2: a conversation with a build-team member, a Thai IT or finance head or a Taiwan-seconded plant head (not only the first contact) in which the plant acknowledges a system need and says whether it will ask HQ or choose locally. Lift to C1: the 立案三要項.
- **Who decides at a listed Taiwanese group's overseas plant ("總部強控、地方建議"):** core ERP (finance, consolidation, supply chain) is decided by HQ IT and finance and rolled out; Thai tax localisation, BOI reporting and floor systems (MES/WMS) are where the Thai build team and plant head have strong influence. Entry when HQ rolls out its ERP: a Thai finance-localisation and BOI layer interfaced to the HQ system, then a floor system. If Thailand may choose: phase 1 purchasing, stock and sales with Thai finance (and BOI if promoted); phase 2 production with MES/WMS at production start.
- **Floor scope for a multi-process metal-casing plant (die casting, CNC machining, anodising/finishing) — priors, not evidence about any plant:** mould life, tool wear, machine utilisation and finishing-bath chemistry life are what a finance-led ERP does not hold; offer them only once the Thai process list is confirmed.
- **Routing:** the field owner asks DigiWin TW's regional business group that covers the parent's city whether the parent is an account and what it runs; never Peter directly, never on a guessed name (consistent with §17, §107).
- **Window:** NOVA computed "sign 2026 Q1–Q2" from the parent's announced end-2026 date and a six-month go-live lead; Claude vetting: when the SDR reports the plant still unbuilt, recompute from the real trial-production month (§17: sign ~6–9 months before go-live; §39: go-live ~3 months before trial production) instead of quoting the announced date.
- **Method (Claude, this run):** a pre-build Thai company with no DBD statement can still be sized from the parent's audited parent-only statements (investee table: address, shares, carrying amount, share of profit; financing-provided table: loan balance, peak and rate). Match on the Thai address and share count, not the name. DBD may code a Taiwanese parent's holding as "other country"; record the gap and recommend บอจ.5 only if registry-grade proof is needed.
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA guessed the parent's core ERP as "高機率 SAP" and DigiWin peripheral systems at HQ (unsourced; ask instead), quoted "九成由台灣總部決定" (unsourced figure, §46 rule), claimed foreign SAP Thai localisation is "客製費用極高" (competitor claim with no source), scripted "我們輔導過很多台商在春武里建廠" (unverified) and assumed BOI in a customer question (BOI not established), and called its plan "唯一勝率路徑" (overstatement). None adopted. No control-workaround, recording, or direct-contact advice was offered.

## §129 — Thai PET-bottle-plus-beverage-FILLING (toll co-packing) plant at START-UP (registered 2024-02, ฿2.3B registered, ฿1.175B paid in, a ฿1.54B bank line guaranteed by its Thai parent and ฿732M drawn, pre-revenue in its FY2025 filing, commercial production announced for Q4 2026) held 100% by the Thai arm of a TAIWAN-LISTED packaging and filling group whose name ("Hon Chuan") is a cleared DigiWin Thailand reference name with entity and product unrecorded; ACP D whose only report is "十月去拜访"; one contact with no title; lead in a colleague's 私田 (NOVA live 2026-10-25 · job 4dc5eb6c · HON SHIN (THAILAND) run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Grade:** a firm production date is the customer's operating timetable, not an ERP buying timetable. Amount 0 + a contact with no title who is not a director + no decision-maker conversation = **D (需求探詢)** (same as ACP). Lift to C1 on the 立案三要項: a quantified must-do the customer's management accepts (for example, a brand audit or first-delivery date that the system must support), a named project owner (plant head or the Thai parent's IT lead), and a dated ERP/MES timetable.
- **New 100% subsidiary of an existing (or suspected) customer — architecture:** the normal route is a new company inside the parent's current system (same instance), because intercompany flows (the parent's preforms and closures into the filler) and group reporting are simplest there and only user licences are added. A separate ERP + MES is plausible only when the new plant's process differs sharply (food-safety traceability) AND the plant runs autonomously. Who decides: HQ IT (group architecture, security, consolidation) leads; the Thai parent's GM holds the local budget; the new plant's head only recommends.
- **Toll filling — the three system problems:** (1) customer-supplied materials (concentrate, labels, preforms) tracked in dedicated, non-valued stock separate from own stock, issued by the tolling work order, reconciled to the brand owner; (2) two-way lot and expiry trace from finished batch back to the customer's material lots, FEFO issue; the books are standard ERP, line-side error-proofing and production records are MES; (3) tolling cost per SKU: ERP actual cost by driver (hours, utilities, depreciation); MES supplies actual machine hours and output for sharper allocation.
- **Late window:** if the plant is going commercial and has not talked to us, assume the company was already opened in the parent's system; the open ground is the floor (traceability, customer-material reconciliation, tolling cost). If the parent's ERP is a foreign or local non-DigiWin product, an ERP sale is unlikely: switch to the MES/traceability complement.
- **Routing:** the field owner (SDR and her leader) asks DigiWin TW's account owner for the Taiwanese HQ and the Thai MA/contract records which entity and product the reference is; never Peter directly, never on a guessed name (consistent with §17, §107, §121).
- **Method (Claude, this run):** a Thai subsidiary of a Taiwan-listed group can be fully sized from the TWSE document server (doc.twse.com.tw t57sb01: consolidated annual EN report and the latest quarterly ZH review): investee table (shares, original investment = paid-in, carrying amount = equity), financing-provided and endorsement/guarantee tables (bank line, drawn amount). DBD equity + accumulated losses should reconcile to the parent's original investment to the baht; a Thai-registered parent shows as a single "ไทย" holder in DBD's nationality tab.
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA suggested Peter's team join the SDR's visit and that the SDR open by citing Peter's name-clearance and a "long partnership" (not adopted: Peter does not contact a colleague's account, and the reference entity and product are unverified); quoted "審廠失敗一天，損失百萬泰銖代工費", a "2 小時內" trace rule and "全額索賠並撤銷代工合約" (unsourced, fear-based; not adopted); treated the ฿2.3B registered capital as "23 億高額設備" depreciation (capital is not equipment cost); asserted the Taiwanese HQ "一定有鼎新的維護主管" (asked instead); cited 五十嵐 and momo as filling-industry successes (unverified case names; use the sweep's verified cases with their dates); and said "絕對來得及" (overclaim). No control-workaround, tax, audit-circumvention or recording advice was offered.

## §130 — MICRO year-one Thai-Taiwanese TRADING company (registered 2024-10, capital exactly ฿5M = the skill's DQ line, Thai 51 / Taiwanese 49, three Taiwanese-romanised directors, registry objective "precision measuring instruments", name "Linear Automation"; first full year ~฿2.8M sales at ~37 gross margin, SG&A ~61 of sales, ~฿0.65M loss, year-end stock ~641 days of COGS on ~฿4.1M INTEREST-FREE current liabilities); no website, job post or any public footprint; ACP D whose only report is "十月拜訪" (a planned visit, not logged by month-end); data-list contact "總經理" sharing only a surname with one director; lead in a colleague's 私田 (NOVA live 2026-10-25 · job 23859543 · GET LINEAR AUTOMATION run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Stage:** a planned-visit note with no visit logged = **E (公田 / 長期經營)**, below the SDR's D (same behaviour-sequence rule as §87/§111/§122/§123). C2 = the visit happens, the contact names a management problem (stock, quote speed) himself, and shows he wants it solved. C1 = 立案三要項 (quantified must-do, named window, a dated timetable).
- **Size and shape:** a ~฿3M no-BOM trader gets **no presales**. If a need is ever stated, the only first cut is a standard cloud light trading scope (item master, real-time purchase-sales-stock, multiple stock locations, landed-cost allocation of duty and freight); never promise customisation, never promise integration with a (possible) Taiwanese principal's system.
- **First-visit diagnostics (by the field owner):** (1) who decides quotes, orders and purchasing: a Taiwanese head office or the Thai team (if Taiwan, the conversation belongs on the Taiwan side); (2) how stock is kept and whether items have gone missing or been ordered twice; (3) how long an enquiry takes from the customer, through the overseas principal's price and lead-time answer, to a quote, and whether slow answers lost orders.
- **Taiwan angle:** the field owner passes the directors' romanised names and the company name to DigiWin TW to see whether a Taiwanese maker or distributor that is a DigiWin TW account stands behind it; the fork is sell-to (group-licence extension if the principal is a DigiWin account) vs ecosystem (a distributor serving Thai semiconductor-equipment builders as a channel, then /research-partner).
- **Claude's reading (this run):** year-one stock far above sales is most likely opening stock of an imported range; read it as a question until the next filing shows it unsold. Interest-free current liabilities ~1.3x stock = supplier credit or owners' money, not distress.
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA stated the stock "cash-flow stuck, very unhealthy" as fact (first-year stocking is likelier; kept as a question); quoted an unsourced "carrying cost 15-20 of stock value, ~฿0.5M a year"; scripted an opener on "the Taiwan parent's efforts in Thailand" (no parent evidenced); offered unnamed peer cases and a salesperson's online demo at the first visit (no verified case; no demo before a diagnosed need, §116); named three Taiwanese linear-motion makers as possible parents (speculation; parent only from the registry); used the loaded term "白手套"; gave a budget band and a "next January" tax deadline without a source. None adopted. No control-workaround or customer-recording advice was offered; the DigiWin TW check runs through the field owner.

## §131 — MICRO year-one Taiwanese-held MAGNET start-up (registered 2025-03, ฿20M registered, ~฿7.1M paid in by arithmetic, ฿7.2M PP&E = 70 of assets, first-period sales ฿0.19M at ~34 gross margin, ฿1.76M loss, ฿4.86M interest-free current liabilities, receivables above a full year of sales); registry objective = eight magnet families (NdFeB, SmCo, ferrite, rubber, electro-magnets, separators); parent NOT in any registry, only a BRAND-NAME lead to a Taiwan-founded Dongguan magnet group; no web footprint; ACP D whose only report is an EVENT INVITATION ("邀請參加活動") to a data-list 經理 who is not a director; lead in a colleague's 私田 (NOVA live 2026-10-25 · job cca6b1a1 · SUNLEI MAGNET ELECTRIC (THAILAND) run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Stage:** an invitation with no needs conversation = **E (公田 / 長期經營)**, below the SDR's D (behaviour-sequence rule, same as §87/§111/§122/§123/§130; §77 gave D for an invitation at a ฿217M plant, so size and a missing decision-maker push this one to E). C1 = 立案三要項: a quantified must-do with a date, a named owner (Thai site manager or the head-office IT), a go-live timetable.
- **Size and shape:** worth keeping warm, **not worth heavy presales**. If a need is stated: standard cloud light subscription or standard Workflow ERP phase 1 (purchase-sales-stock, Thai VAT/WHT documents, simple subcontracting for outsourced plating or coating); no MES, no customisation. Payment: subscription prepaid or a high first payment (consistent with §23, §108).
- **Magnet-processing question themes (NOVA, questions not facts):** how outsourced plating/coating is controlled (blanks out, pieces and scrap back); how fast a finished lot can be traced back to the raw block lot and the magnetising data when a buyer audits; whether Thai VAT filing is done by an outside accountant or in-house; whether the Thai site's books will run alone or reconcile to a head-office system.
- **Brand-name parent lead:** the field owner checks CRM for the candidate group (DigiWin TW and China) only AFTER the contact confirms who the owners are; a brand word plus a matching product list is not a registry link (§108, §130). If the group is an account: the AM asks the group about its Thai plan; if not: the field owner works the Thai site. Never through Peter.
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA's must-do example ("Q4 2026 production start", "before the 2027 Q1 filing") was invented; its event script introduced the SDR as "鼎新的 Nova", invented the event's content, claimed "many magnet-processing Taiwanese" share the pains (unsourced), used a fabricated pretext ("I am visiting other Taiwanese in Bang Phli next week") and offered an unverified "compliance pitfall guide" from magnetic-material peers in Thailand (no verified Thai magnet case); its openers quoted the filing ("equipment has moved in", filings stay questions §43/§55) and presumed the parent ("SUNLEI's overseas expansion"); it proposed the parent as contract-performance guarantor (touches the customer's group contracting; not a sales script, as §108). None adopted. No control-workaround, tax, audit or customer-recording advice was offered.

## §132 — ACP D whose only daily report is "十月去拜访" (no visit logged 25 days later; contact = a data-list 經理, not a director, amount empty) at the RAMPING Thai FPC/PCB plant of a PRC-LISTED circuit-board group listing H shares in Hong Kong (registered 2023, ฿5B registered, ~฿3.5B paid in, ~฿10.3B interest-bearing group-funded liabilities, ฿10.5B PP&E, first sales year ~฿372M with a ~฿794M loss; BOI phase 2 approved 2026-05); the plant's OWN job ad checks raw material "ผ่านระบบ Oracle" and HR runs on a "global HR system"; the group's newly acquired optical-module company has its own Thai company at the SAME address and appears as an Oracle-integration example on a 2022 鼎華 proposal slide; lead in a colleague's 私田 (NOVA live 2026-10-25 · job 219772e7 · MULTI-FINELINE ELECTRONICS (THAILAND) run, parent 東山精密 DSBJ · 南高-商機助手)
- **Stage:** "E 級 (公田客戶/長期經營)" — a planned visit that has not happened, a non-director contact and amount 0 are not D, whatever ACP says. Back to C2 needs all three: a power person engaged (the Thai plant or finance head, or the HQ IT / supply-chain owner in China); a Thai-side pain the group ERP does not cover, stated by the customer (BOI records, floor traceability); consent to a survey or scenario session.
- **Edge order for a group-ERP (Oracle/SAP) Thai plant of a PRC-listed group (hypotheses to test, never claims about the incumbent):** (1) BOI bonded-stock reconciliation and Thai tax output beside the group ERP (non-invasive, the plant may hold some say); (2) barcode / light WMS for FPC laminate and moisture-sensitive SMT parts (FIFO, floor life); (3) MES only together with HQ, which usually has its own MES standard at home.
- **Route:** "海內外協同 (Top-down + Bottom-up)": the China AM or 鼎華 checks whether the group is a live account and works HQ IT for a "local evaluation" green light; the SDR and DigiWin TH bring floor evidence from the Thai plant. Claude vetting: carried by the field owner's line; Peter does not contact DigiWin China, 鼎華 or the colleague's account himself.
- **Wake-up signals:** a customer audit notice for the Thai plant (traceability reports); BOI reconciliation pressure once volume ships; SMT lines entering trial with stock that does not match the ERP; a second unit (e.g. an acquired company's line) starting at the same site and needing its IT aligned with HQ.
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA called Oracle's Thai localisation (especially BOI) "極度昂貴且難用" and Oracle "在現場倉儲細緻度上偏弱" (unverified product claims); stated that the parent and its FPC subsidiary are "極具指標性的既有客戶" of DigiWin / 鼎華 using supply-chain, barcode, MES or PLM systems (unverified; NOVA itself asks the China AM to check the CRM); proposed showing the 2022 integration as "已有成熟接口, 泰國廠可以直接複製, 上線風險最低" (interface promise beyond §22, case currency unknown, customer naming needs Peter's OK); and scripted a visit step that raises "罰款與退港風險" from a customer audit or BOI inspection (fear-selling). None adopted. No control-workaround, pretext, tax or customer-recording advice was offered.


## §133 — ACP D whose only daily report is "了解需求" (25 days quiet, amount 0; contacts = two data-list 經理s, one the plant's Factory Manager per the parent's annual report, neither a director) at a LOSS-MAKING, IATF, high-mix ALLOY-WHEEL plant (casting, flow forming, CNC, automated painting; ~฿705M, four loss years, gross margin ~0 in the latest year, stock ~153 days of COGS) that is the ONLY factory of a 100%-owning SGX-LISTED tyre-and-wheel DISTRIBUTOR group; parent just recapitalised the plant (+฿79M) and says it is upgrading the plant "for efficiency and cost"; ten openings in one week incl. finance, purchasing and painting managers, three of them asking for unnamed "ERP" skills (one "ERP/SAP"); lead in a colleague's 私田 (NOVA live 2026-10-25 · job 3b64031c · STAMFORD SPORT WHEELS run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Stage:** a single content-free "了解需求" with a non-signatory factory-level contact and amount 0 = **D (需求探詢)**, however strong the pain profile (behaviour-sequence rule; same as §78/§106). C2 = a power person engaged and willing to evaluate (the parent's executive who owns manufacturing, or the incoming finance manager with a mandate); C1 = 立案三要項 (a quantified must-do the customer states, a named project team, a dated selection/go-live).
- **First cut for a loss-making high-mix casting-machining-painting plant that already has an unnamed ERP:** the layer beside the ERP: actual cost per model (scrap and rework inside it), paint-line and casting rejects by lot (MES), finished-goods stock by model/finish (WMS). Size frames it at T100 tier, phased; ERP review only once the incumbent's brand and the parent's stance are known (consistent with §18, §22, §78). Phasing is value-first, never to keep a quote under an approval limit (§69, §106).
- **Decision:** a listed foreign parent that funds and consolidates its only plant decides centrally; the factory manager collects data and raises pain. The field owner calls high through the incoming finance manager or the parent's manufacturing executive; the channel head briefs, never contacts. First internal step: a cross-region CRM check on the group's English names.
- **Question themes (questions, not claims):** where the actual cost of a model comes from and whether rejects reach it; how paint rejects are recorded by lot and cause; how a lot is traced from casting heat to shipment for IATF or importer complaints; whether the upgrade programme includes floor systems and who in HQ owns that part.
- Caution (Claude vetting): NOVA offered illustrative must-dos as if targets ("cut stock 20 by FY2027", "halve paint rejects in 3 months"), an unsourced ฿ release from a 15-point turnover gain, a causal claim that missing MES "pulls down" the margin, warm-up and visit questions that quote the capital increase and stock days at the customer (§43), "Workflow cannot handle cross-border consolidation" (unverified product claim), preparing "Thai alloy-wheel" success cases (none evidenced), and reaching HQ via LinkedIn (field owner's call). None adopted as fact or advice. No control-workaround, pretext, tax or recording advice was offered.

## §134 — ACP D whose only daily report is "活动上认识 T100老客，预约十月份去拜访" (event contact = a data-list 專員, not a director; visit booked, not logged 25 days later; amount empty) at a BUILT-and-EQUIPPED, PRE-REVENUE Thai heater plant (registered 2024-05, ฿2M → ฿55M → ฿155M fully paid, PP&E ~฿223M, first ~฿3.6M of stock, zero sales in two filings, ~฿216M interest-free current liabilities), 100 Chinese-held, sole director signs alone, parent UNRESOLVED (only an [EN] name match with a Yuyao stove exporter to US farm-and-ranch retail chains); lead in a colleague's 私田 (NOVA live 2026-10-25 · job ad36f40e · HANKS HEATING APPLIANCE TECHNOLOGY (THAILAND) run · 南高-商機助手)
- **Grade:** an event contact at staff level + a booked-but-unlogged visit + a built plant not yet producing = **D (需求探詢)**; C1 = the 立案三要項 (a quantified must-do the customer states, a named project owner such as the plant, finance or IT head, a dated go-live). If the booked visit does not happen within its month, read §132 (E).
- **"T100老客" on an event note:** the field owner checks CRM on the EXACT company name the contact gives (and any exact [EN] registry or customs name), then asks the group's DigiWin China AM which modules run and whether a Thai IT plan exists; never search CRM on guessed Chinese characters (§97), and never open the visit as the parent's "老客老友" before the account is confirmed.
- **If the group is a live T100 account:** T100 multi-organisation, the Thai entity its own organisation sharing items, BOMs and suppliers, Thai VAT/WHT books and Thai stock and production run locally; DigiWin China = core template and any group licences, DigiWin TH = Thai localisation, Thai/English reports, training, support (same as §43, §61, §76). Phase 1 = finance + purchasing and stock (machine imports, depreciation, first materials, group funding come first); window 3–6 months before trial production. Settle the contract split early: NOVA's named risks are head-office IT quietly copying the system to Thailand and "signed in China, delivered by Thailand for nothing".
- **Question themes for a seasonal heater/stove plant serving US retail (questions only):** how capacity and key parts (thermostats, elements) line up with one short pre-season shipping window; how a unit is traced from serial or lot back to steel or castings, paint lot and assembly QC if a buyer asks; how in-transit parts from China and Thai stock are costed and kept apart. Visit facts to record in ACP: trial and mass-production YYYY-MM; the decision map (Thai GM or plant head, their say over systems, head-office IT's role); copy the group system or choose locally; any buyer audit or Thai tax/BOI pressure.
